bibliographical references and index. | Summary: "The main aim of the  
research is to study and explore the current status of block chain  
technology and, through the latest technology, build a business model to  
secure the future direction in the field of business. This book  
discusses the tactics and methods, as well as their limitations and  
performance. The primary goal is to introduce the most recent  
technologies and methods for developing the business sector. It will  
prove that this technology will control costs in different organizations  
and industries. These block chain technology features are addressed by a  
number of businesses to ensure security for distributed data. Users can  
securely authenticate themselves thanks to the block chain technology  
and trusted hardware combo. Blockchain solutions are one type of product  
that supplier's market to help businesses in a supply chain exchange  
data more easily. The companies who use blockchain technology to manage  
a multisided platform or market. Customers and consumers are the typical  
end users. Blockchain's basic concept is a shared, distributed database  
of transaction records amongst involved parties. Through this book wants  
to knowledge about the blockchain technology which provides many types  
of profitable tasks. Each chapter begins with an introduction, a need  
and motivation for business innovation, as well as applications for  
identifying and improving the system in each and every area of  
business"-- Provided by publisher.

# Advances in Business Information Systems and Analytics (ABISA) Book Series

Madjid Tavana (La Salle University, USA)ISSN: 2327-3275 |
| --- | --- |

### Mission

The successful development and management of information systems and business analytics is crucial to the success of an organization. New technological developments and methods for data analysis have allowed organizations to not only improve their processes and allow for greater productivity, but have also provided businesses with a venue through which to cut costs, plan for the future, and maintain competitive advantage in the information age.

The Advances in Business Information Systems and Analytics (ABISA) Book Series aims to present diverse and timely research in the development, deployment, and management of business information systems and business analytics for continued organizational development and improved business value.

 
| 

### Coverage

*   Data Governance
*   Geo-BIS
*   Decision Support Systems
*   Business Systems Engineering
*   Business Models
*   Big Data
*   Business Intelligence
*   Legal information systems
*   Management Information Systems
*   Business Decision Making

*   Shweta Dewangan  
    ICFAI University, Raipur, India

Blockchain is a decentralized ledger with permanently recorded transactions maintained by a computer system that is not controlled by a single entity. Using a cryptographic chain allows for the privacy of each piece of data to be protected. The term “blockchain” is derived from this. As a result, the blockchain can be trusted to provide a safe and automated way of data transfer. A transaction's initial portion generates a block. Thousands of thousands, if not millions, of machines throughout the network, verify this block. The system-wide persistent chain is then expanded to include the validated block. The result is a fresh document with a distinctive background. To make a single bogus record, one would need to spoof the entire chain millions of times. This is so unlikely that it defies logic that it could happen. Furthermore, none of these has any accompanying costs. This innovation can replace all current business methods and models that rely on charging a single price for any trade between two parties since there needs to be a clear leader on the street.

Simply put, a business model is a strategy or plan a firm employs to sell a product or service and generate revenue from those sales. Every corporation will have its procedures for conducting business. On the other hand, there is also a centralized model, which consists of the organization, the consumers, the staff, and the owners or shareholders of the company. It is decentralized, based on peer-to-peer transactions, and takes place inside a trusted and dependable network. These are the three primary characteristics of blockchain technology, and a blockchain business model possesses them. The nature of blockchain makes it necessary for businesses to adapt their operational procedures in various areas, including the flow of transactions, the change of entities, the profits, and the maintenance of growth throughout the transition. These business models ultimately contribute to improving businesses and benefit the end-users.

Building Secure Business Models Through Blockchain Technology: Tactics, Methods, Limitations, and Performance is an organized collection of 13 chapters written by renowned authors worldwide that gives a complete look at the use of technology in business. It provides extensive and brief coverage of sustainable security growth based on artificial intelligence technical developments. The concept of blockchain technology’s primary goal is to introduce the most recent technologies and methods for improving business transactions to the next level.

This book discusses theoretical, practical, and sociological perspectives on business sustainability solutions incorporating sophisticated technologies such as blockchain. Can utilize efficiently to advance research and technology in business in general.

Chapter 1 introduces blockchain technology use in different sectors. Blockchain was developed to support Bitcoin, a cryptocurrency that lacks confidence. However, many companies and stakeholders view technology as a promising means of resolving current business issues and upending established industries. The distributed ledger, known as the blockchain, has much potential for the financial services sector. Most blockchain use cases have been found in the financial industry. When creating new insurance products, insurers can benefit from blockchain technology—expansion of goods and services, fraud detection, price rises, and cost-cutting. With blockchain technology, businesses can readily verify facts, access vast talent pools, and reduce their dependency on outside sources of labor. Banks, governments, and companies want in on the venture capital action that blockchain has opened up. This article presents a novel approach or methodology for utilizing blockchain technology to manage businesses or other industries.

Chapter 2 involves a Convolutional Neural Network; this study aims to create a model for putting traffic signs in an image into many different groups using a (CNN) and the Keras library to find the signs. Traffic Sign Recognition aims to create a Deep Neural Network (DNN) to sort traffic signs into groups. We should use the German Traffic Sign Dataset to train the model to determine what traffic signs mean from natural images. This data should be pre-processed first to get the most out of the model. After choosing the model architecture, fine-tuning, and training, the model will be tested on new images of traffic signs found on the web. Since we need to classify images, we use a Convolutional Neural Network, which is a common choice for this problem. Tensor Flow library is used to write the code in Python. With 95% accuracy, our CNN model could recognize traffic signs and put them in the correct category. This model's graphical user interface (GUI) makes it easy to understand how signs are put into different classes.

Chapter 3 discussed the future estimation of business through blockchain technology. This article looks at blockchain technology's potential, current state, and how some of its parts could change how “business as usual” is done in different fields. This study combines the theoretical background of many research articles published in respected scientific journals over the past ten years and some of the findings to make our review easier and cover the rapidly growing blockchain domain. Based on a structured, systematic review and thematic content analysis of the current literature, the researcher suggests a thorough classification of blockchain-enabled applications in a wide range of industries, such as supply chain, business, health, IoT, privacy, data management, and others. It also calls attention to essential themes, patterns, new research opportunities, and flaws in the information that has already been shared, such as the limits of blockchain technology and how they affect businesses and other parts of the economy.

Chapter 4 discussed the field construction industry’s use of blockchain technology. Taking into account the importance and problems of blockchain technology in construction work, the current study looks at the potential of blockchain technology in the construction industry and how this disruptive technology could change the way construction projects are done. Using blockchain to simplify the contracts and paperwork for these complicated projects could save money, free up valuable resources, and speed up the time it takes to finish the projects. Blockchain records can't be changed, which will help ensure that work rules are followed and stop people from making up records. Since this is the case, it makes sense to start working on blockchain convergence in these areas and expand them as the technology improves.

Chapter 5 provides how telecom sectors can grow through blockchain. As TRAI had asked, the study looked at how blockchain technology could change the telecom industry. India is a developing country where many sectors depend on telecommunication. This made the telecom industry adopt blockchain technology to fix some problems. Blockchain provides security, transparency, immutability, and control from the point of transaction. It also has immediate and tangible benefits for roaming and settlements, identity management, mobile number portability, SLA monitoring, preventing phone theft, and creating a single source of truth of networks. As 5G participants use blockchain, other use cases will arise, such as a marketplace for telecom infrastructure, payments through mobile wallets, and device identity and security management. These use cases will help settle long-standing industry disputes, show off the newest products and services safely, and drive growth in the new world of digital transformation.

Chapter 6 presents blockchain technology in the retail sector. Blockchain is a novel technology that enables decentralized and transactional data sharing over a vast network of untrustworthy individuals. It allows new kinds of distributed software architectures. Although the technology was initially employed primarily in digital currency, it has also shown promise in other sectors. This paper provides an overview of blockchain technology. It also addresses how blockchain technology can be employed in some retail business processes to benefit customers and retailers. This chapter focuses on retail supply chain management (SCM) concerns and how Blockchain technology is transforming retail supply chain management. The chapter addresses the blockchain adoption market trend as well as some of the hurdles.

Chapter 7 presents the financial institution which using blockchain technology for many years. The combination of Artificial Intelligence and Blockchain Technology will be more potent in the following decades. Blockchain Technology is stable and secure. Thus, countries are adopting it in financial trading. Post-pandemic Indian consumers have overcome their phobia of online transactions. Intelligent devices, fast networks, more product services, and businesses have created a favourable environment for online financial transactions. This study examines how countries adopt blockchain in financial sectors. Blockchain is the future of secure, private, fast, and accurate technology. Financial fraud, data theft, late settlement, and data manipulation plague financial systems. Blockchain Technology will secure India's financial system.

Chapter 8 discussed new technology used in finance. Blockchain technology can efficiently combine financial resources for data analysis and processing. Data, rules, and application layers are utilized to develop new financial forms or service models depending on client needs to improve banking system efficiency and financial services. Financial markets may change as blockchain automates and improves client credit conditions. It can also enhance cross-border economic activities. This study examined blockchain technology's structure and financial applications. Our study concluded with positive proposals to further blockchain technology in cryptocurrencies, smart contracts, initial coin offerings, financial market transaction clearing and settlement, and publicly traded company governance.

Chapter 9 discussed decentralized financial applications (DeFi) use intelligent contracts and permissionless blockchain technologies. This chapter contextualizes DeFi within the theoretical framework of blockchain and related technologies and their possible real-world applications. The authors also examined basic DeFi ideas and application scenarios. They identified distributed ledger technologies, artificial intelligence, machine learning, and zero-knowledge proofs as three essential technologies that will change DeFi. Blockchain is the cornerstone of DeFi; AI and ML help enterprises give crypto insights to their consumers to cope with market volatility, and zero-knowledge proofs secure digital transactions in an uncontrolled, decentralized finance world.

Chapter 10 promotes industry discussion of cutting-edge blockchain applications. This chapter introduces blockchain technology, secure transactions, and the secure element. It addresses implementation issues and presents use cases for commercial or trial projects. The following paragraphs explain blockchains, bitcoin transactions, and transaction completion. Highlight Marco Polo's network. In this chapter, Marco Polo's blockchain and cloud technology will solve corporate pain points. The chapter covers blockchain's function, characteristics, and applications.

Chapter 11 manages blockchain stores of every network-generated transaction. Blockchains are encrypted copies of every register. Decentralized data is blockchain technology's main feature—a decades-long blockchain-based secure database platform. Blockchain can successfully preserve, identify, and develop ICH worldwide. It can be a secure database and a user-friendly market platform for natural ICH practitioners, products, and services. This study will examine Blockchain technology's merits in preserving India's ICH. The data is secondary. This study will aid ICH’s digital protection network security research.

Chapter 12 presents blockchain’s credibility in business to safe data. Blockchain has quickly acquired tech credibility. Data is a company's most valuable asset because it contains all information. Data leaks, piracy, and theft occur in many corporations, businesses, industries, and marketplaces, destroying databases and slowing progress. Blockchain saves these businesses and industries by securing their data and transactions. Blockchain records and manages data, making it difficult to hack or manipulate. A peer-to-peer node-connected “block” or “chain” stores transactional records. A “digital ledger” stores this. It duplicates and distributes transactions across the blockchain's network of computers.

Chapter 13 discussed blockchain's high security ensures data integrity. Cryptography encryption in the blockchain system protects data against attackers, fraud, manipulation, and theft, providing data integrity, confidentiality, availability, security, efficiency, and user privacy. Blockchain communications provide bilinear data integrity. Client, KGC, cloud storage server and blockchain make up the blockchain-based data integrity verification framework. Data integrity verification involves setup, processing, and verification. Simulations reveal that the blockchain approach for IoT data integrity outperforms the high-efficiency blockchain-based cloud data integrity verification scheme.

In this day and age, securely conducting business transactions is not possible. However, blockchain technology can assist businesses in protecting their data and mitigating the dangers associated with data leakage. The application of blockchain technology is helping to streamline the financial industry. If companies embrace this technology in all sectors, then there will be no need for individuals related to those businesses to engage in fraudulent activity.

This book presents an approach to business sustainability solutions that incorporates new technologies, such as blockchain technology, that is theoretical, practical, mental, and sociological. This book has the potential to make significant contributions to the advancement of business-related research and technology. Contributors from all across the world wrote the Handbook that you're reading. They have a large amount of expertise working with blockchain technology, making this a quality-oriented organization that can design healthy and sustainable solutions for a more productive and satisfying enterprise.

TARGET AUDIENCE

This book’s purpose is to serve as a reference for people who work in the business sector, specific producers, manufacturers, etc. The issues and case studies discussed in this book will assist practitioners in comprehending and disseminating the current state of the art and advancements in blockchain technology, business, and extensive data management to interested readers and researchers. Additionally, this book will help practitioners disseminate this information to interested readers and researchers.

# Chapter 1

*   Shweta Dewangan  
    ICFAI University, Raipur, India
*   Santosh Kumar Verma  
    School of Chemistry and Chemical Engineering, Yulin University, China
*   Bhanu Parganiha  
    Independent Researcher, Bhilai, India
*   Smita Dewangan  
    BA Continuum India Pvt. Ltd., Ahmedabad, India

ABSTRACT

Blockchain was originally intended as a means to enable a trustless cryptocurrency—Bitcoin. However, an increasing number of sectors and stakeholders view the technology as an attractive option to address existing business issues and disrupt traditional industries. Blockchain is a distributed ledger with big promise in financial services. Blockchain application cases have mostly been discovered in banking. Blockchain technology can help insurers innovate insurance products, growth products and services, fraud detection, and price improvements and cutting costs. Blockchain technology allows organizations to access enormous talent pools, readily verify facts, and not rely on third parties or third employers. Blockchain has become a new frontier for venture capital, attracting banks, governments, and businesses. In this chapter, the authors present a novel approach or methodology for utilizing blockchain technology to manage businesses or other types of industries.

INTRODUCTION

As a decentralized ledger, the blockchain is often associated with cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin. It collects and organizes data from several sources. The global network of computers maintains and updates a distributed ledger of all confirmed transactions. Decentralized systems, like a bank, feature multiple authorities instead of a centralized one. A broad community oversees the documents, and no, it is under the authority of a single person, and no one can reverse it. Modify or delete a transaction history. In contrast to a typical centralized database, the data cannot be retrieved and manipulated owing to the blockchain's distributed ledger design. In addition to peer-confirmed pledges, the nature of the structure should be considered. In other words, if a typical centralized database is employed, the blockchain would be stored on a single server but spread across several computers among those who utilize the application. Since Blockchain enables everyone on the network to access the entries made by others, it is more difficult for a single business to advance. Whenever a transaction is performed, its data is transmitted to the network, where computer algorithms determine the Verification of the transaction's legitimacy. Once the transaction is complete, this new transaction is linked to the preceding one after verification. A transaction constitutes a sequence of transactions. This is a chain known as the blockchain.

Currently, many business organizations have already implemented blockchain technology, and some businesses are transforming through blockchain technology. There are currently some blockchain networks that are already revolutionizing businesses-

*   1\. Supply Chain: Improve the efficiency of your supply chain by implementing multi-tier visibility and automating workflows. This is particularly important in the modern day since customers have higher expectations about the authenticity and longevity of products. Businesses have higher expectations regarding the integrity of their data and the speed at which they can reconcile differences with their business partners. Get the inside scoop on how Home Depot, Renault, and Atea use blockchain in their businesses.
*   2\. Digital Credentials and Identity: Verifiable credentials, such as mental well-being and education records, should be made available that do not violate an individual's right to privacy. Individuals should be given the ability to manage their information by using an encrypted digital handbag on their device and should be able to retain control over what they share, whom they share it with, and why they share it. You can learn more about the advantages of Ethos Veterinary Health, the state of New York, and True Tickets by reading the following.
*   3\. Digital Assets: The ability to trade any asset with greater liquidity, speed, and cheaper costs are made possible by creating a unique digital representation of an asset, which goes beyond the capabilities of standard financial instruments. Tokenize your digital assets and manage their entire lifecycles on a safe and scalable platform, with risk and compliance programmed explicitly designed for digital assets.
*   4\. Sustainability: Encourage a robust and long-lasting future for your company. Forward-thinking enterprises and industry leaders are adopting sustainability to create new value for their firms. Leverage the potential of blockchain technology to advance the common good and enable businesses to contribute to resolving global issues.

Enterprise-Wide

Organizations must have ecosystem-level coordination and optimization to maintain continuous growth. This is because businesses adjust to a new and unexpected sense of normal. You can drive business outcomes across various companies and sectors using trusted data, end-to-end visibility, and automated workflows.

Five Business Applications for Blockchain Technology

Emerging technologies such as blockchain are frequently discussed but must be fully comprehended. Blockchain is one such technology. Those who do not work in an area focused on blockchain technology or who wish to remain in their current professional lane are not likely to ever comprehend the technology's complexities, which is entirely acceptable. However, to better prepare for the future, owners of businesses would benefit from having a fundamental understanding of blockchain technology and how it could affect the world of commerce.

A distributed ledger, similar to a Google Document, is at the heart of blockchain technology. This type of ledger is only viewable by individuals granted permission to access a specific blockchain network. Every modification made to the ledger is subject to validation by the many computers, also known as nodes, that are a part of the chain. In this way, besides having the advantage of being available from practically any location throughout the globe, the blockchain ledger also provides a level of security that could be improved in older options. The fact that the blockchain is not indefinitely scalable means that certain activities, such as logging and processing Visa's daily transactions, are not suitable for implementation on the blockchain now. This is the disadvantage of using blockchain technology.

Let us explore how blockchain technology can be used to streamline and secure business operations:

Supply Chain Management

Supply chains are complex organizational and logistical networks that transfer items from their point of origin (manufacturer, extractor, farmer) to their final consumer (end-user). Globalization has made it more challenging to manage supply chains, particularly regarding the import and export of raw materials and completed items.

Most of these systems rely on centralized database structures. This can make it hard for supply chain managers to get reliable data on shipments and operations, which can cause friction within the supply chain. Even opacity and fragmentation might have this result. Blockchain technology integration might be helpful in this case.

Anyone with the correct permissions along the supply chain can contribute data to the decentralized ledger known as the blockchain. This would allow all parts of the supply chain, particularly the supervisor, to monitor the movement of goods and their treatment as they are processed and routed to their destination. Stolen, damaged, or counterfeit goods must be accounted for at every stage of the supply chain.

This could be useful in the pharmaceutical and food supply chains since counterfeit goods and food infected with the illness have the potential to contaminate the entire supply chain. Finding the entrance point for hazardous products into the supply chain could save money and lives.

Streamlining Regulators

There is one set of regulators for the government, another for the insurance industry, and another for the loan industry. Cooperation between the many regulatory organizations is becoming increasingly challenging. A corporation would have to halt its activities and sift through rooms' worth of documentation to impress regulatory authorities. The blockchain offers an alternative solution to this nightmare of audits and processes.

Blockchain technology continues to excite me. According to leaders from various industries, it might streamline operations, enable new business models, and even change industries. Commercial adoption of blockchain has been minimal because most organizations have been unable to overcome the constraints that have kept its value as potential rather than actual. Blockchain technology could become mainstream if these five dimensions are developed further.

Enhanced Record Keeping and Sharing

Business is only indirectly affected by public processes like record keeping and law enforcement, but these have an impact anyway.

Have you ever tried to visit your city's records office in person?

The likelihood is high that the answer is “no” if you are neither a journalist nor an attorney. To your credit, existing large-scale recordkeeping systems are a nightmare at best and nonexistent at worst.

Marriage licenses, arrest records, and corporation filings all take up valuable space, are a pain to manage, and have little practical value beyond the requirements of the law. Record requests would be simplified, and cities and businesses might save money by ditching antiquated infrastructure like filing cabinets and paper ledgers in favor of a decentralized digital ledger like the blockchain.

A system that allows for the safe and quick transfer of records and other sensitive information between law enforcement agencies would also be helpful. Many investigations never conclude because the relevant departments either lack the resources to disclose material that could have led to an arrest or are unwilling to wade through complex systems to share a single file with another. Companies, which are frequently the targets of illegal activity like fraud, also have a vested interest in this software.

The blockchain system would allow one agency or department to store and share information with another by just passing along a passkey. This likely leads to increased information sharing and the resolution of more cases due to the lowered obstacles to such sharing.

Voting

Voting affects almost every field. Boards, shareholders, and employees vote occasionally, some more often than others.

Democratic tallies are not always conducive to a practical or comprehensive ballot, especially with shareholder votes. Votes linked to a person's identifying information can be safely distributed via the blockchain. This method would make things more efficient and make sure democratic processes are implemented in a way that is sensitive to geographical and economic constraints.

Such a technology would automatically tally and record the vote, creating an uncontestable and permanent record for all parties. Blockchain has great potential for democratic voting systems across industries, whether for board members, company direction, or CEO pay.

Financial Management

Blockchain technology has the most potential in this area. Even though many of the uses of blockchain technology are most commonly linked with the financial services industry (such as regulatory supervision, voting, and record-keeping), their scope of applicability is far broader.

Speculatively and as an investment, we have witnessed the potential influence of cryptocurrencies on the global financial system. Blockchain technology, on the other hand, has the potential to safeguard user information better, expedite transactions (especially those that traverse international borders), and create an immutable ledger that foils fraud and other malfeasance (looking at you, Wells Fargo).

Blockchain technology can potentially have far-reaching financial consequences, prompting large financial firms like Barclay's and Wells Fargo to invest heavily in its study. This sector has much regulatory oversight, which is reason enough to implement blockchain widely. Meanwhile, the technology may help banks save money, which they may use to offer better rates to clients.

HOW DOES BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY WORKS FOR BUSINESS

The implementation of blockchain technology in commercial settings results in the generation of value for the many parties involved in the exchange of goods and services. The use of distributed ledgers enables participants who have been permitted to access the same information at the same time. This contributes to the improvement of efficiency, the development of trust, and the reduction of friction. In addition, blockchain makes it possible for a solution to expand in size and scope rapidly, and many systems based on blockchain may be adapted to carry out various duties across various industries. These benefits are afforded to companies that implement blockchain technology because of its possessing four qualities that are unique to the technology:

*   • Consensus: Only when a transaction has been validated by all relevant players involved and shared ledgers have been updated with the new information is it possible to consider the transaction complete.
*   • Replication: Once a block, which is the record of an event, has been validated, it is immediately added to the ledgers of all the participants in the channel in which it was formed. Every partner in the network is privy to the same “trusted reality” of the transactions they all share.
*   • Immutability: There is the potential to add more blocks, but the ones already there cannot be removed. Because of this, there will always be a record of every transaction, which will, in turn, increase the degree of confidence among the stakeholders.
*   • Security: Building blocks can only be accessed and used by those entities who have been expressly granted authorization to do so. Access privileges can only be granted to reputable business partners that can be relied upon.

Application and Uses of Blockchain Technology for Business and Different Sectors

Businesses worldwide and in various sectors are impacted by the distributed ledger technology known as the blockchain. Increased levels of trust make it feasible to avoid completing work more than once, which is one-way productivity can increase. The supply chain, food distribution, financial services, government, retail, and many more businesses are all currently in the process of being disrupted by blockchain technology. This includes a wide variety of other industries as well.

The Potential of Blockchain Technology and Its Revolutionary Financial Applications

Blockchain technology has been praised for bringing transparency, efficiency, and productivity to the financial services sector. Blockchain reduces operational and data breaches.

What Is the Function of DLT (Digital Ledger Technology)?

Even though blockchain is most widely used in the financial sector, distributed ledger technology (DLT) can potentially revolutionize various industries. These industries include manufacturing, sustainable energy, electronic health record management, and more. Blockchain is the technology that underpins DLT. A distributed ledger system may record, share, and synchronize electronic ledger transactions across multiple computers. This discovery could boost the financial system’s adaptability, productivity, and stability.

DLT could solve long-standing issues and reshape key financial responsibilities. PwC found that 56% of Indian enterprises want to use blockchain. The nation is ready to integrate blockchain technology into its economy and society.

Major Banks Adopting Blockchain

The Reserve Bank of India published a paper titled “Distributed Ledger Technology, Blockchain, and Central Banks” in February 2020. This article examines the usage of distributed ledger technology (DLT), often known as blockchain technology, and the most recent advances in blockchain technology and its application by central banks in India and abroad.

As a direct result of the objectives of the RBI, various financial institutions have begun to take advantage of distributed ledger technology (DLT) and the blockchain by developing partnerships with the fintech industry and investing in it. Axis Bank, ICICI Bank, and Yes Bank have joined up with the State Bank of India to use blockchain technology as part of JP Morgan's Interbank Information Network. Leading financial institutions in India, including HDFC Bank, State Bank of India, IDFC First Bank, South Indian Bank, and ICICI Bank, have all recently invested in equity shares of Indian Banks' Blockchain Infrastructure Co Pvt Ltd (IBBIC), which has caused a commotion in the digital lending market. These investments aim to provide solutions based on distributed ledger technology (DLT) for the financial sector.

Blockchain Finance Opportunities

*   • Transparency: Transactions made by blockchain participants are recorded chronologically and publicly. The business world is opening to the public. As financial institutions strive to mitigate risk, greater openness may help them spot inefficiencies and find solutions.
*   • Simplifying Operations: Blockchain technology allows financial organizations to monitor transactions in real-time and manage bank guarantees and letters of credit. It makes things easier for both the seller and the customer.
*   • Faster Settlement: Cash, cashier's checks, and wire transfers are safe. Cash is untraceable, cashier's cheques are falsified, and wire transfers take time. Customers have more trust in blockchain-based payments. Real-time fund transfers between financial institutions reduce friction and speed up settlement.
*   • Smart Contracts Enabling Automation: Blockchain disrupts banking beyond wire transfers. It automates and tracks financial transactions. Smart contracts let banks track money and products. Transaction issues are fixed. Automation reduces human errors.
*   • Improving Customer Experience: Most banks use blockchain for international payments, saving time and money. Blockchain money transfers allow mobile users to send funds online. They no longer have to wait in line at a money transfer center and pay fees.

Blockchain Technology: Applications and Uses in the Field of Information Technology

How Does IoT Work With Blockchain?

The Internet of Things enables devices to communicate data to blockchains housed on private networks so that immutable records of shared transactions may be generated. These blockchains can be situated anywhere on the Internet. Blockchain technology removes the necessity for centralized management and control and makes it feasible for your business partners to exchange and access Internet of Things data with you. Verifying each transaction is possible, which helps to lessen the risk of conflicts occurring and raises the degree of trust that members have in one another.

Applications and Uses of IoT and Blockchain

Transportation of Goods and Cargo

Freight transportation takes time and has numerous moving elements. A blockchain for the internet of things may track cargo containers' temperatures, whereabouts, arrival times, and statuses. Blockchain transactions are permanent so that parties can trust the data and transfer commodities quickly.

Compliance Monitoring and Tracking of Components

Tracking a product's components, whether an airplane, a car, or something else, is crucial for safety and regulatory compliance. IoT data can be maintained on blockchain ledgers and distributed across different computers, allowing stakeholders to access a product's component history at any moment. Share this information securely with authorities, carriers, and manufacturers.

Compile Records of Preventative Upkeep

Machine health, security, and upkeep can all be tracked through Internet-connected sensors. Using blockchain technology, operational data, and maintenance can no longer be altered. Everything from motors to elevators is documented in this journal. Preventative maintenance involving third-party repairs can be tracked by the blockchain. Data from operations can help government agencies ensure compliance.

Blockchain Technology in the Health Sector

The patient's information would be central to the system, enhancing data privacy, interoperability, and security. Patients would be at the center of the system under blockchain technology, which has the potential to transform the healthcare industry completely. There is a valid argument in support of taking this course of action. To put it another way, the use of blockchain technology in the healthcare sector has the potential to transform it completely.

After it has been thoroughly deployed, patients will be able to receive the attention they deserve at the center of all operations, which will result in significant changes to the system's level of accessibility, privacy, and security. This is one of the areas in which blockchain technology has the potential to make things better and is one of the areas in which it is now being tested in.

It can perform a broad variety of duties, including the management of epidemics and the secure encryption of patient data. These are just two examples. In conclusion, blockchain has the potential to improve digital health by making it easier and more secure to move data across a range of healthcare systems with the consent of the patient. This would result in an overall improvement in the quality of digital health.

Blockchain in Digital Health: Six Healthcare Blockchain Applications

Accommodating Multiple Sectors

Blockchain technology can link key healthcare industries such as hospitals, laboratories, and pharmacies, making it possible to exchange data in a safe and reliable setting. Most of the information provided consists of diagnostic records, previous treatments, prescriptions currently being filled, and insurance claims.

Faster, Cheaper, and Better Patient Care

Blockchain technology may create a centralized database that authorized individuals could access to store their medical records and retrieve them safely and on time. By eliminating miscommunication between the various healthcare staff members caring for the same patient, it is possible to prevent an infinite number of errors, arrives at diagnoses and begin interventions more quickly, and provide care more tailored to each patient’s needs.

Safeguarding Genomic Information

Theft of genomic data has emerged as a significant problem as many companies offer individuals DNA sequencing. This problem can be solved using blockchain, and the technology can even be applied to establishing an online market in which researchers purchase genomic data for their investigations. This might encourage secure selling while obviating the need for pricey middlemen to facilitate the transaction.

Smart Contract Insurance and Supply Chain Settlements

Pharmaceutical and medical device original equipment manufacturers (OEMs), wholesalers, insurers, and healthcare providers can all benefit from blockchain-based systems that verify their legitimacy, record contract terms, and monitor everything from product sales to insurance claims.

Protection of Patient Health Information

Companies in the pharmaceutical industry spend millions on the research and development of new medicines, but protecting their proprietary information may be even more vital. If such data repositories were to be compromised, pharmaceutical firms might quickly see significant losses in market share. To prevent unauthorized parties from decrypting, altering, or transmitting sensitive medical data, it is common practice to encrypt the data before its storage on remote servers and again before its dispersal into smaller pieces.

Improving Provider-Patient Communication

Using blockchain technology could be an efficient approach to fixing this problem. There are presently numerous distributed blockchain solutions available. As a result, data is not stored in a single repository but spread out over several network nodes. When information is changed in one node, the change is reflected instantly in all other nodes. It streamlines the process of instantaneously providing accurate data to any healthcare provider. In an emergency, providers may rapidly and easily access and analyze verified, up-to-date, and time-stamped data stored on a blockchain. It also allows doctors to diagnose conditions more quickly and provide individualized care.

Pharma supply chain transparency: In today's globalized world, where international trade can cause problems, verifying medical products' origin and supply chain is difficult. Fake prescription drugs and medical devices can kill tens of thousands of people in impoverished countries; thus, this is crucial. Companies at any point in the prescription drug supply chain may use blockchain technology to verify the genuineness of medicines, their expiration dates, and other relevant information.

Blockchain Technology in the Field of Education Sectors

When applied to education, Blockchain technology can speed up disseminating of high-quality educational resources while making them available to a broader audience.

Report Cards, Degrees, and Paperwork

Blockchain's immutable ledger records events chronologically. This can display student transcripts, report cards, attendance, and progress reports. Blockchain lets students submit assignments without losing them. Digital diplomas and degrees are also available. Digital degrees and certificates are convenient and structured.

Education Incentives

Consolidating Fees

Affordable and Universal

What's the Big Deal About Blockchain Technology for Properties?

Businesses across industries invested $6.6 billion in blockchain technology in 2021. It is 50% higher than in 2020. This is not when blockchain technology investments should decrease. IDC's Worldwide Blockchain Spending Guide (infrastructure development costs) predicts a 48% annual rise in global blockchain spending from 2020 to 2024. This shows blockchain technology's rapid global adoption. By the way, firms in several areas use this technology to improve service efficiency and cut costs.

Which Real Estate Issues Does Blockchain Solve?

Over the course of the past several years, the growth rate of the commercial real estate business has slowed significantly. It encountered some challenges, some of which continue to serve as obstacles to the expansion of this sector. While this is happening, the development of blockchain technology helps solve problems like these. Now that we have that out of the way, let us discuss the most pressing issues facing the real estate market and how apps built on blockchain technology might assist in solving them:

Insufficient Transparency and Sluggish Operations

A total lack of transparency amongst contractors is a significant issue in the real estate market, leading to corruption, fraud, and money laundering and stunting its growth. Shared, encrypted databases are a vital feature of blockchain technology, which has several applications in the real estate industry.

In this way, all real estate agents have access to the same information on leases, purchases, and sales, reducing the likelihood of any agent accidentally treading on another's toes. Multiple listing services, which compile property-level data from brokers' and agents' individual databases, illustrate the importance of this emerging technology.

The blockchain-enabled database can be used by many different entities, including genuine stakeholders. As a result, only third-party contractors participating in real estate management—including owners, tenants, lenders, investors, operators, and other service providers—have constant, instantaneous access to all relevant data and the means to update or add to it as necessary.

Unsafe Titles or Management of Data

Unreliable record-keeping is a second problem in the real estate market that impedes otherwise effective business operations. With the help of blockchain, each individual may create a secure digital identity that cannot be lost or compromised in any way. Mortgages, liens, and other forms of property financing are now more easily documented. Blockchain technology has enabled it to eliminate the requirement for middlemen like title companies.

Transactions are Slow and Not Safe

In real estate transactions, there are common to be conditional conditions, prolonged timetables, and safe transfer provisions. Blockchain makes transactions more efficient. Several factors could influence the outcome of a sale. Companies in the real estate industry can use blockchain to verify transactions and conditions. Better protection against malware is required in the real estate industry. The security of data encryption is improved by blockchain.

FIVE ISSUES ARISE FROM USING BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY IN ALL SECTORS

Scalability

Problems with scalability in accommodating a growing user base could plague an enterprise with a lengthy blockchain. Quite a few factors contribute to this situation.

To begin, the blockchain requires all participating nodes to store data from the very first block in the chain (the genesis block) to the very last block (the most recent block). These machines, also known as nodes, must be able to store the information at hand. As a result of the redundancy, the system is more secure, yet the system’s inefficiency is growing as the network and blockchain continue to develop.

After that, the node that validated the initial transactions must send a broadcast message to all the other nodes on the network to add a new block to the blockchain. Then, they can verify the transactions and add the block to the distributed ledger. This could use a lot of bandwidth and processing power as the network grows more prominent.

Energy Consumption

Proof-of-work blockchains can be energy-intensive. The proof-of-work system needs each node to do work. Bitcoin and Ethereum use proof-of-work. This method has nodes race to solve a challenging problem. As the network grows, rivals compete for computer power, consuming more energy. Energy is wasted because just one node will confirm the following block.

The proof-of-stake technique may help blockchains reduce energy consumption. Such a method has many drawbacks. First, a robust proof-of-stake system requires more difficult code than a proof-of-work system. Defects and security issues may rise.

Second, one entity may easily possess the majority of staked bitcoin, giving them too much power over the blockchain. Because a single actor would need to gain most of the network's computational power to exploit the latter flaw, proof-of-work models are less vulnerable. Other parties could gain computational power and take control of the blockchain while maintaining its decentralization. Despite these drawbacks, Ethereum is moving from proof-of-work to proof-of-stake.

Speed

Blockchain transactions are rapid for account-to-account transfers, but its decentralized structure makes it unsuitable for routine transactions. Account-to-account blockchain transactions are rapid.

You may quickly validate a debit or credit card transaction at a retail register. A network of payment processors moves money from your account to the merchant's account, although the process can take one to two days. The merchant can trust that the card issuer will pay for the transaction. Payment card networks execute thousands of transactions per second due to this confidence.

Transactions are not guaranteed until blockchain confirmation. Bitcoin's blockchain is entirely decentralized. Due to the Bitcoin blockchain's low transaction rate, this could take a long time. Retailers may wait an hour to discover if a transaction was successful. Despite the many financial applications of blockchain technology, it is unsuitable for most retail transactions.

Lack of a Global Benchmark

Most blockchain applications are unique. Developers of various applications and enterprises face many challenges.

First, it makes inter-blockchain communication harder. One enterprise may need to develop additional tools to move data across two blockchains. There are already dozens of blockchain interoperability options, but the fact that no one works for everyone shows how fragmented blockchain standards are.

Developers adding new content to blockchains creates the second issue (for example, a smart contract or a decentralized finance app). Because there are no standards, a developer must recreate everything to offer the same product on another blockchain. Without standards, developers working on unfamiliar systems may write more vulnerable code.

Privacy

Blockchain was designed for sharing. This means anyone can view blockchain data. All transaction details may be seen even with blockchain wallet addresses as identifiers. Although no one will care if you send a friend $20 in bitcoin, some data and transactions require more privacy.

Private blockchains restrict nodes and observers. Private blockchains are safer. A private blockchain lets a corporation use blockchain technology without sharing data, but it has limitations. Private blockchains are not decentralized since an authority selects who can participate. Consumers may distrust blockchain-based products.

# Chapter 2

*   Manjushree Nayak  
    NIST Institute of Science and Technology (Autonomous), Berhampur, India
*   Ashish Kumar Dass  
    NIST Institute of Science and Technology (Autonomous), Berhampur, India
*   Sapna Singh Kshatri  
    Shri Shankaracharya Institute of Professional Management and Technology, Raipur, India

ABSTRACT

The objective of this study is to build a model for the classification of traffic signs available in the image into many categories using a CNN and Keras library to detect the traffic sign. The goal of the traffic sign recognition is to build a deep neural network (DNN), which is used to classify traffic signs. The authors suggest training the model so it can decode traffic signs from natural images using the German Traffic Sign Dataset. This data should be firstly preprocessed in order to maximize the model performance. After choosing model architecture, fine tuning, and training, the model will be tested on new images of traffic signs found on the web. Because it deals with image classification, a convolutional neural network is chosen as a type of DNN, which is a common choice for this type of problem. The code is written in Python with use of tensor flow library. The proposed CNN model identifies traffic signs and classifies them with 95% accuracy. GUI of this model makes it easy to understand how signs are classified into several classes.

INTRODUCTION

In this era of Artificial Intelligence, humans are becoming more dependent on technology. With the enhanced technology, multinational companies like Google, Tesla, Uber, Ford, Audi, Toyota, Mercedes-Benz, and many more are working on automating vehicles. They are trying to make more accurate autonomous or driverless vehicles ([Harley, 2015](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r10); [Saini et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r25)). You all might know about self-driving cars, where the vehicle itself behaves like a driver and does not need any human guidance to run on the road. This is not wrong to think about the safety aspects—a chance of significant accidents from machines. But no machines are more accurate than humans. Researchers are running many algorithms to ensure 100% road safety and accuracy like Traffic Sign Recognition ([Jensen et al., 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r13)).

Autonomous vehicle is utilized in every field whether it is in road, water, sky for different intentions. In the advent of neural science development many researchers mainly depend on the automation of vehicle for different purpose ([He, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r11)). For road safety autonomous vehicle used as important tool for self-driving envelopes and path tracking ([Brown et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r4)). In underwater node localization the autonomy vehicle provides the accurate and effective result with less cost and improve lifetime of the underwater network ([Dass, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r5)). Sky–farming uses autonomous vehicle for agriculture and smart farming other application ([Ather et al., 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r3); [Yinka-Banjo & Ajayi, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r28)).

For all the autonomous point researchers have given emphasis on smart prediction using machine learning for various application such online retail market analysis ([Nayak & Narain, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r21)),

Dynamic product price prediction ([Nayak & Narain, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r20)), etc. As in real life while using road with heavy traffic there exist various traffic signs like traffic signals like turn left or right, speed limits, no passing of heavy vehicles, no entry, children crossing, etc., that you need to follow for a safe drive ([On-Road Automated Driving Committee, 2014](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r22)). Likewise, autonomous vehicles also have to interpret these signs and make decisions to achieve accuracy. The methodology of recognizing which class a traffic sign belongs to is called Traffic signs classification ([Diaz et al., 2015](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r6)).

In the real-world, traffic sign recognition is a two-stage process:

*   1\. Localization: Detect and localize where in an input image/frame a traffic sign is.
*   2\. Recognition: Take the localized ROI and recognize and classify the traffic sign.

Deep learning object detectors can perform localization and recognition in a single forward-pass of the network ([Ellahyani et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r7)). Various Traffic sign detection and classifications have proposed in various articles with description of neural network and image processing technology ([Garg et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r8); [Kamal et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r15)). Convolution Neural Network Model is an efficient way of description of traffic signal detection and data processing for accurate result ([He et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r12); [Karthikeyan et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r16); [Peng et al., 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r23)). Hence in our model we got approached with description of convolution neural network its description and various layer application and execution process. Our model takes the dataset for various traffic signals execute through our proposed scheme. Then it finds out accuracy using various matrices and displays the result in comparison with other available model.

MODEL ARCHITECTURE

The Model architecture is an overview of the entire model. In this architecture, the input is a traffic sign image, and the output is a name of the sign. In this case, it will notify about the traffic sign. The input shape is 30 × 30, and there are three channels. In the first two layers of the designed architecture, the filter size is 32 with padding, kernel size of 5,5,5,5 and activation function as ReLU. Thereafter, there is the first maxpooling layer, which has a pool size of 2 and strides of 2. The following layer is a flat layer that converts pooled features into a single column. Finally, two dense layers were formed. The first one has ReLU as an activation function, and the least dense layer’s activation function is softmax. After preprocessing, the features enter the network.

Convolution Neural Network

The most drastically identified and used in deep learning models are convolutional neural networks. CNN consists of one or greater convolution layers, pooling layers, and dense linked layers ([Mao et al., 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r19)). Dropout etc. can be delivered in different layers. One of the biggest benefits of CNN layers is that the model is realized the use of information at as soon as in the training device. The extraction of feature maps takes vicinity in the convolution and pooling layers. The classification system takes place at the end of the entirely associated layer. Defining Data: Standard dimension data is given as enter to the network. The indispensable thing proper right here is the size of the data. Performance might also moreover range relying on the model used.

Convolutional Layer

The convolutional layer is the basic layer of the CNN ([Qian et al., 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r24)). This is accountable for determining the design characteristics. The input picture is passed through a filter in this layer. The function map is obtained from the output of the same filters by convolution operation.

The multiplication of sets of weights with the input is performed by a convolution operation. A filter consists of a two-dimensional collection of weights multiplied by an array of input data. A dot product is a type of multiplication that is applied between a filter-sized patch of the input and the filter, which results in a single value. This product is applied between the filter-sized patch of the input and the filter. The filter is smaller than the input, and the same filter is used to multiply the input from different points. The filter is designed as a special technique to identify specific types of features as it systematically covers the entire image.

Assume that the NN input is, where A denotes the number of features that indicate an input frequency band and B denotes the total number of input frequency bands. The size of the filter bank function vector is represented by B in the case of filter bank features. Assume that, where denotes the function vector for band b. The activations of the convolution layer can be calculated as where is the jth feature map’s convolution layer output of the convolution layer band of kth, s indicates the filter scale, indicates the weight vector for the jth filter’s b th band, is the jth feature map’s bias, and (x) represents the activation function.

Pooling Layer

The pooling layer summarizes the presence of features by facilitating the down sampling features. It is normally applied after a convolution layer and has some spatial invariance ([Géron, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r9)). Two popular pooling methods, average pooling, and max pooling, summarize the average presence of a function, and the most activated presence of a function.

In fact, the pooling layer deletes the unnecessary features from the images and makes the image literate. In average pooling, the layer averages the value of its current view every time. When using maxpooling, the layer selects the maximum value from the filter’s current view each time. By using the matrix size specified in each feature map, the max-pooling technique selects only the maximum value, resulting in reduced output neurons. Thus, the size of the image becomes very small, but the scenario remains the same. A pooling layer is important for reducing the number of feature maps and network parameters, and a dropout layer is used to prevent overfitting.

The activation of max pooling can be calculated as follows where is the performance of the pooling layer of the jth function map and the mth pooling layer band, n is the subsampling factor, r is the pooling scale which is the number of bands to be pooled together, and n is the subsampling factor.

Flattened Layer

The flattened layer is used to convert data from the matrix into a one-dimensional array for use in the fully connected layer and to create a single one-dimensional feature that is both long and narrow. Flattening vectors are an option. Finally, it connects the single vector to the final classification model, which is also known as a fully connected layer ([Géron, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r9)). All pixel data are given in one and connect with fully connected layers. Flattening and fully connected layers are the last few steps of the CNN. It is prepared for the next fully linked layer of picture categorization by converting it into a one-dimensional array.

Fully Connected Layer

CNNs rely mostly on fully connected layers, which have proven to be quite useful in computer vision image recognition and classification. Convolution and pooling are the starting levels of the CNN process, which breaks down the image into attributes and analyses them separately ([Abadi et al., 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r1)).

In a fully connected layer, each input is connected to all neurons, and the inputs are flattened. The ReLU activation function is commonly used as a fully connected layer. The softmax activation function was used to predict the output images in the last layer of the fully connected layer. The convolutional neural network architecture uses a fully connected layer. These are the last few layers and important layers of the convolutional neural network.

The technique of a convolutional layer occurs as depicted in Figure 1: First, an enter image is convoluted with filters, consequently decreasing its size. Then, a new matrix will exhibit the outcomes of the convolution operations. These consequences are grouped via a pooling operation, which extracts the maximum values from small regions of interest ([Ketkar, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r17)). Finally, the grouped matrix is decimated by using a factor of two to produce the remaining result, via the decimation operation. The end result is carried out with the aid of a classification technique responsible to predict the label which summarizes the foremost content material detected on the image. However, nothing prevents the use of different models at this step.

Some studies observed this approach, the use of an SVM technique at the cease of the convolutional layer instead of the regularly used neural network. The layers used are a set of filters that are convoluted in order to generate a set of elements map. Among the convolutional layers used, there is a pooling layer, which operates over blocks of the input set and combines the activation features ([Jogin & Mohana, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r14)). This combining operation is described by way of a grouping function, such as common or maximum. This pooling technique step is used to summarize the close by outputs statistically in accordance with the pooling layer growing patches of characteristic maps. Since pooling downsizes the output, every other step of pre-processing the photo is zero padding, which permits us to control the measurement of the output. Padding is the manner of including pixels to the border of the image. After this function extraction step, this output is dispatched to a totally related layer. In general, a traditional CNN makes use of dense multi-layer perceptron as the classification method.

The Dropout Layer

The Dropout layer is a mask that nullifies the contribution of some neurons towards the next layer and leaves unmodified all others ([Aghdam et al., 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r2)). We can apply a Dropout layer to the input vector, in which case it nullifies some of its features; but we can also apply it to a hidden layer, in which case it nullifies some hidden neurons.

Dropout layers are important in training CNNs because they prevent overfitting on the training data. If they aren’t present, the first batch of training samples influences the learning in a disproportionately high manner. This, in turn, would prevent the learning of features that appear only in later samples or batches:

The Dense Layer

The dense layer’s neuron in a model receives output from every neuron of its preceding layer, where neurons of the dense layer perform matrix-vector multiplication ([Stallkamp et al., 2011](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r27)).

Matrix vector multiplication is a procedure where the row vector of the output from the preceding layers is equal to the column vector of the dense layer. The general rule of matrix-vector multiplication is that the row vector must have as many columns as possible like the column vector.

PREDEFINED MODEL

VGG-19

VGG-19 is a convolutional neural network that is trained on more than a million images from the ImageNet database. The network is 19 layers deep and can classify images into 1000 object categories, such as a keyboard, mouse, pencil, and many animals. As a result, the network has learned rich feature representations for a wide range of images ([MacKay & Mac Kay, 2003](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r18)).

So, in simple language, VGG is a deep CNN used to classify images. The layers in the VGG19 model are as follows:

ResNet50 and DenseNet121

We performed transfer learning on a popular image classification architecture ResNet50 pretrained on ImageNet. The actual motivation to use ResNet50 which consist of 50 layers was to strengthen feature propagation across different layers and encourage feature reuse ([Singh et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#r26)). It uses skip connections to add outputs of previous layers to the current layer which helps it reduce the residual error between the preceding and the cur- rent layer.

Secondly, we tuned DenseNet121 (total 121 layers) architecture pretrained on ImageNet dataset for traffic sign detection. DenseNet is the logical extension of the concept used in building ResNet architecture. The feature maps of all preceding layers.

CONVOLUTIONAL RECURRENT NEURAL NETWORK

It is another important neural network architecture is Recurrent Neural Network which uses hidden states of previous layers as inputs which makes it a suitable choice for sequential inputs. This architecture was implemented to explore if there exist any sequential relationship in the pixels which can be utilized to boost accuracy.

PROPOSED CNN ARCHITECTURE

Finally, we implemented a convolution neural network architecture that has two stacks made up of three convolution layers followed by one average pooling layer and two fully connected layers. We used batch normalization after every convolution layer to avoid exploding and vanishing of gradients while back propagating across different layers. Dropout layer was applied after each stack to avoid overfitting.

| Figure 5. Block diagram of proposed CNN architecture |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.f05](i/ch002.f05.png) |
| --- |

Fully connected layer is abbreviated as fc. The first two convolution layers have 32 convolution filters with kernel size = 3. Grid Search over different kernel dimensions: 3, 5, 7, 9, 11, 19 and 31 for convolution layers was performed. The highest accuracy was achieved with kernel size = 3. ReLU activation function was used as a non- linearity after each convolution layer which set negative input to zero. We did not use MaxPooling layer after every con- volution layer to avoid losing spatial information of feature maps at early stages. Subsequent three convolution layers have 64 convolution filters each to increase the receptive field. Moreover, the last convolution layer has 128 convolution filters. This is followed by an average pooling layer to gather essence of all received feature maps. Its output is given to a fully connected hidden layer consisting of 512 neurons. Final output layer has 43 neurons with softmax as activation function to calculate the probability corresponding to 43 classes of GTSDB dataset. Most of this architecture was designed using Random Search for various parameters.

EXPERIMENTAL RESULTS

In this section the results of our proposed models and its combination are provided.

DATASET DESCRIPTION

The image dataset is consisting of more than 50,000 pictures of various traffic signs (speed limit, crossing, traffic signals, etc.) Around 43 different classes are present in the dataset for image classification. The dataset classes vary in size like some class has very few images while others have a vast number of images. The dataset doesn’t take much time and space to download as the file size is around 314.36 MB. It contains two separate folders, train, and test, where the train folder is consists of classes, and every category contains various images.

Performance Metrics

We evaluate the performance of our model by various evaluation metrics which are given below. Confusion matrix was used to evaluate the performance of the classification models used in the study. Commonly used performance measures, accuracy, precision, and F-1 score values were obtained from the confusion matrix. To obtain these values, there are 4 values in the confusion.

![Mathtype978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.m01](i/ch002.m01.svg)

![Mathtype978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.m02](i/ch002.m02.svg)

![Mathtype978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.m03](i/ch002.m03.svg)

![Mathtype978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.m04](i/ch002.m04.svg)

![Mathtype978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.m05](i/ch002.m05.svg)

Matrix as True positive (TP), true negative (TN), false positive (FP) and false negative (FN) values. TP and TN indicate the number of correctly predicted positive and negative samples, FP and FN indicate the number of incorrectly predicted positive and negative samples. Performance metrics are calculated with the formulas using these four values. ROC (Receiver Operator Characteristics Curve) curves are also used to measure the performance of a classification model.

In ROC curve, sensitivity is shown on the y-axis and specificity is shown on the x-axis. These values are shown as dots and eventually converge, resulting in the ROC curve. The area under the curve is called AUC (Area under the ROC curve) and the AUC value varies between 0 and 1. As this value approaches 1, the predictive value increases, and as it approaches 0, the predictive value decreases. In [Figure 7](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#f07), the ROC curve and AUC area are shown.

RESULTS

After executing the experiments for different models, best results for each model were noted. validation accuracy achieved for each model over 15 epochs. First thing to be observed is that all models give an accuracy above 80% in the final training stage. VGG-19 gives the best performance (98.57%) amongst all the models and the new implementation (given by “Our Model”) gives comparable accuracy (98.27%) as well. Another critical observation is that RNN gives worst performance (89.54%) on validation data. This might be because the image pixels do not show good sequential relationship. Further, it can be noticed that models using skip connections, i.e., ResNet and DenseNet and Inception Module, i.e., GoogleNet give decent accuracy as well. A key observation in this plot is the accuracy in initial training stages. Although VGG-19 gives slightly higher accuracy, its initial accuracy is quite low. On the other hand, our proposed model gives decent initial accuracy.

Next, we observed the trends in all training, validation and testing accuracy at respective best hyper parameters settings represented by [Figure 10](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#f10). Training accuracy goes to around 100% for all models. The highest test accuracy is given by VGG-19 (98.12%) and our proposed model gives an accuracy of (97.71%). As expected, RNN gives the worst performance (89%) and ResNet (94.8%), DenseNet (95.59%). The next criteria these models were judged on is evaluation time. [Figure 11](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch002.xhtml#f11) represents evaluation time taken by each model to predict labels corresponding to the entire test dataset. Clearly, best results were achieved by our proposed solution performing around three times faster than VGG19. Furthermore, RNN not only gives the worst accuracy, but it is also the slowest model. Additionally, we wanted to explore the roughly 2%.

CONCLUSION

In this paper, various deep learning models were compared not just in terms of their classification accuracy, but also in terms of their prediction speed. Recurrent Neural Networks do not perform well as compared to Convolutional Neural Networks in terms of both accuracy and evaluation time for image classification purpose. One thing to be noted is that even simpler architectures namely, our proposed implementation and the VGG-19 outperform much more complicated, deep neural networks. Thus, performance of a particular model totally depends on the problem and data at hand, and it is not necessary for deeper neural networks to always surpass the rest. Additionally, our proposed model gives the best response time while providing reasonable accuracy of 95% and loss changes over a large dataset.

In this paper, an approach based on the combination of color transformation and Convolutional Neural Networks (CNN)is proposed. Working on the image preprocessed by color transformation, the CNN with fixed and learnable layers has achieved good results. The merits of the CNN we used are as follows: First, fixed layer can reduce the number of areas the classifier need to deal with, which could speed up the detection significantly. Second, the ROIs generated by fixed filter are very close to the borders of traffic signs, therefore the problem of alignment is avoided; otherwise, performance of supervised convolution network would degrade. Third, CNN with appropriate architecture learned in the supervised way has been proved to be suitable to extract features for traffic sign classification. Our experiment results strongly supported our conclusion.
