# Chapter 4

*   Mushtaq Ahmad Shah  
    Lovely Professional University, Jalandhar, India
*   Furquan Uddin  
    Aliah University, Kolkata, India

ABSTRACT

Taking into account the significance and hurdles of blockchain technology in construction work, the current study examines the potential of blockchain technology in the construction industry and how this disruptive technology could revamp construction projects. Using blockchain to simplify the contractual processes and paperwork associated with these complex projects could save money, free up valuable resources, and accelerate project completion. The blockchain records are tamper-proof, which will help enforce work compliance and discourage potential record fabrication. As a result, it makes sense to begin focusing on blockchain convergence efforts in these sectors and then extend them as the technology improves.

INTRODUCTION

The construction sector in India is expanding rapidly, with a 10% increase predicted over the next five years. It is the country's second-largest source of employment, employing approximately 50 million people. However, in the construction projects, a slew of additional undesirable factors such as design flaws, inadequate design documents and missed deadlines, budget overruns, and regulatory restraints have slowed several projects across the country ([Shah & Ghayas, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r24)). These factors cause half of all building projects in India to be delayed. Infrastructure projects need the collaboration of several suppliers and contractors, all of whom must be compensated for their efforts. The present invoice and payment method is sluggish, opaque, and prone to fraud. To achieve its $5 trillion economic objective by 2025 and to continue developing at a rapid rate, the government must use cutting-edge technologies in infrastructure construction. The key constraints identified may be considerably reduced by introducing new technologies in safety, transparency, payment processing speed, and sustainability. Blockchain technology has streamlined this process by generating a transparent and immutable record of all transactions and allowing assets to be tracked from manufacturing through distribution or usage by the end user.

[Seebacher and Schuritz (2017)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r23) define Blockchain as “a distributed database, which is shared among and agreed upon as a peer-to-peer network. It consists of a linked sequence of blocks (a storage unit of the transaction), holding timestamped transactions that are secured by public-key cryptography (i.e., “hash”) and verified by the network community. Once an element is appended to the Blockchain, it cannot be altered, turning a Blockchain into an immutable record of past activity.” In other terms, Blockchain is described as a growing collection of data known as blocks that are connected together via encryption. Each block includes a hash value of the preceding block, a timestamp, and the user of the data records ([Wang et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r31)). A blockchain is often operated as a public ledger by a peer-to-peer community that follows a mechanism for inter-node communication and verifying new blocks. Once recorded, the contents in any specific block cannot be changed retrospectively without affecting all following blocks, which needs network consensus agreement.

Blockchain technology (BCT) has been deployed in a wide range of sectors, including healthcare, manufacturing, and engineering. Because of its potential benefits, blockchain has sparked the curiosity of building researchers. Smart contracts, for example, are being used to facilitate security and privacy, preserve transaction records, and give anonymous information accessibility ([Das et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r6)). Novel blockchains are being used to solve information gaps in the construction supply chain. Blockchain has been integrated with the building information model (BIM) to improve the BIM working environment ([Liu et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r18)). Through the provision of transparent data and a clear communication of requirements and expectations through smart contracts that precisely describe the parameters agreed upon by the participating parties, BCT adoption can be a successful solution for the better management of construction projects.

The use of blockchain technology has the potential to improve project management and finance functions, including bidding, contract management, project planning, risk assessment, and insurance. Additionally, it would significantly shorten the timeframe and costs needed to transfer money for multinational projects from weeks to minutes ([Luo et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r20)). The construction sector may be able to overcome numerous barriers with the use of latest technologies like the “internet of things (IoT), building information modelling (BIM), and artificial intelligence” when combined with Blockchain technology. A framework stressing innovation, success, and sustainability has arisen because BCT platforms are decentralized, cutting superfluous bureaucracy will increase open communications, and BCT application will prevent disagreements based in the digitalization of corporate operating models. Due to upgraded technologies, improved communication technology, a shorter lead time, a better working environment, and higher-quality products, Industry 4.0 technology is essential for manufacturing industries and their long-term survival ([Kamble et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r12)).

Blockchain technologies are widespread, particularly in conservative sectors like the construction industry, which has been sluggish to accept technology innovations to enhance operations and workflows. Nonetheless, numerous solutions have overcome construction industry difficulties with exceptional outcomes. Blockchain-based contracts, smart contracts for transparency and traceability, and enhanced information flow are driving this expansion ([Wang et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r31)). Construction is behind other businesses (Ye et.al., 2018). Researchers must provide construction practitioners with a full analysis of blockchain applications, including their advantages and disadvantages in order to increase blockchain adoption. Blockchain uses in building are poorly understood. This research examines how blockchain technology might enhance construction project workflows and identifies the constructions industry potential, limitations, and issues.

MATERIAL AND METHODOLOGY

The construction industry has always been striving to improve productivity and effectiveness while also seizing the advantages availed by technology advancements. This study investigates the possibilities of blockchain in the construction sector and examines how this digital revolution has the power to disrupt the construction business by boosting the efficiency of procedures and operations. This research paper identifies the function and major elements that drive BCT adoption in construction organisations, as well as the causal links that affect and enable blockchain technology implementation in the construction sector. These influencing elements for BCT acceptance were investigated in order to assure a comprehensive blockchain integration in construction enterprises and projects.

A systematic literature analysis and search methods were employed to filter blockchain-related research publications from the Scopus and Web of Science databases. The research strategy and framework used a holistic approach in which blockchain adoption in the construction industry was based on three fundamental factors: drivers, rationale, and advantages. The conclusions of the study will assist construction stakeholders, businesses, and policymakers in understanding the complexities of the linked aspects that impact blockchain adoption and how to address them. The insights will also help the industry's important stakeholders deploy blockchain and related technology. The article emphasised the need of the construction industry investigating the many functions of BCT in order to measure the actual advantages.

Blockchain Characteristics

Technology breakthroughs have provided the groundwork for information sharing; nevertheless, these technical advancements confront limitations in achieving their needs, such as a lack of safety, transparency, confidence, and connectivity, to mention a few. These difficulties have been solved by a blockchain system. BCT, for example, safeguards data in IoT that is susceptible to hackers by preserving it in blocks indefinitely. (Amanullah at el., 2020). The unique characteristics of BCT that have evolved businesses and their systems are as follows:

| Figure 1. Blockchain characteristics |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.f01](i/ch004.f01.png) |
| --- |

Decentralization

Decentralization transfers authority and responsibility of major government functions from central to sub-national governments including local governments, civil society, and the private sector. A decentralized system is a network whose members can be computing nodes, manufacturing machines, robots, or humans. Every node is a part of the network that copies all blocks. The nodes verify data security in a network whose members are identified by an address (Arashpour et al., 2019). The platform is not administered by a single body. All operations in a widespread peer-to-peer connection are stored in a list of public blocks. No individual has total authority over the system, and each participant has the opportunity to see the data; accessibility is determined by the network's access rights. Because of the high degree of accessibility, all participating nodes check the recorded data; hence, no one can edit a specific document to their advantage. ([Wang et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r30))

Distributed Ledger Technology (DLT) is one of the latest innovations that has received the greatest attention. The widespread use of the cryptocurrency Bitcoin has increased interest in blockchain technology and distributed ledger technology (DLT) in general. A digital asset called a cryptocurrency is made to be a means of trade. Transactions and represented assets are protected against tampering and fraud by the use of encryption protocols ([Chohan, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r4)).

Consensus

Consensus algorithms create trust amongst unidentified peers in a computing environment and provide dependability in the Blockchain network, The consensus process serves as the foundational element of a blockchain network, ensuring that in such trustless settings, all participants concur on the network's state. The consensus method also controls the network's other functions, like transaction addition and motivating players to act ethically. Due to delay in connection or classical failures, nodes inside a blockchain may be defective, engaging in arbitrary or hostile behaviour, or holding inaccurate information. The consensus mechanism of a blockchain network assures that in such thrustless contexts, all participants agree on the network's state.

Blockchain Immutability

Immutability, often known as irreversibility, is a basic blockchain attribute resulting from the fact that operations cannot be altered or removed after they have been properly authenticated and stored in the blockchain. This attribute is the result of cryptographically linked blocks concatenated together with the hash algorithm of the previous (parent) block. Each block, in specifically, carries a reference to the prior block by adding a hash function of the transaction information in the previous block in its header. This cryptographic hash is calculated using a Merkle tree on all of the block's transactions.

Security

By enabling only approved stakeholders to transmit information, the BCT encrypted protocol ensures the validity of data and participants. In this aspect, the protocol employs a “one-way” hash function to turn any length input into a user defined output termed hash value. When a new transaction has been made, cryptography connects it to previous ones; this is how blockchain technologies are resilient and secure. Because an encrypted hash function encrypts data, attackers cannot foresee the starting data and, as a result, cannot modify or tamper with it (Fang et al., 2020).

Transparency

Blockchain has the ability to create a professional chain of evidence. This capacity promotes traceability and openness in the data sharing process ([Kshetri, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r15)). Transparency contributes to being conscious of the current state of the record. Transparency makes people understand the ledger's functioning. Furthermore, because various blockchain technologies allow users to search entries on a repository, members may track data with verifiable timestamps. A centralized repository cannot provide a consistent, safe, and transparent network for managing high-quality data.

Privacy

The term “privacy” in blockchain networks refers to two aspects: user privacy and transaction data privacy. The following are the two forms of transaction privacy in blockchains:

User Privacy (Anonymity): the capacity to change a blockchain user's genuine identity into one that cannot be recognized, while also assuring that the original identity remains unattainable. This is more usually referred to as anonymity. It hides the user's true identity by disguising the user's real network address with a computer-generated address. By concealing the details of an operation, blockchain data privacy is preserved. Confidential information is also known as privacy. At the simplest basic level, the data contents of a transaction are normally encrypted to ensure network secrecy. Maintaining data confidentiality protects transaction contents against unauthorised access, tampering, and alteration.

THE ROLE OF BCT CHARACTERISTICS IN ADDRESSING CONSTRUCTION ISSUES

Blockchain offers various possible applications in the early stages of an infrastructure project. Smart contracts and cryptocurrencies are the most apparent outcomes of implementing blockchain into a building project during the design stage. When the stages of a design process (identification, planning, purchase, execution status, and so on) are transferred to blockchain, every member in the chain validates and updates credible project information to work with. This strategy can reduce uncertainty, boost productivity, and minimize errors. The person who generated the block contains information on the identity and it may also identify who is responsible for specific information. Smart contracts can be utilized to link internal functions to a BIM model in order to increase supply chain transaction efficiency; to confirm important process events like as permitting and inspections; to enable financial transactions; to confirm modification orders; and to provide notice. The end result is a clear record of a project's procedure as well as the structure's as-built condition.

Major delays and disagreements in Indian construction projects are related with payment claim resolution, documentation, and stakeholder participation. BCT can handle a wide range of system difficulties by providing decentralisation, security, and transparency. Globally, capital construction activities have a highly fragmented, dispersed, and complicated supply chain. For example, at the height of its development, the Burj Khalifa employed nearly 12,000 employees from more than 100 nations. Massive effort and resources are required to manage such a large supply chain and keep track of work in progress, schedule, cost, and payments.

[Figure 2](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#f02) depicts the relationship between BCT features and related responsibilities and payment, collaboration, and documentation traceability issues in construction.

| Figure 2. Blockchain in construction |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.f02](i/ch004.f02.png) |
| --- |

Globally, the supply chain for capital building operations is highly unstable, scattered, and complex. During the peak of its construction, the Burj Khalifa employed almost 12,000 workers from more than 100 countries. To handle such a broad distribution network and maintain records of work in progress, scheduling, cost, and payments requires massive effort and resources. In addition to these challenges, construction projects encounter a range of mistakes, delays, and accidents at different phases and to varying degrees. In the construction industry, irresponsibility has been an issue for decades, and with profit margins so low, firms are searching for ways to minimise costs and avoid liability for the resulting tragedies. These are the key “pain points” and areas where blockchain technology might be utilised to make the process more effective, transparent, and responsible for all project participants.

Progress in Collaborations

Construction has always been a team effort including a large or small number of people. Communication technology has had a tremendous influence on the relationships of individuals connected throughout history. Construction projects are complex where great number of stakeholders are involved in the project leads to fragmentation issues among the professionals and organisations. Existing construction methods, on the other hand, face several issues relating to trust, knowledge transfer, and operational processes ([Wang at al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r29)).

| Figure 3. Blockchain collaborative project system |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.f03](i/ch004.f03.png) |
| --- |

However, distributed ledger technology, among other things, offers trust, data integrity, accuracy, confidentiality, and transparency, allowing management difficulties to be resolved. Construction designs, for example, might be provided at various stages, and all parties must be notified. However, because the stakeholders operate in distinct companies, numerous challenges may occur, such as identifying the most recent set of drawings, who issued the most recent drawing, whether it was included with the architectural/structural/service designs, and so on. All of these concerns may be overcome by using a blockchain system with smart contracts, since the most recent information can be updated and made available to the appropriate parties.

Blockchain technology enables people to securely interact, organise, and transact. As a result, BCT promotes user cooperation by increasing data sharing and decreasing hierarchy. key stakeholder of a construction project is part of a blockchain empowered network. In this way the governance of the network is based on the consensus and agreement of all stakeholders. Each participant would have their own, but same, copy of the blockchain, which includes every interaction and transaction between the parties. Interactions and payments are documented, tamper-proof, and cryptographically signed regardless of whether this information is private or completely contained for each stakeholder. This produces an easily traceable record of every commercial transaction of all parties in the past. In this manner, the complexity of project control and the risk associated with it might be greatly decreased, possibly by as much as the cost of administration. To improve operational efficiencies and traceability, cryptographic protocols (self-executing conditions that may be integrated in blockchain-based systems) can be used. With such a system, trust and collaboration among all players would be greatly enhanced.

Having Equal Right to Share Ideas

BCT fosters a culture of collaboration in which duties are clearly defined and risks/rewards are shared among participants. Smart contracts manage access responsibility and reward users and application owners for providing user data. By combining the security and immutability of data kept in the blockchain with the special characteristics of two prominent blockchains—MultiChain and Ethereum—participants may combine their advantages: adequate data storage and data sharing, as well as smart contracts for access control mechanisms. As a result, our new platform overcomes the inadequacies of traditional centralised user models employed by internet enterprises, which have security flaws, lack accountability, and deprive users of ownership, control, and incentives for data sharing. BCT defines process routes in each project, fostering trust and collaboration among stakeholders (Nawar & Ravindran, 2019). Because construction projects have a competitive atmosphere, BCT avoids unhealthy competitiveness among participants by granting equal rights to input data and equal standing in consensus decision making. In general, a blockchain is utilised to eliminate weak and distrusting interactions in physical areas, hence altering interactions in organisations.

Reducing Bureaucracy and Corruption

Building projects are participatory processes that require less red tape and greater openness. Due to knowledge asymmetry, players in traditional contracts shift away from immediate contractual arrangements and toward more and more expensive modes of engagement. BCT transforms a hierarchical organisation into a productive network by providing project stakeholders with a distributed blockchain and up-to-date information. This network results in a transparent interaction and awareness of the state of the site. Financial intermediaries can significantly decrease information asymmetry because they are more accessible and self-executed, which means that once ascertained and in operation, their information is available to all parties. If one of them is a public organisation, the information about the contracts is presumed to be available to the entire society. Furthermore, contracts cannot be deceptively amended, which helps to the contracts' promises and claims while also reducing asymmetric knowledge. Smart Contracts are self-contained and perform all actions without the assistance or intervention of a service provider, providing unrivalled transparency, enhancing efficiency, and reducing the possibility of malpractice in construction. Open communication is established for subcontractors, general contractors, inspectors, and other stakeholders, allowing persons to provide and access information in a systematic method ([Lu et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r19)).

Automation With Smart Contracts

Automation using smart contracts is a huge benefit, especially for business owners and executives. Smart Contracts shorten payment times, enable rapid payments, and automate payment processes. When developing smart contracts, be sure to include all of the payment conditions. When the required credentials are satisfied, the individual is automatically compensated. Ethereum smart contracts allow safe data exchange and high-integrity storage information ([Gürsoy et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r11)). One of the earliest blockchain-based apps, the Ethereum platform, allowed computer instructions to be performed on the blockchain. A smart contract is a major invention that has been revitalised by distributed ledger technology. Smart contracts, like conventional contracts, may set rules and automatically enforce them through programming. Smart contracts are inherently irreversible and cannot be erased by default. A smart contract's terms are expressed in a computer language that is also human-readable. When the established prerequisites in a digital contract are satisfied, the contract's provisions are automatically carried out ([Allen, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r1)).

Funding Management

Most building projects need significant investment from investors, particularly historic structures that serve as vital landmarks for a country. As a result, blockchain-based crowdfunding provides a fantastic chance for project clients to launch a secure platform to seek funds for their projects. The blockchain crowdfunding platforms are peer-to-peer fundraising platforms that potentially replace the requirement for traditional capital finance to start an enterprise ([Swan, 2015](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r28)). This is achievable through the development of own digital currencies and the sale of “cryptographic shares” to early backers. The blockchain crowdfunding platforms also provide investors and clients with trust because the usage of funds is completely transparent with the decentralized blockchain. The consequent outflow from the blockchain ledger may be used to trace the spending of project budgets. Furthermore, due to the decentralised nature of blockchain technology, all financing (outflow and inflow) financial transactions are unchangeable.

Development of Payment Systems

Blockchain innovations have energised smart contracts and payment systems in order to improve financial flows. Every precise transaction data on a cryptography network may be stored on a single encrypted digital gateway. As a result, it offers users with a protected site while also increasing execution speed ([Gao et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r10)). thus, the contractual conflicts and payment issues can be overcome.

Improving Cash Flow

Using a fictitious contract between an employer and a contractor as an example the execution of the contractual obligation can be eased by a blockchain-based consensus mechanism (Khan et al., 2018). The funds are restricted until delivery once the employer deposits a certain amount into the network at the start of the project. When a project is finished, it is ready for distribution, and the blockchain stores details like the time the job was delivered. An automated payment is issued when the supplied job's quality has been verified. However, neither client nor the programmer who wrote the algorithms may alter the contract or postpone payment by relying on the identity codes. Traditional auditing schemes may have restrictions on clients' pay-as-you-go service due to conditions like upfront payment. So, researchers created a system where a cloud service provider (CSP) and data owners manage a fair payment smart contract. CSP gives documentation proving its ownership of the data but is not paid until the verification is successful. The lack of confidence between the buyer and seller is a problem in conventional payment systems; a smart contract built on blockchain can address the issue. [Luo et al. (2019)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r20) used a blockchain-based smart contract to tackle construction payment issues. This technique formalises parties, their relationships, transaction sequences, and building contract clauses into smart contract code. Automation triggers interim payments following consensus.

A consensus mechanism verifies payment applications from authorised participants. Then, the blockchain ledger stores the application unaltered. The system will record any changes to recorded data. The agreement is issued to stakeholders after transaction confirmation. Hence, system security may be ensured without bank systems ([Wang et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r29)) With an immutable system, audit trails may be used to determine who is at fault for a late payment, whether it was the customer who delayed the payment permission (without legitimate reason) or the contractor who was late with the progress invoices. Thus, a paradigm of searchable encryption that relies on the combination of blockchain technology and searchable symmetric encryption is necessary ([Li et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r17)). This reasonable plan accomplishes a few of things. First, it finds the variance to identify erroneous results. As a second benefit, the blockchain's payment method protects the rights of all participants.

the potential blockchain-based applications involve different construction stages, from design and construction to maintenance. Various project management areas, including cost management, asset management, contract management.

Improvement in Documentation

The validity and reliability of construction quality documentation is critical to establishing confidence. So that all authorized individuals have access to legitimate data, approvals about the quality of raw materials and products, as well as the quality and amount of work progress, can be kept in the blockchain. Uncertainty arises in an insecure centralised system when participants are unaware of the state of the project and the activities of other stakeholders; this provides participants with an opening to exonerate themselves. During disagreements, an opportunistic participant might intentionally prevent other users from using the system. In addition, if the paperwork isn't clear, it might derail any attempts to settle the disagreement without going to court ([Gürsoy et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r11)). As blockchain records cannot be altered after they have been created, this technology may be used to ensure that all work is done in accordance with regulations and prevent any record forgeries. By increasing the safety and openness of document management systems, blockchain technology offers a solution to these problems.

Strengthening Security

BCT's primary use case is in the administration of databases with accurate record presentations. Having easy access to data is critical in such systems, but ensuring that data is secure against manipulation is also important. In order to do this, [Sheng et al. (2020)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r27) suggested a blockchain-based system. They came up with a method to boost the quality of project data and gave it the catchy moniker “the product organisation process (POP)”. In order to facilitate online cooperation between contractors, employers, consultants, and subcontractors, this framework provides a set of guidelines. They have joined forces by agreeing to work together on a project and by taking part in the POP quality Chain. The procedure is initiated once the asset owner generates a high-quality information document within the framework. The administrative team and the contractors work together to guarantee that the framework is implemented in all projects only once permission is granted to do so. A uniform, safe, and decentralised architecture provides high-quality information management for the accepted protocol. All parties involved in the project have access to a wealth of reliable data thanks to the framework.

Adding Transparency

The credibility and shared understanding that come from open communication are crucial in the conflict resolution process. Due to the lack of openness in centralised data management, a decentralised system is required to provide a shared, immutable, and auditable ledger of all transactions ([Dubovitskaya et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r8)). According to previous studies, blockchain smart contracts might help bring about complete transparency across the whole lifetime. An outside party initiates the workflow by making a networked request that specifies the document number, document verification, the next party, and the due date. Incorrect paperwork is the reason for the request. The architect then evaluates the submitted materials in order to evaluate the claim and reply to the contractor. Blockchain technology makes it possible to track who did what and when in any given workflow. As a result, the process may be seen at any time, and the records can be consulted in the event of a disagreement ([Pradeep et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r22)).

Cost Effective

Blockchain is a new disruptive technology that may increase transparency, lower transaction costs, ensure the safety of financial transactions, and streamline operational procedures. Court proceedings using smart contracts were far lower, saving both time and money. The majority of clients in today's construction business conduct financial and real estate transactions through centralised middlemen like banks and brokers. These middlemen collect fees from their customers in exchange for their services. Yet, the implementation of Blockchain technology in the building sector allows customers to avoid these middlemen and save money on transaction fees ([Current, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r5)). Project managers may have more influence over the budget and more effectively manage costs by integrating blockchain technology with BIM during the preconstruction phase. Quantity take-off (QTO) based on building information modelling (BIM) and linked to blockchain and smart contract for cost control and payment increased the security and privacy of critical cost data, as did a series of studies that used blockchain and smart contracts to facilitate cost management and trigger automated payment ([Ye et al., 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.xhtml#r32)).

LIMITATIONS OF BLOCKCHAIN IN CONSTRUCTIONS

While blockchain technology has the potential to revolutionize the construction industry, it also has some limitations:

Scalability

The construction industry involves large-scale projects that generate significant amounts of data. Blockchain technology can be slow and inefficient when it comes to processing large volumes of data, which can limit its effectiveness in the construction industry. Blockchain technology can be slow and inefficient, particularly when it comes to processing large volumes of transactions. As more users join the network and more transactions are added to the blockchain, the network can become congested and slow.

Limited Adoption

The construction industry is known to be slow in adopting new technologies, and blockchain is no exception. Many construction firms are still unfamiliar with blockchain technology and are hesitant to invest in it. As mentioned earlier, different blockchain platforms often use different standards and protocols, which can make it difficult for them to interoperate. This can make it challenging for construction firms to collaborate effectively and share data across different platforms.

| Figure 4. Limitations of blockchain in constructions |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch004.f04](i/ch004.f04.png) |
| --- |

Complexity

Blockchain technology is complex and requires specialized knowledge to implement and manage. This can make it challenging for construction firms to adopt and utilize the technology effectively.

Cost

Implementing blockchain technology can be expensive, particularly for smaller construction firms that may not have the resources to invest in the technology.

Security Risks

While blockchain technology is often touted as being highly secure, it is not completely immune to attacks. In particular, 51% attacks, where a single entity controls more than 50% of the network’s computational power, can pose a serious threat to the integrity of the blockchain. While blockchain transactions are public and transparent, the underlying technology does not provide complete anonymity or privacy. In the construction industry, this can be a concern for companies that want to protect confidential project data.

Energy Consumption

Blockchain technology requires significant amounts of energy to operate. The process of verifying transactions through mining requires vast amounts of computational power and consumes a lot of electricity.

Lack of Regulation

Blockchain technology is still largely unregulated, which can create legal and security issues. The decentralized nature of blockchain networks can also make it difficult to hold individuals or organizations accountable for fraudulent or illegal activities. Blockchain regulation is important for several reasons. One of the main reasons is to protect consumers from fraud and other financial crimes. Cryptocurrencies, a relatively new and largely unregulated asset class, have been vulnerable to scams and other illicit activity. By establishing rules and standards regulators can help reduce the risk of such activities and protect consumers from financial harm.

CONCLUSION

This study provides consolidated both quantitative and qualitative data to provide a deeper understanding of how distributed ledger technology potentially disrupt the construction industry. The application of blockchain in construction can bring about significant benefits, such as improved supply chain management, project management, smart contracts, and asset management. Blockchain technology can provide a transparent, secure, and efficient platform for all stakeholders involved in a construction project, reducing the risk of fraud, improving collaboration, and increasing accountability.

Blockchain technology is still in its early stages in the construction business and is currently in its exploration phase. The functions and possibilities of blockchain use in the construction firms, on the other hand, should not be underestimated. The potential applications of blockchain in the construction sector have been discovered, and these include contract administration, Building Information Modelling (BIM) systems, Electronic Document Management (EDM), property maintenance, supply chain management, and fund management. The decentralised, fully independent, peer-to-peer, and irrevocable record properties of blockchain boost work productivity, save time and money, and stimulate worldwide collaboration in the construction business. Business procedures and administrative operations may be automated using block chain-based smart contracts to boost efficiency and ensure that they always adhere to the contractual conditions. This can result in significant cost savings in construction and better project cost control. Blockchain can simplify the procurement process and reduce the fragmentation and complication of huge projects. The source of the materials has the potential to reduce waste and drive quality of products and services forward with high responsibility. These tools can increase predictability not just in procurement, but also in total project delivery.

Despite the foregoing, there are still numerous steps that must be performed before we can declare that Blockchain is an essential element of the construction process. One of the major obstacles of blockchain implementation in the construction sector is the government's approach to regulating blockchain technology in the nations themselves, namely the regulation of initial coin offerings (ICO), which are mostly utilised for project fundraising. Several nations have outlawed the use of blockchain technology in their countries due to the negative consequences of blockchain activities (such as criminal activity and unrestrained currency and financial outflow), whereas others have applied and developed more of the potentials of blockchain technology in their countries. However, another barrier to blockchain deployment in the construction industry is the industry's delayed acceptance of technology in comparison to other industries.

CONCLUSION

The 5G setup is an accelerator in favor of blockchain, and it is a form of body armor for the 5G arrangement. This upswings the need for a blockchain arrangement stage inside the threshold cloud to strategy recognizable proof, installment exchanges, and confidential information transmission aimed at terminals getting the 5G setup. Blockchain can offer advantages such as trust building, accelerated data exchange, cost reduction, improved security, ensuring accountability and immutability, efficient supply chains, automation via smart contracts, and decentralization. However, Blockchain-based IoT will suffer the challenges such as high storage capacity and scalability, high processing power and time, new security and privacy challenges, and legal issues.

The opportunities for Blockchain in India are ample and it is a necessary for a diffusive methodology too many extents to hold back money and time whereas safeguarding transactions. Despite the fact that in transaction management, 54% agree with the need for Blockchain as the awareness is less. As an ever-increasing number of state-run administrations, controllers, and organizations change their working productivity with blockchain, it determines India to be further advantageous in saving time and exertion. Blockchain in India has begun to be adaption across different areas, nonetheless, this could require an investment to get the acknowledgment of general society and carry out it across various interventions of everyday exchanges.

Ultimately, this study expresses that approach and guidelines will help in driving the development of the Blockchain environment and more advancements in India. India has a wary methodology towards molding the blockchain environment, i.e., India is still during the time spent giving a firm administrative direction towards Blockchain environment. The administrative climate requires a consultative methodology for the development of the Blockchain startup environment and advancement in India.

FUTURE RESEARCH DIRECTIONS

Future analysts can recognize chances to build awareness as well as utilization of Blockchain. This research is being focused on unambiguous geography also other geographic areas that comprehend the ways of behaving arrangements of diverse groups. In India, three significant telecom pioneers were viewed as implementing Blockchain technology rapidly. Airtel, Vodafone India, and Reliance JIO have started this cycle, while Vodafone is in the POC (proof of concept) stage. While Airtel is in the underlying stage and is doing the Research and development process. Furthermore, Reliance Jio has arranged different group exert and coordinate Blockchain to foster new use cases and applications (www.ETTelecom.com, n.d.).

The adoption of Blockchain in telecom environment would be helpful to various internal functions of the telco and would be driven by following factors such as (LinkedIn, n.d.):

*   1\. Existing telecom infrastructure topology & growth path of the telco.
*   2\. End customer requirement for use of Blockchain. This customer can be either internal or external.
*   3\. Type of speed of Transaction.
*   4\. Regulatory Compliance, i.e., data privacy and locations of servers.
*   5\. Size and speed of transactions required.
*   6\. Requirement of smart contracts.
*   7\. Opportunity to integrate blockchain with AI or other technologies.

The telco industry may not be ready for a public or hybrid blockchain today, but it is recommended to start with a private blockchain and gradually grow to the full-fledged blockchain offering and services for internal and external customers. This study is being propelled by our itemized overview of research concentrates on the union of blockchain plus 5G networks, that will bring up conceivable exploration headings which ought to be viewed as in later research they are:

# Section 2

# Chapter 6

*   Archi Dubey  
    ICFAI University, Raipur, India

ABSTRACT

Blockchain is a new technology that allows for the decentralized and transactional sharing of data across a large network of untrusted participants. It allows for new types of distributed software architectures. Although the technology was initially used primarily in digital currency, it is a promising technology in other areas as well. This chapter provides a simplified introduction to blockchain technology. It also discusses how blockchain technology can be used in some business processes in the retail sector to greatly benefit both customers and retailers. This chapter specifically discusses issues of retail supply chain management (SCM) and how blockchain technology is transitioning the retail supply chain management. The chapter discusses the market trend in blockchain adoption as well as some of the challenges.

INTRODUCTION

Blockchain is a digital system of transaction and data recording. It serves as a public ledger of shared digital events or transactions over a sizable network of unreliable individuals, or as a distributed database of records. Any industry that has previously depended on third-party verification is now disrupted by its removal. The majority of system members are supposed to confirm each transaction in the public ledger. Because information entered is immutable, it can never be erased.

Blockchain has drawn interest from a variety of sectors besides the financial one and has applications in the manufacturing, healthcare, consumer products, public sector, food business, and many more. Blockchain might have revolutionary uses in the retail supply chain.

Blockchain has been characterised, even in its infancy, as a shared ledger in which all financial transactions are recorded, so avoiding the inaccuracies that might happen when each party taking part in a transaction maintains its own data set for the transaction. Blocks, including timestamped blocks, serve as the blockchain's data structure by listing aggregated data on transactions that have taken place inside the network. With both financial and non-financial applications effectively applied, blockchain technology is undisputed and has operated without a hitch for years. To learn how retail businesses regard blockchain, we polled a large number of retail professionals as part of this research. The findings are shared and discussed in the parts that follow.

Permissioned and unpermissioned blockchains make up the majority of the market. When using open distributed ledger technology without authorization, the data is shared across a number of machines in the network rather than being owned by a single person or database. Those transaction records are viewable by anyone with network access. The information is timestamped once a transaction is recorded and cannot be changed or removed again. New records or later additions to the ledger are monitored and updated in real-time for all users. Due to its distributed nature, where each copy is present in a distinct location, blockchain is challenging to hack.

Similar to permissioned ones, they can restrict who in the network can authorise transactions. By creating a cryptographic key pair in conjunction with wallet software, a blockchain makes it possible for transactions to be made securely online.

Simple contracts can be created using distributed ledger technology and will be carried out when specific requirements are met. A blockchain project called Ethereum was created with this objective in mind. Although it is still in its infancy, it has the potential to fully use the benefits of blockchain on a worldwide scale.

The retail sector is now driven by data. In order to grow their clientele and enhance customer service, retailers are seeking to concentrate more on personalised retailing.

As an enabler, blockchain technology will help retailers more effectively accomplish their goals. A few of these procedures are mentioned here. In the retail industry, blockchain can assist merchants in enhancing their current business processes, which will spur business growth.

This chapter focuses on a few retail-related situations where blockchain technology can be employed to the advantage of both consumers and retailers. Several components make up the document. The general definitions of Blockchain technology are provided in Section 2, while the evolution of retail supply chain management is covered in Section 3. The main problems with retail supply chain management are covered in Section 3, along with solutions that leverage Blockchain. Section 4 looks into how it might be used in the retail industry, and Section 5 identifies potential difficulties. The paper's sixth and final section is Section 6.

Definition

Blockchain definition has not materially evolved in previous years. Using blockchains, transactions can be recorded in a decentralised and transparent manner ([Boucher, Nascimento, & Kritikos 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r4)). This is the biggest definition, and Lewis may narrow it down even more (2016). He adds that the blockchain maintains a network of databases that are synced amongst various entities, and it records more than simply transactions ([Lewis 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r17))

However, before becoming the single term it is today, the word blockchain was initially split into two words: block and chain or chain of blocks. These phrases first appeared in “paper Bitcoin of Satoshi Nakamoto: A Electronic Cash System which maintains Peer-to-Peer”. Satoshi Nakamoto, a pseudonym for the person largely regarded as the inventor of Bitcoin and blockchain, wrote the paper under this pseudonym (2017) Bashir There is no precise description of blockchain in Nakamoto's article because it focuses on introducing Bitcoin, a solely peer-to-peer form of electronic cash, and outlining its characteristics. But there are a few important blockchain-related aspects that are discussed. He says that an electronic coin is a chain of digital signatures, which is an indirect definition of chain. Additionally, a group of goods is referred to as a block.

GENERIC ELEMENTS OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY

Transaction

[Bashir (2017)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r3) defines a transaction as the movement of value from one address (identifier) to another. Information and data not only created and held by users but also information and data owned by other parties are all included in the transferred value. A transaction is the exchange of a digitally recordable asset between two contracting parties, such as data, money, or contracts ([Seffinga et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r27)).

Block

Block A block consists of numerous transactions as well as additional elements like the block hash and date ([Bashir, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r3)). A fixed-length digital fingerprint or unique identifier is a block hash (hash pointer) ([Gupta, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r9)). By incorporating the previous timestamp and creating a chain with each new timestamp strengthening the previous ones, the timestamp establishes the data's existence at the time necessary to enter the hash ([Nakamoto, 2008](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r20)).

Node

Depending on its location, a node can perform a variety of activities. In order to foster consensus and protect the blockchain, it is in charge of 15 mining as well as suggesting and authorising transactions. A node may also be assigned validators and straightforward payment verification tasks. (2017) Bashir Among other node types, a network may contain complete nodes and lightweight nodes. The former can store a full local copy of the ledger and process proposed transactions in line with all consensus mechanism criteria, but the latter can only retain a subset of the replicated ledger's data. in [Nelson (2017)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r21).

Peer-to-Peer Network

One of the essential skeleton's bones that makes up the blockchain is this network topology. As suggested by the name, network peers are able to directly communicate and exchange messages. A peer serves as both a server and a client (requestor) at the same time in order to share resources and have direct access to those of the others (Steinmetz & Wehrle, 2005; The Government of the HKSAR, 2008). The generic elements are shown below in [Figure 1](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#f01).

RETAIL SUPPLY CHAIN MANAGEMENT

Blockchain enables retailers to resolve this customer conundrum and build confidence. How? Each product is given a “digital ID” that secures all of its information from its point of origin to the retail retailer. “At its core, blockchain is a distributed database ledger that uses cryptographic protocols for data sharing, validation, and authentication across a network of nodes”. By maintaining an immutable chain of data, the network enables trust less parties to concur on the existence and state of data stored there. The ever-expanding retail supply chain can be made more efficient and reliable by using blockchain as a platform for mass collaboration and the implementation of collective self-interest.

As you read this, blockchain is still in its early phases and developing. However, there is little doubt that it can enhance the collaboration and transparency of the supply chain. Retailers who can free up their supply chains will be better able to operate efficiently, project a stronger sense of brand value, and deliver a vastly improved consumer experience (www.tcs.com/Unblocking-retail-supply-chain-with-blockchain-1017-1.pdf).

To cut waste, accelerate monitoring, enhance risk prevention, and improve accountability, Walmart is promoting the use of “Blockchain technology in the food supply chain”. The retail behemoth submitted a number of patents pertaining to Blockchain, one of which was for a “Smart Box” system, or a set of apps, that would provide data on a package's contents, environmental circumstances, location, and more. Starbucks is also experimenting with blockchain technology. Pilot projects are being run in Colombia, Costa Rica, and Rwanda to help the business track its “bean to cup” coffee and exchange real-time supply chain information. The major French supermarket chain Carrefour has introduced the first “food Blockchain” in Europe that tracks its animal and vegetable products using the Blockchain technology. Smartphones can be used by consumers to scan QR codes.

Blockchain Supply Chain in Retail

Although the manufacturers seem to be at the top of the supply chain for retailers, many of them are equally worried about the moral standards of their suppliers. This could cover a variety of topics, such as the provenance of the parts that the manufacturer receives, statements about the sustainability of the raw materials, regulatory compliance, waste management, working conditions, and much more.

Regrettably, licenced agencies, foreign distributors, and the brands themselves regularly keep retailers cut off from the manufacturing process. How can they ensure that their suppliers' sustainability and ethical production claims have pleased them and their clients? Currently, this can be done by simply looking at a certificate, which may or may not be authentic.

The legitimacy of raw materials is guaranteed by a blockchain supply chain, supporting claims of fair trade, sustainability, ethical sourcing, and organic production. Additionally, it permits the verification of provenance, giving the battle against fake goods a much-needed boost. This kind of completely interconnected supply chain increases transparency for all parties and enables theoretical planning to be improved with the most recent data without having to wait for the previous stage to be finished.

Transitioning Retail Through Blockchain

The e-commerce revolution began to affect the retail industry in the late 1990s, and retailers who switched from their conventional systems to e-commerce are aware of the potential of disruptive revolutionising technology. The broad range of potential applications for blockchain and the necessary infrastructure modifications will alter the retail industry.

The most significant stakeholder in every retail industry is the consumer, and their trust is both difficult to gain and simple to lose. Blockchain represents a new development in a time when more and more transactions are conducted online. While the retail industry won't be completely transformed by blockchain overnight, there will be enough changes in the next few years to alter the way that retail is currently conducted with deliberate innovation and the right amount of investment.

The adoption of Blockchain technology by retailers can help them greatly with their current business operations, which will help them develop their businesses, according to later sections of the report. Here are a few major areas where this development may be made to help merchants enhance their current business operations, which will promote business expansion. Here are several conditions in which this advancement can be made.
