Key Issues in Retail

Changing Consumer Expectation

Consumer expectations are one of the many volatile elements that the retail industry must deal with. In reality, consumer expectations appear to shift quite quickly, making it difficult to keep up. There are simply too many factors at work to change consumer tastes overnight. For example, a large portion of consumers may love a product today, but the next day, they may have different opinions, and some may no longer like the product. Seasonal changes, events, holidays, and trends are all important factors in this. As a result, the retail industry must be astute enough to comprehend all of this information. However, accurate forecasting is still impossible to achieve today.

Retail Price Inflation

These days, consumers must spend an increasing amount for their basic needs. Due to this, individuals frequently have less money available to spend on any luxuries. However, the inconsistent retail prices and large price tags further deter buyers from making purchases. Retailers must thus provide special deals or discounts to get shoppers to buy their goods.

Retail businesses, however, are currently unable to handle the high expense. What gives, though? In other words, improper automation and planning may lead to increased manufacturing costs, which in turn may lead to higher retail pricing.

Ineffective Modern Marketing Strategy

Yes, a lot of retail organisations are increasingly integrating online marketing into their operations. They wind up investing a lot of money, though, with little to no outcomes since they lack the necessary knowledge on the subject. You can sell your products to customers in a variety of methods; you shouldn't restrict yourself to social media.

These days, consumers prefer one-on-one interaction with their personal brands. They might become disinterested in your brand if you can't give them this functionality. Once more, retail businesses may overmarket their goods, which would result in more unhappy customers.

Counterfeit and Forged Goods

One of the biggest problems the industry is facing is the rise in fraud and fake goods. Internal fraud activities also have an effect on income, and there are too many counterfeit goods on the market, which damages the reputation that retail organisations have worked so hard to establish.

Contrary to popular belief, it is getting harder to spot fake goods, which is making customers increasingly angry. Both the client and the retailer will continue to lose money if the problem is not resolved appropriately. Therefore, retailers need to deal with this problem as quickly as feasible.

Cyber Attacks

The growing frequency of cyber-hacks is another obstacle for retailers. The majority of retail establishments really employ the quite insecure client-server networking approach. As a result, breaking into servers and stealing confidential data is a rather simple process. Additionally, since every retailer stores information about its customers, it is comparatively easy to manipulate that information and obtain access to their credit card or bank account details. This might easily turn into a serious security breech, which would cause a huge loss of revenue. Additionally, it suggests that customers are not confident in you with regard to their data, which is bad for your business.

How Blockchain Is Addressing Issues in Retail SCM

Although the financial industry has been successfully using Blockchain technologies for many years, Blockchain technology has the potential to disrupt a variety of other industries such as retail. Some of the advantages of applying blockchain technology in retail supply chain management are as following-

Customer Data Management, Security, and Sharing

Large volumes of client data are regularly registered, stored, and used by retailers. In this area of their work, blockchain applications for simplifying operations represent a less-explored yet very potential topic. Utilizing distributed ledger technology will improve security, allow users more control over their data, and open up new marketing possibilities, assisting merchants in more efficiently meeting customer wants and gaining the value they might have missed otherwise. Retailers will be able to identify the demands of customers and market highly tailored discounts to them with the use of an artificial intelligence-powered recommendation system. Retailers would be able to reward those who have decided to open their data by giving them spendable tokens at the store in exchange for the lowered advertising costs.

By sending retailers shopping lists in the form of smart contracts that make use of the Blockchain data-sharing network, customers can also communicate with them about their wants and needs. Blockchain could serve as the main platform for the future of fully automated shopping when combined with the Internet of Things' potential to assign order execution and distribution to computers.

Transforming Payment

One of the first online merchants to accept Bitcoin payments was Overstock in 2014. Online merchants of all sizes are now accepting cryptocurrency payments, but it will take some time before digital wallets and cryptocurrency payments are widely accepted in the retail industry. The great majority of online shops also frequently accept traditional modes of payment due to the notable fluctuation in bitcoin pricing, while some have found implementation to be too challenging. This implies that the potential of this online payment room is also largely unrealized.

Retailers who are open to experimenting with cryptocurrency payments will open up a new revenue stream and draw in more customers from around the world. A further benefit of blockchain technology is that it allows businesses more control over how their customers distribute and use coupons and discounts. MasterCard, for instance, has applied for a patent for a system that verifies discounts using Blockchain technology. Retailers may more effectively offer highly targeted discounts and avoid coupon abuse thanks to the programme. In order to enhance its programme for rewarding loyal customers, American Express is now experimenting with blockchain technology. The capacity to assist shops speed online payments and reduce fraud is among the most interesting prospective uses for blockchain technology.

Improved Customer Identity Administration

As you are aware, the majority of retail businesses use the very unsafe client-server networking approach. Because of this, it is fairly easy to manipulate that data and obtain the customer's credit card or bank account details. This is a flagrant breach of privacy and a contravention of state law.

Companies in the retail industry can use blockchain to safeguard customer data to solve this problem. In fact, they can authenticate systems and store all data in immutable storage. The customer will only have access to their information as a consequence, and no outsider will be able to hack the system and misuse that information.

Rationalise the Retail Sales Tax

The practise of lowering retail sales tax is another benefit of blockchain in retail. In actuality, sales taxes are an annual requirement for all retail businesses. Many people do, however, attempt to avoid paying this $14.5 billion tax! Additionally, the majority of this tax fraud is committed by internet businesses. Companies could be charged with felonies if this keeps happening.

Blockchain-based automated sales tax collection can help with this problem by helping to collect taxes based on the volume of sales or profit produced. Retailers can also upload their documents, which the network will then verify. This approach is excellent.

Transactions Between Individuals and Merchants

Many retailers are already striving to use this amazing blockchain function. Blockchain can enable person-to-person transactions in a physical store. Actually, both sides gain from the payment procedure. The benefit of taking money directly from the client without utilising any third-party applications is that the customer is not charged more for using other third-party financial services. Additionally, this could promote continued trust between the two parties, which is extremely advantageous for retail businesses.

Reduction in Counterfeit Items

For manufacturers of high-end consumer products, a fall in the price of the upscale good can be caused by the sale of counterfeit goods, which can result in significant losses for the producers and retailers. For this kind of issue, blockchain serves as a straightforward, verified answer. By allowing a blockchain digital ledger record to store all information about every product, including those that are counterfeited, the spread of forgeries can be prevented. Therefore, in these situations, when the end-user receives the package deal of goods, try a code that is totally carved into the product so that you may access all the data stored inside the blockchain's virtual ledger document. retailers vulnerable to product counterfeiting.

Logistics

The same blockchain-based record-keeping tools will make logistics tracking easier and more effective. Logistics experts and customers will be able to inspect and update the blocks that make up your order fulfilment transaction rather than depending on inaccurate and asynchronous databases.

Blockchain-powered logistics is demonstrated by a warehouse employee scanning a tag as they get ready to send a product. The scan on the blockchain provides an immutable record, unlike the initial step in the majority of warehouses today. The manufacturers may have scanned the same tag, updating the same blockchain that you and your customers will read to obtain the same information, if everyone in your ecosystem has access to it. Any node in the supply chain can initiate a smart contract (a programme that operates without the involvement of a human user) by scanning a product, for example, to automatically complete electronic cross-border customs paperwork. A logical outcome of blockchain-based logistics is confidence. People can feel secure knowing that everyone has access to the same information, from the manufacturing plant to the warehouse to your customer's home.

B2B and B2C Smart Contracts

Everywhere you employ software in your retail operation, you should think about transitioning to smart contracts. B2B transactions will be completely automated, and B2C transactions will be able to benefit from a customised experience that is adjusted according to a set of criteria. Smart contracts are the “meat-on-the-bone” of blockchain technology. They'll use previously unheard-of methods to automate the flow of data.

Unlike conventional software, smart contracts can operate without a user's input. These types of B2B and B2C transactions can be automated by using smart contracts, which will assume control and carry out your predefined commands. Some of the most intriguing potential of the blockchain is encapsulated by smart contracts. Engineers might someday replace current huge clouds with these in order to decentralise all data storage. They refer to the seemingly endless possibilities of blockchain and what this technology means for merchants like you now and in the future, knowing that it will be very challenging to forge or trick smart contracts on the blockchain.

Promotions and Vouchers

There's no reason to confine yourself to documents for internal use. Blockchain can also be used to produce coupons and provide discounts for your goods. Again, you may use the security and immutability of the blockchain to more accurately organise your promotional campaigns. You'll be able to calculate the precise effects of each campaign on your business since you'll constantly be aware of how many coupons you have distributed and how many your consumers have utilised. Running your promotions on the blockchain has additional branding advantages. Your coupons will take on a new flavour once your customers realise you are setting a significant forward trend.

Paperless

You will be able to save any warranty you offer with your items as a link to a block on a blockchain rather than producing a physical paper to represent it. To prevent future changes to the file, the external file will have a “checksum” that may be compared to the block.

Going paperless and using it to increase your brand's green credentials would make you feel good knowing that paper demand exceeded 399 million metric tonnes in the previous year. You will also receive the extra benefit of having all of your papers go through the same anti-counterfeiting procedures. You can edit a block to manage your records rather than printing out a piece of paper that can be readily duplicated.

Integrations for Digital and In-Store

By applying the same reasoning, you'll see that you can use blockchain to represent yourself as a forward-thinking shop everywhere you can incorporate it into your physical location or online site. Practically speaking, cross-channel blockchain interfaces will make it simpler to uphold enduring interactions with your clients. Due to the tracking features of blockchain technology, you may first establish a connection with a consumer through your e-commerce platform before realising they are also making purchases from you online. In that instance, you can tailor each customer's online experience based on the learnings from their blockchain data.

Fostering Disintermediation

The Internet “made disintermediation possible and fundamentally altered how firms distribute their goods and services” ([Buhalis & Licata, 2002](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r6)). By eliminating reliance on existing middlemen and creating new types of electronic intermediaries, new technologies have replaced outdated trade practises ([Cort, 1999](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r7)). New online middlemen have also emerged as a result of the Internet, offering a variety of goods and services ([McCole, 2004](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r18); [Pineda & Paraskevas, 2004](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r23)). The provision of trust is one of the services new e-commerce intermediaries provide.

The popularity of social media has also highlighted how crucial it is for businesses to interact with their clients on platforms like Facebook, Twitter, and YouTube. These platforms rely on data and targeted advertisements to make money rather than on user fees for content and services. Furthermore, according to [Nieves and Diaz-Meneses (2016)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r22), “They have developed a positive feedback loop wherein increased consumer engagement leads in higher expertise and improved content integration.” These digital intermediaries gain the ability to lock businesses and customers onto their exclusive platforms while also assisting them by personalising their brands and offerings.

Changing Customer Profiles

A very country's economic, social, legal, and political changes, as well as most crucially the changes in technology, can result in major changes in the psychographic profile of the customers. Customers' buying behaviours are never static, so it may seem to them that following their behaviour is simple work, but this isn't the case, according to a few research studies by the organisation Project Guru. Today, collecting and preserving such complex and voluminous data is a significant challenge for marketers.

Marketers who work with several stores will implement a blockchain solution. Blockchain technology can be used to create sophisticated information storage systems for retailers because the data stored on the distributed ledger and retrieved through it are both permanent records. This information can then be used to forecast the specific needs of a given neighbourhood, recommend and store enough goods, and improve their just-in-time inventory facility. The amount of information generated in the modern e-commerce world, where same-day and same-hour deliveries are used by all retailers, is enormous. Blockchain serves as the most efficient platform because it can store and retrieve ongoing information, as well as have algorithms that can predict a customer's next purchase. A significant factor is already being produced by the distributed ledger system across its computing network.

Transparency

Customers, retailers, and suppliers can see the data stored in the blockchain and are able to see the product supply, whether or not it was made with child labour, and whether or not there are any harmful or hidden components. These details help retailers and customers make decisions about the product. This blockchain-enhanced openness can more correctly identify client behaviour patterns.

Loyalty

Blockchain will modernise the loyalty programme by securely storing customer information such as coupons and discounts and making it available to all or specific retailers for deeper analytics on customer data. Customers can read all of their loyalty information from all of the retailers in one place thanks to a blockchain loyalty guarantee.

Retail Companies Using Blockchain Technology

Retail customers today are more sceptical and concerned about the veracity of the data offered or the claims made by the seller about the goods. On the other side, it will help the store acquire the consumer's trust if it can give the information or statements in a way that they cannot be disputed. The use of blockchain technology here will be very beneficial. The following businesses are utilising blockchain technology-

Walmart

Walmart is creating blockchain-based retail food traceability systems. For this, they are making use of Hyperledger Fabric. They are now collaborating with IBM to get the necessary technical assistance for the solution. The retail giant has already finished two blockchain proof-of-concept initiatives that were successful. They claim that blockchain can help them maintain food quality and prevent any negative effects.

Amazon

Amazon is making the most of blockchain technology's promise. In their advertising business, they are currently utilising blockchain. Actually, because it can greatly increase their effectiveness, this is an effective use of Blockchain properties. In addition, they offer network creation based on the client's demands when selling Blockchain solutions like Blockchain as a service.

Alibaba

Blockchain-based retail solutions are something else Alibaba is working on. Blockchain technology is being used by the business to enhance product traceability. Additionally, they have already created the Kaola cross-border e-commerce platform on the blockchain. All customs clearances, logistics information, and product registrations will be tracked via this platform. They plan to use it to combat fake goods with a two-dimensional code as a result.

Aldi

Blockchain is being used by Aldi to control its retail supply chain. Currently, they are validating their corporate purchases with technology. With Verifish, they are monitoring Irish seafood that is both sustainable and organic. The project was also developed by Verifish, and the solution is being helped by BIM. The project will last a year, but the company wants to finish it by 2021.

Challenges

Since blockchain technology is still in its infancy, is still at the proof-of-concept stage of development, and few blockchain-based systems have been deployed on an industrial scale, real risks to it may not become apparent for several years until it is more widely employed. This technology should be thoroughly researched before being employed; rash decisions should not be made. Systemic risks could result in catastrophic consequences from failure to implement. As a shared ledger system, blockchain is anticipated to hold sensitive data as well. In order to comply with industry best practises, it must make sure that its encryption and cyber-security are robust. For cloud-based retail solutions, data security and segregation should also be taken into account.

Laws and Standards

Blockchain is currently subject to little or no regulation ([Kumar & Mallick, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r14)). The adoption of blockchain is facing considerable obstacles from regulations and laws. Without them, supply networks cannot rely on the technology ([Kamilaris et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r12)). Lack of regulation is also problematic because it is unknown how the system should be set up and new rules could be implemented at any time, requiring modifications to the system that has already been established. 2019 (Saberi et al., n.d.) Lack of standards and regulation lead to a diverse range of blockchain implementations with poor interoperability (Tribis et al., 2018; Kesharwani, 2019).

Security

The original blockchain has clear data. Because it was intended to be that way, everyone has access to the data. However, access must be limited because some data may be private for supplier chains. Blockchain does not by default support this, and it is challenging to implement. This has been acknowledged by certain blockchains, like Hyperledger, which has enabled access control ([Rabah, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r24); Behnke & Janssen, 2019).

Privacy

Despite being regarded as a very safe technology, blockchain does have some drawbacks. Even while a 51% attack is generally improbable, it has the potential to take over the entire blockchain. It happens when one party takes control of the blockchain by acquiring 51% of the pool's power. The type of power may vary based on the validation technique. For proof-of-work, 51% of processing power would be required (Aliyu et al., 2018; Mohsin et al., 2019). Additionally, blockchain has certain design problems. There is no “getting it back” if you “lose” the private key because it is both too crucial and too light. Users must wait a while before being sure that a transaction has been successfully completed on the blockchain because of the way the nodes communicate.

Adoption

It is challenging to convince parties to cooperate in order to devote time and energy to developing blockchain systems. Despite the fact that it might significantly improve future competitiveness and possibilities, the cost and risk are both substantial (Kesharwani, 2019). Most blockchain technology is experimental and unproven ([Rabah, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r24)). The creation of new IT tools would be required if blockchain were to be used. Some people in the supply chain might find this challenging ([Saberi et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r26)). In addition, because blockchain has the potential to be a disruptive technology, existing legacy systems may need to be changed or possibly replaced ([Saberi et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r26)).

Management

The success of supply chain practises is largely dependent on top management's support. On the other side, managers might not have the long-term dedication needed to encourage the adoption of new technologies and uphold sustainability.

Lack of knowledge and dedication will jeopardise choices on how to spend money and allocate resources. Adoption of blockchain would be challenging in the absence of proper corporate policies since blockchain could change organisational cultures ([Saberi et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r26)). In addition, blockchain may change how businesses interact and conduct business ([Edwards & Chan, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r8)). Blockchain will drastically reduce human intervention due to automation and the elimination of intermediaries, which will lead to employment losses. However, adopting blockchain calls for the development of new positions, duties, and knowledge. Everything must be controlled ([Saberi et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r26); [Kamilaris et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch006.xhtml#r12)).

RECOMMENDATIONS

Businesses that wish to benefit from blockchain should approach cautiously, according to a Deloitte report. They recommend beginning with “trial projects” that highlight the benefits of blockchain technology for a particular company model. Before using blockchain more broadly, they think it is wise to “experiment, wait, and see.” They advise all business owners to properly design blockchain integrations, but more crucially.

If you are a retailer, you might want to think about creating a loyalty programme that uses blockchain technology or converting an existing loyalty programme to work with a blockchain system. In this manner, you can personally see how blockchain functions within your business model. As you plan your promotional activities, keep in mind the advantages of blockchain technology: you know what you're giving away, no one can fake it, and you represent yourself as a technologically advanced company. This is a win-win situation for your business, and it will probably cost considerably less than you anticipate because the majority of the blockchain technology code is made available under open-source licences on websites like Github. As a retailer, you should also think about how blockchain may affect privacy regulations like the General Data Protection Regulation. In the future, customers may be able to view which businesses have accessed or may access their data thanks to blockchains' ability to store individual data preferences on shared blockchains. Even if this is a longer-term factor, retailers should be aware of it.

CONCLUSION

An economy's foundational component is marketing. In terms of marketing as a function, blockchain technology, which is disruptive, has gotten less attention. Although blockchain has been researched in many different industries, its use in marketing has gotten less attention despite having a lot of potential. In light of the integration of blockchain and marketing that has gaps. The advent of blockchain technology has revolutionised social, financial, and technological developments. Blockchain is now regarded as a reliable and secure technology, despite initially being referred to as a disrupted technology.

As you read this, blockchain is still in its early phases and developing. However, there is little doubt that it can enhance the collaboration and transparency of the supply chain. Retailers with clear supply chains have the chance to boost operational effectiveness, project a stronger brand image, and deliver a vastly improved customer experience.

The blockchain technology was covered in this essay, along with some of its main characteristics and advantages. The technology is still developing, has a wide range of uses across numerous businesses and domains, and is ready to revolutionise the world. It does, however, face several difficulties, some of which have been recognised. Blockchain is the technology that underpins Bitcoin, but it has uses outside of the financial industry. The retail sector will start to benefit from blockchain through increased product transparency, improved supply chain management, a better loyalty management system, improved customer profiling, counterfeiting prevention, and other factors, leading to higher profit margins for retailers and increased customer satisfaction. In 2016, it was discovered that blockchain technology would disrupt the retail sector more than radio frequency identification (RFID).

# Chapter 7

*   Sadhna Bagchi  
    Aaft University, Raipur, India
*   Debasis Mohanty  
    Kalinga University, Raipur, India
*   Ram Girdhar  
    Kalinga University, Raipur, India

ABSTRACT

Blockchain technology is creating triumph all over the world with its presence, and the combination of artificial intelligence and blockchain technology will prove to be more impactful in the coming decades. The various countries of the world are assimilating blockchain technology in the financial trading system because it is robust and secure. It has been observed that after the pandemic, Indian consumers have overcome the fear of using online transaction systems. The usage of smart gadgets, speedy networks, increased product services, and the flood of startups has created a conducive ecosystem for online financial transactions. This study attempts to assess the use of blockchain in financial sectors by various countries. Blockchain is the next generation of technology for any field that needs security, privacy, speed, and accuracy. Financial systems is a field facing problems like financial fraud, data theft, late settlement, and data manipulation. The use of blockchain technology on a large scale will create a secure financial ecosystem in India.

INTRODUCTION

The changes in the external environment bring threats and opportunities both for any business, some of the external factors which change the phenomena of any business are government policies, legal policies, social and cultural changes, changes in tastes and preferences of consumers, environmental concern, and technological changes etc. Out of these all the change in technology is inevitable, constant and fast compared to other factors because it is a well-known fact that to be in competition companies keep upgrading the technologies to gain a competitive advantage. In this era of innovation and creativity technology is the tool to create differentiation in the market. Since when the use of the internet started, we have seen new technologies coming very fast and it is creating opportunities for businesses more than threats, come it may email, Facebook, artificial intelligence, meta verse or blockchain. Every new technology has its pros and cons such as blockchain. The use of blockchain technology with bitcoin created a new digital currency which is not in a regulation of any central authority of any country. This has brought forth the problem of parallel economy in front of all countries where the creation of digital currency is in the hands of any individual who knows how to develop the cryptocurrency instead of the central bank of that country, but the concept of cryptocurrency solved one of the problem of digital currency i.e. ‘Double spending’. Digital currency is a digital file that can be falsified or duplicated but cryptocurrency could not because it uses the blockchain technology which is peer to peer computer network use as a public distributed ledger. Blockchain files cannot be altered because every block is securely linked together using cryptography, so it is almost impossible to change the data secured through blockchain technology. This security feature of blockchain has been proved path breaking for financial institutions who were worried about growing online financial transactions and threat of frauds related to it.

Blockchain Technology

The word blockchain is a buzzword heard frequently in social as well as in electronic media. Still, it is a mysterious word for few the country men. The use of block chain is very frequent in banking system and other online transactions. The technique may seem straightforward, yet data may be compromised. Transaction records can easily be modified. Blockchain solves the problem of record keeping and other insecurities.

In a blockchain, a block is a collection of data that may be accessed as a whole. A blockchain works by adding data to a block, which is then linked to other blocks in a chronological order to build a chain. Take a program like a Google doc or Microsoft excel (Windows). It's safe to assume that everyone with access to any of the many networks has their own copy of this spreadsheet. Personal financial dealings are recorded in the spreadsheet. That spreadsheet is view-only; only the original creator can make changes to it. Blockchain technology operates in this way. Instead of “rows” and “columns” in a spreadsheet, it employs blocks.

| Figure 1. |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch007.f01](i/ch007.f01.png) |
| --- |
| *Source: [https://medium.com/@ipspecialist/how-blockchain-technology-works](https://medium.com/@ipspecialist/how-blockchain-technology-works)* |

The blockchain is a decentralized and unchangeable database that keeps track of the activities and assets of businesses. Assets can also be intangible, such as a company's reputation or a patent, for example (intellectual property, patents, copyrights, branding). On a blockchain network, nearly anything of value is capable of being recorded and shared.

Benefits and Properties of Blockchain Technology

Distribution

There are a great number of copies of the ledger spread out across the network. Everyone connected to the network gets a copy of the newly added transaction and block whenever one of these is added. Although there is no one body that manages the ledger, the system is designed to make sure that all users have access to the same information.

Immutability

comes from the fact that a blockchain can record transactions in an accurate and chronological order. Because every participant in the network has their own copy, it is extremely difficult, if not impossible, to change or delete entered transactions or add information that has not yet been confirmed. To accomplish this goal without failure would need a well-coordinated assault on hundreds of thousands, if not hundreds of hundreds of thousands, of computers all at the same time, which is highly implausible.

Transferring of Money

Consumers and banking institutions confront many challenges when transferring money abroad. Despite being time-consuming, difficult, and error-prone, people send hundreds of billions of money across international boundaries each year.

Blockchain could change that. Many of the world's major institutions have adopted blockchain-based international payments, saving time and money. This technology allows money transfers consumers to make electronic transactions from their electronic devices, eliminate the need to visit any money transfer centre, stand in line, and pay fees. Most money transactions occur at banks and credit card processing centers. Each stage adds complexity and prices that can build up quickly. Blockchain-based transactions offer shops several benefits:

*   • Lower prices Credit card processing fees, lower a business's profit when customers pay with plastic.
*   • Reduce transaction fees by streamlining money transfers.
*   • Bad checks result in losses and fines for merchants.
*   • Possibility of legal bother to recover the monies.

Block chain-based payment solutions can give business owners piece of mind in seconds or minutes.

Financial Inclusion

The reduced overhead associated with blockchain technology is helping startups compete with traditional financial institutions, which in turn is expanding access to banking services. Due to issues with minimum balance requirements, limited availability, and high banking costs, many consumers are looking for an alternative to traditional banks. With digital identity and mobile devices, blockchain technology can offer an alternative to cumbersome conventional banking.

Fraud Control

A blockchain ledger is organized so that each block contains transaction data as well as a hash that references the hash of the block that came before it. All network nodes are given a duplicate of all completed transactions. These characteristics make blockchain technology resistant to attacks such as distributed denial of service (DDoS), hacking, and other fraudulent schemes. Reducing the price of doing business for everyone when cyber dangers are eliminated is a win-win situation.

Cryptocurrency

Bitcoin and other digital currencies are the newest type of asset to use the blockchain. The use of digital currency is not new, but blockchain firms are making it easier to transact with the most popular cryptocurrencies.

Transaction details

The revolutionary potential of blockchain in banking extends far beyond just money transfers. Blockchain is a reliable and secure system for keeping track of financial transactions and other sensitive data.

| Figure 2. |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch007.f02](i/ch007.f02.png) |
| --- |
| *Source: [https://medium.com/@kalyanicynixit/applications-of-blockchain-technology](https://medium.com/@kalyanicynixit/applications-of-blockchain-technology)* |

Elements of Blockchain Technology

*   • With distributed ledger technology, all nodes in a network can have access to the ledger and its immutable history of transactions. By centralizing record-keeping in one place, this distributed ledger eliminates the need for double entry of data, a problem plaguing traditional business networks.
*   • Immutable records: Once a transaction is recorded in the distributed ledger, no other user can make changes to it or remove it. A new transaction must be created in order to undo an error in an existing one, and both transactions will be visible if the error is discovered.
*   • Smart contracts: A prearranged set of instructions known as a “smart contract” is uploaded to the blockchain and is then automatically carried out in order to speed up transactions. In addition to many other things, a smart contract may be used to establish the requirements for the transfer of company bonds, include the terms for the payment of travel insurance, and more.

Impact on Financial Service

*   • Peer-to-Peer: There is no governing or controlling body. All of the applicants have one-on-one conversations with one another. This paves the way for direct data exchange without involving any intermediaries.
*   • Distributed: Since the ledger is replicated over the whole network, it is virtually hard to modify the data once it has been recorded.
*   • Secured Cryptographically: This technology apply cryptography for the secure services to make the ledger tamper-proof.
*   • Add-Only: Data must be included in this system with time-successive requests. A time-consecutive request is required to add data to the blockchain. This feature suggests that once data has been posted to the blockchain, it is extremely difficult to alter. To all intents and purposes, this holds true.
*   • Consensus: This is the most important part of the blockchain because it enables the distributed ledger to be updated through a process called consensus. This demonstrates that blockchain technology possesses the capacity to decentralize. The ledger is not being kept up to date by a centralized authority of any kind.

BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY IN FINANCIAL SECTORS

The digitalization of financial products and services, which comprises digital assets, programmable money and smart contracts, these all extends the benefits by generating unprecedented levels of connectedness and programmability across enterprises, services, assets, and holdings. This is accomplished through the introduction of virtual assets, Smart contracts and crypto money. The operational functions of commercial and financial markets will be changed or advanced as the introduction of these digital instruments, which will build a new paradigm in which value will be created at every point of interaction.

Blockchain technologies like Bitcoin ([Nakamoto, 2008](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch007.xhtml#r12)) and Hyperledger (2015) have had a significant influence on conventional financial and commercial services. According to Peters et al. (2015), blockchain has the ability to change the financial industry. Numerous industries, such as the clearing and settlement of financial assets, might benefit from the use of blockchain technology. Furthermore, [Morini (2016)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch007.xhtml#r11) and [Zheng et al. (2018)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch007.xhtml#r20) showed that blockchain has valid business applications, such as the collateralization of financial derivatives, which might reduce costs and risks. With services like Blockchain-as-a-Service now being offered by IBM and Microsoft Azure (Azure, 2016), major software companies are also paying special attention to blockchain (IBM, 2015).

Assets Management

Liability risk management, dynamic decision-making processes, and dealing with constantly shifting regulations are all things that the venture capital, private equity, real estate fund, and specialty markets sectors must improve. The mutual funds and other assets management company work hard increase the fund value but in the back end they need support to get the details of fund they get from investors and the allotment of units to their account. Blockchain serves the purpose and reduces their work significantly and flawlessly.

Blockchain helps manage assets and stakeholders. It allows:

*   • Auto-funding.
*   • Digital assets and services seamlessly involve stakeholders.
*   • Digital or virtual portfolio and holdings for speedy market access, fractionalization, and liquidation of holdings.
*   • To minimize the possibility of error, shareholder voting rights and others responsibilities are embedded in digital assets.
*   • Customizable transactions and privacy settings.
*   • Incentives to increase participation and punish bad behavior.
*   • Better investor governance and transparency.
*   • Cap table efficiency.
*   • Fund automation.

Banking Sector

The key functions and important aspects of a bank are its transaction, lending, and mortgage, and provide services on payment basis. Most of these services are dependent on outmoded procedures so that they can continue operating. Individuals should anticipate that the processing and funding of their mortgage application would take between 30 and 60 days, whilst small and medium-sized organizations should anticipate that their application will take between 60 and 90 days. The banking and lending industries stand to gain from the implementation of blockchain technology due to its ability to streamline procedures, reduce counterparty risk, and quicken the issue and settlement of transactions. This makes it possible for:

*   • Financial documents to be verified in real time using KYC/AML data and other authenticated documentation, hence lowering operational risks.
*   • Networked user behavior and sanctioned data can be instantly compiled to inform credit prediction and credit score markets.
*   • Reducing the time, effort, and expense of syndication through the use of automated processes for syndicate formation, underwriting, and delivery of money (principal and interest payments).
*   • Collateralization of assets was made easier because of how real-time asset management, tracking, and regulatory control enforcement are made possible by digitalization.

Insurance Sector

Insurance claims for property and casualty can take a long time to process and are subject to fraud. Further the premiums received online are needed to be allotted to the policy holders without any flaws. Blockchain accelerates the processing times for data verification, claims processing, and disbursement. It helps in managing the database of policy holders It will enable the system to:

*   • To reduce the risk of and facilitate assessment of claim it allows integrating an authenticated documentations and KYC/AML data.
*   • With use of smart contracts application allow to automate claims processing.
*   • To pay out upon incidence of certain risk it allow to automate parameterized contracts.
*   • Automated disbursement of insurance payments.
*   • It smooths the progress of policy reinsurance in open marketplaces, take beyond from conventional broker system and relationship-based systems a tokenized reinsurance markets.

Trade Finance

Protecting and enhancing the trade finance lifecycle is something blockchain technology can do. It has the potential to lessen fraud, human error, and counterparty risk while simultaneously speeding up processing times and decreasing capital requirements.

*   • Documents (such as letters of credit and bills of shipping) can be digitized and authenticated, and KYC/AML data can be verified in real time.
*   • To reduce settlement time, digitize assets.
*   • Funding methods made possible by digitalization and shared secure networks are more effective.
*   • Develop a single finance mechanism applicable across the whole scope of the trade lifecycle to do away with the current practice of negotiating separate vehicles for different phases.

Facilitate Compliance

The commercial and financial sectors have recently recognized the need of regulatory compliance. It is essential for ensuring that financial institutions comply with all relevant laws, rules, and regulations. When a company operates internationally, it faces the additional difficulty of being subject to the rules of various jurisdictions. These are some of the advantages that blockchain technology provides:

*   • Digital assets with built-in governance and compliance features that automate data verification and reporting, ease regulatory monitoring, cut down on operational friction, and get rid of human mistake in manual audits and other procedures in real time.
*   • Establishing and enforcing incentive mechanisms for better network administration.

Blockchain for Stock Market

In order to reap the benefits of this technology, Sebi is taking preventative measures now so that it can learn how it is being used in markets around the world. The NYSE and the Deutsche Borse are just two of the many worldwide exchanges and market authorities that have signaled their intention to investigate the potential benefits of blockchain technology.

Blockchain might track securities lending, repo, margin financing, and systemic risk.

Blockchain can solve fragmented market system interoperability, trust, and transparency challenges. Stock market participants like traders, brokers, regulators, and stock exchanges must execute transactions in 3+ days due to intermediaries, operational trade clearance, and regulatory processes. Blockchain automation and decentralization can improve stock markets. It can lower customer commission expenses and speed up transaction settlements.

The system can be used in clearing and settlement, safely automating the post-trade process, and simplifying trade documentation and security ownership transfer. Smart contracts would impose laws and regulations with each trade to register transactions, making the blockchain network a regulator for all transactions.

Global Payments and Remittances

Global payments and remittances are handled by intermediaries that charge fees. $200 sent across nations takes 2-7 days and costs 6.94% globally. Fees, intermediaries, and banks limit remittances by $48B. Blockchain streamlines payments and remittances, cutting settlement times and costs. It allows:

*   • Quick and safe retail payments.
*   • Secure settlement for wholesale and securities transactions.
*   • Fast international payments.
*   • Settlement in gross terms between the Central Bank and commercial and independent banks in real time.
*   • Automated and authenticate KYC/AML data and transaction history to reduce fraud and facilitate real-time authentication.
*   • Digitized regulatory auditing.
*   • It enables multiple payment types; Tokenized fiat, stablecoin, crypto.

EVOLUTION OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY

Inception Stage

| Figure 3. Inception stage |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch007.f03](i/ch007.f03.png) |
| --- |
| *Source: [https://www.naukri.com/learning/articles/evolution-of-blockchain-technology](https://www.naukri.com/learning/articles/evolution-of-blockchain-technology)* |

1991

The development of Blockchain systems could be traced back to 1991. When computer scientists Stuart Haber and W. Scott Stornetta needed a simple way to store copies of their digital files, they came up with the idea of cloud storage. In the future, they hope to strengthen the integrity of the timestamps on those files. An article outlining the concept of using a chain to encrypt blocks for the purpose of preserving data from the past was released a short time later.

1992

In 1992, they introduced the idea of Merkle trees into the mix. It is beneficial to compile a larger number of documents into a single block. As a result, the effectiveness of the model is improved. In 1993, a method known as Proof-of-Work (PoW) was suggested as a means of preventing spam and other forms of network abuse.

2004

RPoW was created in 2004 by computer scientist and cryptography specialist Hal Finney (Reusable Proof of Work). Using a proof-of-work (PoW) token's unchangeable (or non-fungible) hash cash as input, the system generates a tradable RSA-signed token. Most importantly, the protocol eliminated the occurrence of double expenditure. Because of this, it is imperative that RSA tokens continue to have their ownership details stored on the reliable server. In a distributed system where, other nodes can check and maintain document integration.

2008

Then, in late 2008, a whitepaper by the nameless Satoshi Nakamoto titled “Bitcoin: Peer-to-Peer E-cash system” emerged. He devised a Proof-of-Work (PoW) technique for hash currency using only software. As opposed to RPoW, which relies on in-built features of computers. In addition, Bitcoin presents the idea of “currency mining.” It also guarantees the nodes in a distributed network can verify each other's transactions.

2009

Individuals called “miners” use the PoW algorithm to earn Bitcoins as a reward. Even more impressively, it has been verified by the other decentralized nodes in the chain network.

Bitcoin's groundwork solidified on January 3, 2009. Satoshi Nakamoto received 50 bitcoins for mining the first block. Furthermore, the first block mined is now known as the “Genesis block of Bitcoin.” Satoshi sent 10 bitcoins to Hal Finny on January 12, 2009, marking the first-ever Bitcoin transaction. Trade in bitcoins for fiat currency has been possible since Oct. 31, 2009, when the Bitcoin exchange marketplace first opened to the public.

2012

Nakamoto also founded Bitcoin Talk Forum to share news. Bitcoin has expanded rapidly since 2009. Bitcoin surpassed $250 in 2012. Nakamoto limited the system to 21 million bitcoins. Mined bitcoins exceed 18 million. Depending on computation resources and mining speed, all bitcoins will be mined by 2140.

It's unclear who made this popular discovery. Satoshi Nakamoto departed and left their expertise to computer scientists and developers. Since then, global blockchain-based application inventions have been reported (https://www.naukri.com/learning/articles/evolution-of-blockchain-technology).

The Growth of Blockchain Despite Its Many Challenges as a Potential Future Technology (2013–2018)

| Figure 4. |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch007.f04](i/ch007.f04.png) |
| --- |
| *Source: [https://www.naukri.com/learning/articles/evolution-of-blockchain-technology](https://www.naukri.com/learning/articles/evolution-of-blockchain-technology)* |

2013

Bitcoin was the first to spearhead the charge in 2013 to challenge conventional economic thinking. Over $1 billion was worth of Bitcoins exchanged, or 11 million Bitcoins. Contrarily, Bitcoins are illegal in Thailand and China.

Meanwhile, confidence in Bitcoin was restored, and its price reached $1,164. Later, Vitalik Buterin, co-founder of Bitcoin Magazine, expressed a need for a scripting language to construct Decentralized apps. In spite of the fact that he was met with opposition to his proposal. He started constructing a new blockchain-powered distributed computing platform called Ethereum.

2014

Vitalik Buterin launched the Ethereum Foundation in 2014. Ethereum has evolved into much more than just a money or a decentralized network.

One of the most widespread uses of Blockchain is the idea of “Smart contracts,” which first appeared in 2015. An FYI, smart contracts are self-executing program built on the Ethereum blockchain. It goes into effect when certain criteria are satisfied. Many programmers have joined the experimentation with decentralized applications made possible by smart contract development (or Dapps).

Afterward, Hyperledger was released by the Linux Foundation. An open-source initiative with the goal of spurring the creation of decentralized software. Everything needed in terms of frameworks, tools, and libraries is included.

2016

By 2016, blockchain has more uses than cryptocurrency.

*   • Bank of England investigated blockchain for transaction settlements. Later, the ECB and the Bank of Japan established a cooperative blockchain research effort.
*   • IBM's blockchain-as-a-service (BaaS) concept is futuristic.
*   • R3 introduced Corda distributed technology.

Corda is a financial services-only permissioned distributed configurable platform. Here, only transaction parties can decide. Corda and Hyperledger lack native cryptocurrencies. They produce decentralized apps.

2017

In 2017, the blockchain was honored by the Harvard Business Review with the title of “Foundational Technology.”

The price of bitcoin almost doubled to $20,000.

*   • Bitcoin has been given the green light as a mainstream investment by US officials.
*   • Bitcoin has been given official status as a currency in Japan.
*   • A first step toward the formation of a digital commerce chain consortium was taken by seven European banks. to build a trading platform utilizing the blockchain's underlying technologies.
*   • By the end of the year, 15% of banks around the world had begun investigating blockchain technology.

2018

The beginning of 2018 was a difficult time for people who were interested in blockchain technology.

*   • The value of a single bitcoin fell to $3,800.
*   • The advertising of cryptocurrencies was prohibited by major tech companies such as Google, Facebook, and Twitter.
*   • Trading in cryptocurrencies was made illegal in South Korea. On the other hand, they disclosed significant investments in blockchain start-ups.
*   • The Blockchain Observatory and Forum has been established by the European Commission, which is encouraging news.
*   • Baidu has given the Blockchain-as-a-service (BaaS) platform some further support.

Blockchain's Growing Reception Around the World (2019–Present)

2019

In 2019, people all around the world began investing in bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies in the same way that they invest in traditional financial markets like the stock market and mutual funds.

| Figure 5. |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch007.f05](i/ch007.f05.png) |

*   • Since announcing their own blockchain-based cryptocurrency called Libra, Facebook has demonstrated an interest in the sector.
*   • The Amazon Blockchain service was later introduced by Amazon Web Services (AWS) on its cloud infrastructure.
*   • The People's Bank of China issued bonds using Blockchain technology, and China led the way by unveiling a state-backed digital currency.
*   • A football club in Gibraltar called Gibraltar United made history that year by accepting cryptocurrency as payment.
*   • Nearly 450 million transactions were completed using the blockchain by the end of the year.

Furthermore, several specialists have doubts about Libra's centralization claims. However, with Facebook now in the crypto game, many other tech behemoths and top VCs have been jolted into action.

2020

In 2020, the price of Bitcoin soared to $30,000, making it the most valuable cryptocurrency. The FinTech titan PayPal has begun to deal with cryptocurrencies.

According to Deloitte's Global Blockchain Survey Report 2020, over 40% of respondents implemented blockchain into production, while the remaining 55% envisioned it as their future strategic strategy. Ethereum revealed the development of Ethereum 2.0. To address the gas problems and other severe constraints.

Interest is growing in combining blockchain and AI to optimize company processes.

2021

Bitcoin value peaked at $64,829.14 in 2021 and dropped to $35,000. Elon Musk, the world's richest man, accepted Bitcoins for Tesla automobiles in March. Due to environmental concerns, he rescinded his comments.

El Salvador legalized Bitcoin first. After that, a massive wave of NFTs offered developers tremendous earnings. Unique, transferable digital assets including digital art, music, films, and collectibles are NFTs. Open Sea, a prominent NFT platform, gave artists and traders a stage. Blockchain, AR/VR, and web3 created Metaverse. Virtual world construction was inspired by the COVID-19 pandemic.

2022

NFTs and Metaverse gained popularity in 2022. That increased trading options and earnings. Nike, Adidas, Walmart, Puma, Gucci, Ferrari, and Disney used metaverse to market their avatar NFT designs.

India taxed digital asset transactions at 30%. It's not legal in the country. Other blockchain use cases are being tested and released. Smart contracts for satellite communications in space, Blockchain in supply chain, entertainment, healthcare, transportation, real estate, automation, and many other industries. Due to Russia-Ukraine war tensions, the Bitcoin community helped injured Ukrainian soldiers and civilians.

Although the evolution of blockchain technology started from 1991, it has got the contribution of various other concepts and technologies to become today’s technology. Likewise, the concept of ‘Merkle Trees’ which helps in collection of large numbers of documents in a single block. A ‘Proof of Work’ mechanism helps against spam and other network related abuses. In 2004 Hal Finney a computer scientist developed RPoW (Reusable Proof of Work) which is very useful for transferable RSA signed token, a non exchangeable token which solves the problem of double spending.

In 2008 the biggest step of blockchain technology evolution was introduced in the form of “Bitcoin: Peer to Peer E-Cash System” a white paper given by anonymous Satoshi Nakamoto. This system has brought the concept of mining coins which has given rise to debate of use of blockchain technology all over the world.

Till 2013 the various economies of the world started putting a ban on cryptocurrency transactions because somewhere it was against the rule that any individual miner can develop the coin without the control of the central bank of that economy. It has started a debate on parallel economy, black money and valuation of money like so many traditional economic concepts which is not tolerated by any country’s government.

NEW TRUST OF PARADIGM

After 2016 the long debate of almost ten years some people of society understand the use of blockchain technology could be used for advanced technological transactions. People started showing faith in block chain technology and its wide applications, followings are the list of such acknowledgment:

*   1\. The Bank of England, a central bank of the United Kingdom, is working on the use of blockchain technology in the real time gross settlement system of banks which could reduce the settlement time of transactions as well as it will give the accessibility to central bank to monitor all payment system transactions without relying on bank providing the reports. This will lead to speedy settlement of transactions as well the data redundancy or any mismanagement could not be hidden.
*   2\. The European central bank is also working on digital currency or digital euro which would not be a replacement of cash, but it will provide one more option of payment to users. Seven European banks formed a digital trade chain consortium to create a trading platform based on blockchain technology.
*   3\. Japan is first among the few countries which adopts blockchain technology. In 2017 the Japanese government passed a law perceiving bitcoin as a legal currency. The Japanese Financial Services Agency is accepting digital currency as an actual asset.
*   4\. United states based companies like IBM started it’s cloud based solution into Blockchain-as-a-Service (Baas) offering, R3 announced new distributed technology called Corda, Facebook announced their cryptocurrency named libra, Amazon web services announced amazon blockchain services on its cloud platform and the other multinational companies started showing their interest in blockchain technology.
*   5\. China initially declared a restriction on crypto mining but later on started working on usage of distributed networks with the introduction of blockchain industrial framework. China has developed its own smart contract programs like TRON, NEO, VeChain, and Qtum.
*   6\. The minuscule Swiss town named Zug is known as the home of the famous Ethereum network. Switzerland’s this small town has over 450 blockchain based businesses. The parliament of Switzerland recommended bitcoin as fresh money. They are soon going to bring amicable guidelines for use of blockchain technology in the financial sector.
*   7\. In 2018 South African Reserve Bank (SARB) used Quorum to start a blockchain based project “Khoka” with the help of 7 different business banks of their country. These banks created an interbank framework which handles the settlement of all transactions on a real time basis with complete transparency and secrecy. The success of this project backed them to start new phase of 2 “Khoka” in which member bank will buy debt instruments with a wToken (wholesale digital settlement token)
*   8\. The central bank of Bahamas developed a CBDC named “Sand Dollar” with intent to create a secure and transparent financial framework. In October 2020 it became the first central bank digital currency that accomplished an official take off.
*   9\. The Chilean government's energy agency is among the first to implement blockchain technology for use in the energy sector. To promote transparency and accountability, the government is utilizing Ethereum blockchain to log data and monitor financial transactions. The findings from this study will be implemented in several fields around the country.
*   10\. One of the populous states of Australia i.e. Queensland allowed crypto payment in the tourism sector to boost the tourism industry in this state. Tourism is the biggest economic booster for this state.

The blockchain market is growing very fast and each country of the world is trying to find out the useful application of blockchain technology in their economy. There are so many instances which prove that every country in the world has been finding a way to control this advanced technology.

INDIA AND BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY

In recent years, blockchain has gained significant popularity in India. This technology has been tested in a variety of use cases by numerous banks and corporate giants. Blockchain has to be investigated across businesses and in a variety of use cases as it is still in its infancy. But there is more to the Indian blockchain chapter than first appears. Continuous blockchain adoption requires a large human capital base that is knowledgeable, capable, and eager to participate in this new paradigm shift. There are several chances as well as difficulties for India to utilize blockchain technology.

One of the factors encouraging blockchain adoption in India is digitalization. Over the course of the last ten years, India has successfully developed the fundamental digital infrastructure required to support private sector applications like Aadhaar, UPI, e-Sign, and Digilocker, as well as digitally enabled tax governance networks like GSTN or digitally enabled health coverage like Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (PM-JAY). These currently offer a strong basis on which we may base our exploration of using this technology across other sectors.

There are a wide variety of practical uses for blockchain technology. It could introduce a novel strategy for more efficiently resolving corporate problems. Many businesses already employ the strategy, and that adoption is only expected to grow in light of India's future goals in the information technology industry. Not only does India have international assistance for its technical endeavours, but so do many other countries. Rapid development in the country's technology industry has led to a tripling of foreign direct investment (FDI) in computer software and hardware during 2020-21, as reported by the Department for the Promotion of Industry and Internal Trade. The sector is expected to bring in $7.67 billion in FDI in 2019–20, up from $6.41 billion in 2018–19. Computer hardware and software accounted for roughly 43% of the market.

India has witnessed 638 million USD business during 2021 by its blockchain industry.

Opportunities and Threats

Opportunities

*   • India has adopted a progressive attitude toward digitalization in the wake of demonetization and is cognizant of the advantages it offers.
*   • The blockchain projects of the Indian government are currently in use and are a showcase for numerous proof-of-concept (POC) that have been shown in the fields of banking, insurance, and registry.
*   • Several Indian states are considering aggressively utilizing blockchain technology for security and digitized property records.
*   • With regard to implementing transparent and secure voting procedures, a significant opportunity is arising in the voting industry.
*   • In order to investigate and monitor the development of new blockchain technologies, RBI established a unit in 2018. This study's main objective was to look into the feasibility of developing a decentralized, cashless banking system.

Challenges

Many Indian and international industries are interested in blockchain technology. India recognizes blockchain's governance uses. Though blockchain has obvious advantages, there are still challenges before we can aspire for big scale adoption of it by the industry. The following are well known:

*   • As more users join, the network slows down, increasing transaction fees. This is the biggest challenge.
*   • The second biggest issue is lack of standardization, which is necessary for global technological adoption. All networks employing the technology must speak the same language to communicate and conduct transactions. All new technologies suffer from this at the beginning till the standards progressively built up from experience.
*   • Blockchain privacy and security are next. The Blockchain's most appealing feature is transparency, yet its open access platform puts users' privacy at risk. Lack of regulation can lead to even bigger issues. Scams and market manipulation might destabilise the world economy. Due of global opposition, some countries have outlawed bitcoin currency.

Since blockchain is still in its infant stages, it is too soon to tell whether or not it will become a technology that is utilized by a significant number of people. The lack of regulation and compliance is the primary obstacle standing in the way of the widespread use of blockchain technology. The incorporation of blockchain technology into everyday life is a tough endeavour since millions of people still do not understand the concepts of the internet and digitalization.

WAY FORWARD SECTOR WISE

Technology is the way of the future, and as time goes on, society as a whole will see a growth in the usage of a wide variety of technology in our day-to-day lives on a scale that has never been seen before. In point of fact, in our day and age, the application of numerous technologies such as Artificial Intelligence (AI), Machine Learning (ML), Robotics, etc. is becoming an increasingly common practice. In terms of technology, if there is one particular technology that has been quite the craze as of late, then it has to be the blockchain technology. This particular technology has become quite the phenomenon in recent times. The blockchain technology was first developed in 2009 by Satoshi Nakamoto to function as the public ledger for the Bitcoin network. Since then, however, it has found various applications across the board in every industry in every region of the world.

One country, India, has already implemented and is making plans to implement blockchain technology across a variety of sectors. Let us have a look at a few of the areas in which the Indian government has already begun making plans to implement blockchain technology.

The Tea Board of India

The Tea Board of India intends to use blockchain technology to improve supply chain traceability. Due to adulteration, the quality of tea being produced has declined, according to the Indian Tea Board. Maintaining records at each stage of the supply chain will be made easier with the implementation of blockchain technology. This would improve accountability and make it easier for the authorities to identify and address problem areas (Business Today, 2022).

E-Voting

Three students from Malla Reddy Engineering College for Women have developed a novel idea that highlights how blockchain technology may be used to make the voting process in our country safer. The importance of applying the concept in practical settings was made clear by the results of a pilot project carried out by these students in several gated communities and housing complexes.

The Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI)

has issued a directive requiring all depositories to embrace blockchain technology in order to maintain accurate records. As a consequence of this, the process of keeping records, as well as the process of monitoring the production of securities and the covenants of non-convertible securities, will become more open and accessible to the public.

The Telecom Industry

The Telecom Regulatory Authority of India (TRAI) has mandated that all telecom companies launch blockchain initiatives immediately. The implementation of blockchain technology in the telecommunications sector will aid in the reduction of unwanted calls. Government agencies can use blockchain technology to locate unlicensed telemarketing companies.

In addition to these areas where blockchain technology has been utilized or is being adopted, the Indian government plans to incorporate it into other sectors. The Indian government wants to create a national blockchain framework to alter 44 sectors, including education, pharma, farming, energy, e-governance, and more. The government will help India incorporate and implement blockchain technology faster and easier. Blockchain technology will revolutionize India soon. It will improve most government services by fixing their major issues. By 2050, blockchain technology is expected to generate $176 billion. Thus, blockchain technology will elevate India to a new level and boost its ranking among nations.

CONCLUSION

Most of the Indian industries are growing at a swift rate, as a result the economy of the country is moving at a similar pace. The adoption of IT and AI has brought a muscular change in the industry. The use of manpower in few industries is being lowered. However, the technological advancement and development of startups is leading India to a new phase.

The evolution of blockchain technology has an enormous impact on industries like Banking, Insurance, Investment etc. Digitalization is one of several variables that are influencing blockchain adoption in India. India has successfully built the foundational digital infrastructure needed to support private sector applications like Aadhaar, UPI, e-Sign, and Digilocker, as well as digitally enabled tax governance networks like GST etc.

Though there are a lot of advantages, blockchain is also not free from challenges. The biggest obstacle to the successful implementation of the Blockchain is a lack of regulation and compliance. Making blockchain a part of everyday life is a difficult endeavor given that millions of people still do not understand the concepts of the internet and digitization.

There are several sectors where Govt. pf India is planning to implement blockchain technology. The few notable sectors are telecom, e-voting, agriculture, depository, etc. The way is not that easy to cover but once it is implemented so many problems like fraudulent, misconduct, dependency on human eyes etc. can be minimized. The current state of blockchain technology is comparable to that of the internet in the early 1990s. While we have seen how the 'Internet of Information' has changed our society over the course of the past two decades, we are now entering a phase where Blockchain may do the same by ushering in a new paradigm comprising the 'Internet of Trust' and the 'Internet of Value'. While we have seen how the 'Internet of Information' has changed our society over the course of the past two decades, we are now entering a phase where blockchain may do the same.

It is possible that the financial services industry will be one of the first to feel the effects of the widespread adoption of Blockchain and the Distributed Ledger Technologies that are linked with it. The magnitude of this influence will be directly proportional to the speed with which various industry participants will be able to profit on this technology and the level of support it will receive from various larger stakeholders.
