The innovation of blockchain is rather recent. A blockchain is “a continually increasing rundown of documents, called squares which are joined and anchored via encryption.” They will differentiate a delegate evaluation of flow topics in blockchain and investigate as well as analyses the potential repercussions of our ideas. Even though blockchain is probably not well known, it is rapidly emerging as a medium, and it is a topic that is being discussed in the media with a great deal of intensity right now ([Gökalp et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r16)).

Components of Blockchain Technology

Block chain architecture has the following main components:

A Distributed Ledger

A distributed ledger is a shared database in a blockchain network that contains the transactions. An example of this would be a file that everyone on the team is able to modify and is shared with everyone else. Any user who has been granted editing privileges in a shared text editor has the ability to delete the whole file. On the other hand, systems based on distributed ledgers contain stringent constraints regarding who may edit and how they can edit. Once an entry has been recorded, it cannot be deleted under any circumstances ([Korpela et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r24)).

Smart Contracts

Rather than relying on the assistance of a third party to handle their business contracts, companies are turning to something called “smart contracts.” They are computer programmes that are saved on the blockchain and will execute themselves automatically when certain criteria are satisfied. They perform if-then tests to ensure that business transactions may be carried out with full confidence. For instance, a logistics business may use a smart contract that enables payment to be made immediately after the shipment of goods has been received at the port ([Cole et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r9)).

Cases and Applications of Blockchain Use

A blockchain is a digital database of transactions that is copied and dispersed across several computers. Each transaction is recorded in a new block that is added to the distributed ledger and visible to all users instantly. As a whole, the chain of transactions relies on each individual connection ([Zhang et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r53)).

| Figure 2. Applications of blockchain |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.f02](i/ch010.f02.png) |
| --- |

From the Figure 2, Blockchain technology may have significant implications for the online advertising market. Customers might be more precisely targeted with personalized messages and offers if a decentralized database of customer information is created using blockchain technology.

Cryptocurrencies

According [Zhang et al. (2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r53)), cryptocurrencies are digital currencies with the ownership records of individual coins maintained in a public, distributed ledger. Because of its 'decentralized control,' cryptocurrencies are not under the authority of any single entity. Launched in 2008, Bitcoin eliminated the need for trusted intermediaries like banks by facilitating peer-to-peer transactions between users. There have been almost 4, 000 unique cryptocurrencies introduced since then. Bitcoin, Ethereum, Dogecoin, Fantom, and many others are all instances of cryptocurrencies.

Legal Documents

Therefore, blockchains excel at preserving data integrity over long periods of time. So, it's not just for odometers; it may also serve as a notary or maintain track of intellectual property. To validate and verify signatures on legal papers, they need the services of a notary (such as the federal government). To counter this, they may employ blockchain technology instead. Stampd.io is one such service that facilitates adding these papers to the Bitcoin or Ethereum Blockchain ([Pilkington, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r37)).

Digital Voting

Digital voting is another exciting use of this technology. Either paper ballots or EVMs (electronic voting machines), which are specialized computers with proprietary voting software, are being used for voting. Paper ballots have high production and disposal costs, whereas computerized voting systems have security concerns. Countries have been moving back to paper ballots in recent years out of concern that hackers may tamper with or influence the results of an electronic vote. They have had politicians here in our country quarrel over alleged “EVM hack” incidents ([Pilkington, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r37)).

Smart Contracts

So far, they have looked at ways blockchains can be used to keep track of information and verify its integrity. But blockchains are even more powerful when they use them as smart contracts as one of its applications. These contracts live on the blockchain and can perform actions when various conditions are met. Insurance companies could use smart contracts to validate claims and keep a record of all the people who are buying insurance and paying their premiums on time so as to continue the terms of the policy. Or they could allow us to only pay for car insurance when they are driving ([Pilkington, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r37)).

Advantages and Disadvantages of Blockchain

Like all forms of technology, blockchain has several advantages and disadvantages to consider:

Advantages

The high degree of security that can be provided by blockchains is one of the most significant benefits they provide. Because of this, blockchains are able to protect and secure sensitive data that is involved in online transactions. Anyone interested in transactions that are both quick and convenient will find that blockchain technology provides this as well. In point of fact, the total time required is only a few minutes, although the completion of other transaction methods might take several days ([Pilkington, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r37)).

Disadvantages

It has been stated that there have been issues with private keys, which is problematic given that blockchain and cryptography both necessitate the usage of public and private keys. Another drawback is the constraints on scalability, as there is a cap on the amount of transactions that may take place on each node. As a result of this, it is possible that completing many transactions and other chores will take several hours. Another significant drawback of blockchain technology is that after information has been recorded, it can be challenging to make changes to it or add new information ([Pilkington, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r37)).

DIGITAL TECHNOLOGY AND SERVICES

Digital Technology and Services Helps OEMs integrate and automate design processes for smooth data sharing throughout product development lifecycles. Not only does this make engineering more digital, but it also makes it possible to optimize designs. Additionally, collaborates with original equipment manufacturers (OEMs) to provide digital solutions that span ([Attaran, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r4)):

*   • The boosting of output and productivity and the maximization of resources.
*   • Predictability of field services may be improved by the use of analytical models and digital twins.
*   • Information is sent back to engineering teams so they may improve product performance, increase design accuracy, and reduce the amount of physical validation needed.

Companies who can provide their clients with the quickest and most immersive digital solutions will emerge as the industry's future leaders. Delivering connected experiences across mobile, web, and the internet of things is one of the ways that our team at assists customers in meeting the specific business demands and problems they face ([Attaran, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r4)).

Digital Strategy

Analyzing the present state of the organization, outlining a specific road map, and developing a plan to accomplish your digital objectives are all important steps.

Digital Experience Design

The process of designing user experiences that span several channels in order to offer a perspective that is interactive and consistent across mobile devices, websites, and applications.

Digital Development and Process Engineering

Development of digital solutions covering the whole lifecycle, administration of business processes, and linkages to improve and alter your operational effectiveness.

Digital Analytics

Actionable insights in real time to assess an organization’s performance across multiple digital channels.

Blockchain-as-a-Service

The term “blockchain as a service,” or “BaaS,” refers to the practise of having a third party (usually a cloud provider) construct and manage a blockchain network on behalf of blockchain application developers. The abbreviation is often used to describe this method. These outside agencies are a relatively new development in the rapidly expanding blockchain industry. They're a relatively new addition. The most well-known use of blockchain technology so far has been in the exchange of virtual currencies, but the technology is now being put to use in the protection of all types of financial transactions. In the world of finance, blockchain technology is most often used to facilitate the exchange of digital currencies ([Mettler, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r33)).

| Figure 3. Blockchain-as-a-service |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.f03](i/ch010.f03.png) |
| --- |

*   • From Figure 3, Blockchain as a service, abbreviated as BaaS, is cloud-based infrastructure and administration provided by a third party to businesses who are developing and running blockchain applications.
*   • The back-end operations of a blockchain-based app or platform can be managed by BaaS, which performs this job in a manner analogous to that of a web host.

For a charge, businesses may use the Blockchain as a Service (BaaS) model, in which an external service provider is contracted to set up the necessary block chain technology and infrastructure. The provider will handle all the client's complex back-end operations even after the account has been set up. In many cases, the BaaS provider will handle customer support tasks including bandwidth management, resource allocation, hosting, and data security ([Mettler, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r33)).

CHALLENGES OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY

The efforts that have been put towards applying blockchain technology have brought to light a number of different technological problems. Among these is the requirement for more robust security technology that permits its use in a risk-free manner as well as a more rapid processing speed ([Pilkington, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r37)).

More Robust Security Technology

The technology known as blockchain is distinguished by the fact that it ensures security by requiring the verification of transaction information by all participants in the network. This serves to prevent illicit activities. This makes it possible for a person who is not part of the blockchain to access a transaction that is being processed by the blockchain. Transaction information is not accessible to an individual in certain circumstances and for some applications except if the individual is actively involved in the transaction. The process that is implemented in order to ensure that a transaction is conducted in complete secrecy by utilizing a blockchain presents a considerable obstacle ([Pilkington, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r37)).

Faster Processing Speed

Processing time for a blockchain transaction is longer than that of a traditional transaction since the transaction needs to be validated by every participant in the network. Therefore, increasing processing speed might be a challenge for applications that involve a large number of transactions ([Pilkington, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r37)).

Objectives

*   • The idea behind blockchain is to allow for immutable digital storage and distribution of information.
*   • Blockchain transactions are permanent and cannot be altered or deleted, they form the foundation of unchangeable ledgers.
*   • Consumers' activities and contacts with businesses might be tracked and rewarded in a decentralized loyalty programmer built on the blockchain.
*   • One potential use of blockchain technology is the creation of a decentralized advertising market, which would allow businesses to send advertisements to consumers with more precision.

LITERATURE REVIEW

Impacts on Global Economy and Economic Benefits of Blockchain

[Laroiya et al. (2020)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r25) argue that one description of blockchain technology is a “chain of blocks” containing data, often known as “digital ledgers.” These books are linked chronologically and stored in a system of databases rather than a single centralised location. The chain may get upgrades in the form of blocks, but it can never be deleted. The chain will also keep track of and verify any changes. The data stored in the blocks is encrypted using a variety of methods to ensure that only authorized users may access the data. While there exist private blockchains, the term “decentralized” is most often associated with public blockchains. The four main use cases for blockchain technology are monetary transactions, property registration, legal contracts, and identity verification. Cryptocurrencies are a kind of digital currency that uses public-key cryptography for security and counterfeit-proofing transactions. Blockchain-based applications are commonly used in tandem with cryptocurrencies. Both technologists and economists may appreciate the blockchain for its revolutionary potential. Blockchain technology, also known as a distributed ledger, may be utilised as a transactional system for services linked with the “sharing economy” since it naturally addresses the challenge of trustworthy recording of large-scale peer-to-peer activities. Financial innovation blockchain solves issues that come when dealing with a decentralised system where not all participants can be relied upon to accurately record and report on transactions. With the use of blockchain technology, a new global economy has emerged that is founded on instantaneous trust and value. The foundation of this economic system is a set of complex computer algorithms and a mutual agreement. Through the utilization of digitalized money streams and payment channels, a just-in-time economy for monetary transactions may become feasible. The 30-, 60-, and 90-day terms now employed in supply chain financing may be shortened using this method, as could the issue of uncollectable debt. The phrase “Blockchain economy” refers to the shift away from national hard currencies and legacy, antiquated ledger systems and toward digital cryptocurrencies and ledgers. Financial management tools in a blockchain economy are often technology like bitcoin and blockchain rather than traditional software application programmes that handle previously existent national currencies. Further, a blockchain economy is a potential future setting in which cryptocurrencies would replace traditional monetary systems worldwide. Distributed ledger technology (DLT), also known as blockchain, is projected to boost economic growth in a research titled The Future of the Blockchain Market. Revenues might reach $120 billion worldwide between 2018 and 2024. Due to its many benefits, the research found that blockchain technology's broad adoption was very plausible. They estimated that by 2024, blockchain technology might bring in between $87 billion and $120 billion, depending on which industries embrace it and how quickly it spreads ([Laroiya et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r25)).

[Tönnissen and Teuteberg (2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r48)) argue that blockchain technology holds great promise for the insurance business as well as the financial sector, especially in the area of financial market infrastructure. At the heart of the buzz around blockchain technology is the revolutionary decentralized transactional architecture it enables. This model's foundations come straight from Satoshi's (2008) white paper, where the concepts of decentralized, peer-to-peer communication are laid forth. Blockchain technology is expected to have far-reaching consequences for the global economy and online trade because of the decentralized transactional paradigm it enables. Because it makes it easier to adopt such a framework, this is the case. Moreover, the dominant transactional paradigm is still a centralized one, despite the fact that the percentage of business transacted online has steadily increased since the introduction of the Internet, or more especially over the course of the previous 10 years ([Lee, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r26)).

NEW BUSINESS APPLICATIONS FOR THE BLOCKCHAIN

According to [Lindman et al. (2017)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r27), it wasn't until lately that anybody outside of the financial industry showed any real interest in blockchain. Blockchain's ability to verify digital information and generate smart contracts is significantly increasing the technology's worldwide impact. New programmable blockchain platforms with decentralized computing capability are emerging, increasing the applicability of this technology and opening up new avenues for its use. One such platform is Ethereum. Blockchain technology may make many different services more affordable and competitive ([Lindman et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r27)). Blockchain technology is being considered by a variety of sectors, including the food and beverage, automotive, electronics, aerospace, and military industries, for use in protecting batch and lot data, as well as quality, safety, and traceability information throughout supply chains. Businesses like IBM and Microsoft are offering blockchain-based solutions to a wide range of markets. People commonly use the term “blockchain” interchangeably with “bitcoin,” the digital currency. An optimistic outlook for the blockchain economy may be laid if bitcoin were to be adopted globally as a replacement for current monetary systems. The future of exchanges of value will be profoundly affected by this (Rouse, 2018). There are a few ways in which blockchain's digital democratisation might boost economies in the developing world (Saberthey et al., 2019):

*   • Resource sharing economy with no authoritative hierarchy since users of distributed ledgers may transact financial transactions directly with one another, this opens the door to a decentralised, collaborative economy. OpenBazaar is an online marketplace that uses blockchain technology to facilitate transactions between users, much as eBay does. When making a purchase from an OpenBazaar vendor, the customer will not be charged any additional costs ([Mamun, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r32)).
*   • Micro-grids are used to describe communities of individuals that live in close proximity to one another and who are able to generate, consume, and trade electricity amongst themselves. Blockchain technology enables the purchase and sale of surplus energy produced by solar panels via transactive energy marketplaces. New York City-based company LO3 Energy is developing software for a decentralised power grid that uses renewable energy sources to meet the evolving demands of businesses and consumers. In 2016, the firm made it feasible for Brooklyn, New York locals to participate in small-scale, environmentally responsible energy trading with their neighbors, some of whom did not have solar power systems of their own and others of whom generated extra solar power (Mainellthey & Smith, 2015).
*   • The use of social media sites like Facebook has led to the widespread free exchange of user information. Historically, the people whose work generates personal data have been left out of the transactional process of buying and selling such data. The technology underpinning blockchain allows people to own and benefit from the data created by their online actions. Protecting the privacy of users is crucial to developing a personal data market (Madan et al., 2021). A blockchain network guarantees there will always be a smart contract between a data buyer and a data seller, regulating the usage of consumers' personal data. This agreement may be used to authenticate and approve financial dealings. Some businesses, including Wibson and Opiria, have declared the introduction of blockchain-based, decentralized personal data marketplaces. Founded in 2017, Opiria is a European company based in London. The internal money for incentives may be anything from bitcoins or tokens to personally identifiable information sold directly by customers to merchants. Customers are paid money in return for their personal information if and only if the transaction is completed. Opiria's founders claim that $250 billion has been sold in personal data on a worldwide scale (Raja Sanththey & Muthuswamy, 2022).
*   • Transactions between machines, often known as M2M: M2M is an abbreviation for “machine to machine,” which refers to a technology that enables networked devices to remotely exchange information and carry out tasks without the intervention of a human. Because M2M is capable of real-time communication, it enables manufacturers to monitor warehouse operations and remotely track and manage their supply chains, allowing them to do so from any place. The terms “warehouse management,” “traffic control,” “logistical services,” “supply chain management,” “fleet management,” and “telemedicine” are frequently used in conjunction with “M2M communication”. Another growing usage for blockchain technology is machine-to-machine transactions, or M2M transactions. It is anticipated that future developments in artificial intelligence (AI) would make it possible for machines to rent themselves out, pay for their own maintenance, buy their own replacement components, and store their own transactional records utilizing blockchain technology ([Yeoh, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r52)).

THEORETICAL CONCERNS

[Al-Saqaf and Seidler (2017)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r3) argue that there have been several technical and legal obstacles to executing online financial transactions that include many parties, but this has only become a big problem in the past few years. The emergence of blockchain technology has spawned novel lines of inquiry into distributed transactional systems, including methods of making and accepting digital currency payments online and the complexities of asset allocation and administration on the blockchain. Records of public and legal proceedings, such as marriage and birth certificates, court notices, registration and sale deeds, etc. Smart contracts, the acquisition and sale of real estate, and the trading of numerous other physical assets (such as gold, diamonds, and automobiles) are examples of further tools. These difficult problems are driving the boundaries of IS research in ways that were unthinkable just a few short years ago ([Sharma et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r43)).

[Durach et al. (2021)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r10) argue that the openness of a digital transaction and its supposed anonymity is a major theoretical topic in blockchain IS research. A proper framework for addressing this connection must give a means to guarantee the advantages of both without compromising the veracity of either. An important area of study is the theoretical possibility of solving this problem using public-private and its general form, multisig encryption techniques. Open source software, systems, and computational platforms are already the focus of a great deal of IS study. Theorists in the field of information systems are very interested in exploring how the transparency of digital currencies and the blockchain technology that powers them might be applied to the study of openness in the field. The underlying governance structure of the open-source software development community responsible for implementing and improving blockchain technology is a related issue with as-yet-unclear ramifications. Generally speaking, this is referred to as a “governance crisis ([Gohil & Thakker, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r15)).”

[Trautman (2016)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r49) argue that a blockchain is a public ledger, they may rest certain that the information it contains has not been tampered with. It's common knowledge that it's the underlying mechanism behind Bitcoin, the ubiquitous cryptocurrency. Bitcoin was the first digital money to use blockchain technology. Bitcoin is the most well-known use of Blockchain technology at the present. The Blockchain, a distributed public database that serves as the backbone of Bitcoin's decentralized digital currency payment system, is where all Bitcoin transactions are recorded and made available to the public. The decentralized nature of the Bitcoin network allows for instant international transactions. Bitcoin is unique because its price is independent of any one bank, government, or other centralized authority. The Bitcoin network's monetary volume and user base have been steadily rising. There is constant trading in the foreign exchange market because major currencies like the Korean won, the Euro, and the US dollar are traded so often. This is why Bitcoin has surpassed all expectations and became the most well-known cryptocurrency built on the Blockchain ([Trautman, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r49)).

According to [Prokofieva and Miah (2019)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r38), public key infrastructure is crucial to Bitcoin's operation (PKI). Each user has their own set of private and public keys. Addresses for Bitcoin wallets are produced with the help of public keys, while authentication is handled with the help of the private key. Each Bitcoin exchange involves the sender's public key, the recipient's public key(s), and the value being transferred. After around 10 minutes, the transaction will be put in a block. After finishing a new paragraph, it is added to the existing text. A replica of each block and the associated transaction data is stored on the local discs of each user, or node. Each node in the Bitcoin network stores the whole blockchain, so when a new transaction is made, it can be checked against the prior blocks. Nodes are compensated for their time and effort in ensuring the integrity of financial transactions. “Mining” refers to this process, and Proof-of-Work is used to verify it. This is a fundamental concept for realizing Blockchain's potential. The process of “mining” is how digital currencies are created. After all transactions have been validated, the network nodes will come to an agreement. The most modern block is the last in a sequence that spans centuries. The Bitcoin network use blockchain, a distributed database made up of blocks, to record and verify transactions (Centobellthey et al., 2022).

According to [Sun et al. (2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r44)), the term “blockchain” is used to describe Bitcoin's distributed ledger technology. Its principal function is to make it easier for its users to do financial transactions using Bitcoin, such as buying and selling. In the absence of a supervisory body, this strategy has the potential to provide legitimacy to the public record of all Bitcoin transactions ever done. One of the benefits of Blockchain is that after the data have been authenticated by all nodes, the public ledger cannot be updated or withdrawn. These protections are largely responsible for Blockchain's meteoric rise to prominence. Future applications of blockchain technology are likely to increase significantly. The broad use of cloud computing, for instance, would make possible the generation and execution of digital contracts, as well as the exchange and transaction of data amongst peers in the system. Since the primary selling point of Blockchain is the security it provides for stored data, it has found significant application in a variety of settings ([Mackey et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r28)).

TAXONOMY OF BLOCKCHAIN

There are now three distinct types of blockchain systems, however they may be summed up as follows: public blockchains, private blockchains, and consortium blockchains. They evaluate these three distinct blockchains side by side ([Abeyratne & Monfared, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r1)).

*   • Warning: Permission required reading. In a public blockchain, anyone may view the transactions, but in a private blockchain or a consortium blockchain, access to the ledger is restricted. To what extent the collected data is made available to the public is a decision that could be made by the consortium or the organization ([Mackey et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r28)).
*   • Effectiveness: Because there are so many nodes on the public blockchain network, propagating transactions and blocks takes a considerable amount of time. When considering the security of the network, limits on the public blockchain become even more stringent. This results in slow processing times and increased latency for transactions. Consortium blockchains and private blockchains may function more efficiently with fewer validators ([Berdik et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r6)).

Due to its universal accessibility, public blockchain has the potential to serve a large user base. There is a lot of activity within communities as well. Business consortium blockchain frameworks are now being developed by Hyperledger. Ethereum has also offered resources for constructing consortium blockchains. Private Blockchain is still being implemented by many businesses due to its efficiency and auditability ([Teufel et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r46)).

The Byzantine Generals (BG) Problem is a variant of the consensus problem in blockchain technology. In the BG problem, the commanders of a Byzantine army division are encircling the city. If only some of the generals launch an assault on the city, it will fail. The decision to assault requires consensus between the generals. But even among the generals, there could be spies. The traitor may inform separate generals of conflicting decisions. There is no one to put your faith in here. Finding a way to agree on anything is difficult in such a setting. Similarly, the decentralized nature of the blockchain network makes this an issue. In blockchain, there is no centralized authority making sure all of the nodes' ledgers are identical. There is zero trust required between nodes. Therefore, specific protocols are required to guarantee the consistency of ledgers across nodes ([Hileman & Rauchs, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r21)).

TAXONOMY OF SECURITY PROPERTIES FOR BLOCKCHAIN

Here, they develop a taxonomy of security features to evaluate the robustness of blockchain systems, with the CIA model serving as our guide (confidentiality, integrity, and availability). Our suggested qualities may be broken down into two categories: those that relate to the reliability of the consensus protocols employed in blockchain systems and those that relate to the platforms and technologies that underpin blockchain services ([Khezr et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r22)).

In a decentralized network without a central authority, all transactions are tracked and recorded using a secure distributed technology called a blockchain. Such a system should have both reliability (or consistency) and vitality. The first is related to the platform's consensus mechanism, while the second is established by the architecture of the platform. Thus, they get off by describing the three safety features that should be included in every blockchain consensus process (Zubaydthey et al., 2019).

APPLICATIONS OF BLOCKCHAIN

Many industries and occupations could be greatly affected by blockchain technology. Numerous people are familiar with and make use of blockchain-based cryptocurrency apps, but the technology's usefulness has since spread beyond the realm of cryptocurrencies. Many companies have recognized its value and are investigating it further. The financial sector isn't the only industry where blockchain is showing promise. Enhanced usability is predicted for future software as developers focus on creating user-friendly interfaces and new use cases. Businesses and sectors are motivated to experiment with and build blockchain-based applications by, among other things, the need to improve access to information, data integrity, and operational resilience ([Tönnissen & Teuteberg, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r48)).

FINANCIAL APPLICATIONS

Following the development of blockchain-based cryptocurrency systems, there are many possible applications in various financial fields. In the financial sector, transactions between individuals and institutions are typically handled via third-party intermediaries. Blockchain's built-in qualities and functionalities make it an ideal replacement for these trusted middlemen, especially when it comes to preventing duplicate financial transactions and registering and validating financial operations. The following are some examples of blockchain-enabled financial applications that bypass traditional intermediaries like banks and financial institutions ([Rijanto, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r40)).

Payments

For this reason, blockchain has being tested as a potential new method of doing financial transactions. Hundreds of trillions of dollars are transferred every day, but the world still uses an antiquated financial system that increases costs and makes transactions take longer than necessary. By eliminating the need for trusted third parties in the verification and settlement of financial transactions, public blockchain-based cryptocurrency systems like Bitcoin and Ethereum have made it possible for anybody in the world to send and receive payments instantly. The European Payments Council nearly unanimously predicted that by 2025, blockchain technology would completely revolutionize the payments business. Cryptocurrencies are not likely to totally replace fiat currencies any time soon. However, in terms of monetary exchange, the volume of cryptocurrency transactions, especially Bitcoin and Ethereum, has grown steadily over the past few years. Financial institutions such as banks have also begun exploring blockchain technology. The American multinational investment bank and financial services holding company JPMorgan Chase & Co. announced on October 16, 2017, that it would be launching the Interbank Information Network (IIN) in partnership with two international partners: Royal Bank of Canada and Australia and New Zealand Banking Group Limited. More than 75 financial institutions joined the IIN on September 25, 2018. In this network, numerous banks are using blockchain technology in a real-world setting for the first time anywhere in the world ([Zhang & Zhao, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r54)).

Financial Clearance and Settlements

By using blockchain, businesses and institutions can track, verify, and conduct financial settlements without involving a third-party clearinghouse. Adjusting financial commitments to authorize payments is one example of a clearing operation that can be made easier with the help of blockchain technology. Transactions may be settled instantly and tracked more efficiently than with current systems like SWIFT thanks to blockchain technology. One of the earliest users, the Royal Bank of Canada, has begun using Hyperledger, a blockchain-based platform, for its interbank settlements with the United States and Canada. Ripple and R3 are two examples of startups that are collaborating with conventional banks to enhance transaction quality via blockchain technology ([Tijan et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r47)).

Stock Trading

Typically, a single entity, such as an exchange market, oversees all stock trades. All trades and payments are meticulously recorded by this centralized administration. Fees go up and settlement times go up with this arrangement. In this regard, some blockchain-based solution have been created. For instance, Polymath5 is creating a platform and marketplace for trading digital securities through the use of blockchain technology. The launch of security tokens on the platform is being done in collaboration with Blocktrade, Corl, and Ethereum Capital. For another, consider Overstock's tZERO6 trading platform. In October of 2018, tZero successfully completed a private digital token offering that generated $134M. Trading of these tokens on tZero began at the beginning of 2019 ([Swan, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r45)).

Trade Finance

Loaded paper, increased errors, and a lengthy manner of processing transactions between counterparties are only some of the problems that have been linked with traditional trade finance. Blockchain provides many potential benefits for the trade finance industry, including the elimination of paperwork, the automation of processes and payments, the reduction of fraud, and the reduction of expenses, as well as improved cargo tracking and visibility for all parties involved. There have been numerous consortia established by businesses and financial institutions with the goal of bettering trade financing. One such blockchain initiative was established by IBM in partnership with the bank-led group R3. Twelve major financial institutions, including BBVA, Mizuho, and U.S. Bank, collaborated on an initiative to digitize paper letters of credit. The two major banking institutions in the world, Standard Chartered and HSBC, are collaborating to better trade finance through the use of blockchain technology. Wave, a Fintech startup based in Israel, has created a blockchain solution that can be used in financial transactions including letters of credit. With this platform, Barcelona's EuroFinance was able to offer a blockchain solution to businesses like Ornua and the Seychelles Trading Company. As a result of using Wave's platform, the company was able to optimize its supply chain, which in turn decreased transaction costs, eliminated errors in paperwork, and speed up the delivery of papers to clients throughout the world ([Kim & Shin, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r23)).

ACCOUNTING APPLICATIONS

The accounting industry has much to gain from blockchain technology. When it comes to verifying a company's financials, a self-auditing and irreversible record can have far-reaching effects, including significant reductions in the difficulty and complexity of audits. The accounting industry and the business ecosystem may both undergo significant shifts as a result of blockchain technology's innovative approach to recording, processing, validating, and storing financial transactions and data. Perhaps the accounting industry will look to blockchain as the next big technological advancement. Businesses can forego maintaining their own records based on transaction receipts and instead enter their transactions directly into a shared ledger, therefore establishing a system of interlocking and permanent accounting records. In addition, transactions with different business partners can be compared with minimal effort and without compromising individual privacy (Haddoutthey & Ech-Cherif El Kettani, 2019).

INSURANCE-RELATED APPLICATIONS

Sales, underwriting, customer onboarding, claims processing, payments, asset transfers, and reinsurance are just some of the sectors where the insurance industry is exhibiting an increasing interest in blockchain technology and its implementation. Transactions between customers, policyholders, and insurance providers can be facilitated by blockchain technology. In the insurance industry, blockchain technology can be used for several purposes, including underwriting, policy issuance, claim filing, and reinsurance assistance. In addition, blockchain can improve the insurance value chain since it can provide long-term strategic benefits including decreased operating costs because of streamlined processes, less counterparty risks, enhanced automation, and decentralized, secure transactions. Managing current policies digitally would drastically cut down on administrative expenditures. Smart contracts' capacity to readily manage “if-then” transactional connections enables automatic execution of the terms using digital processes that put into effect the agreed-upon insurance policies, thereby avoiding the issues of arguments and misinterpretations over policy terms. Travel insurance, crop insurance, property and casualty insurance, and health insurance are just some of the areas that stand to benefit from blockchain technology. Insurers, medical facilities, mortuaries, government agencies, and beneficiaries might all function as nodes in a blockchain-based multiparty share network. When it comes to insurance and claims, this approach can provide the disintermediation and speed essential to streamline the processes and eliminate fraud. It is possible that B3 they will create a proof-of-concept blockchain for property reinsurance policies. With the help of these 38 insurers and brokers, the business will be able to move the entire reinsurance contract process on the decentralized blockchain ([Ahmad et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r2)).

SUPPLY CHAIN MANAGEMENT APPLICATIONS

Some of the numerous advantages of blockchain technology include increased supply chain transparency and accountability. Blockchain-based supply chain management (SCM) processes are associated with increased success and improved quality. Distributed ledger technology makes it impossible to modify tracking data once it has been entered. The increased trust among suppliers is a direct result of blockchain's ability to track and verify the status of products and services at every step of the supply chain. Blockchain allows suppliers to conduct audits independently at any time, saving both time and money while improving efficiency. The quality of sent items may be monitored and maintained with the use of blockchain technology. The stakeholders in the supply chain may identify whether an item has been held for too long or is not where it should be by looking at the shipment and storage timestamps. These worries are especially important when working with perishable commodities, such as perishable food. This is just one example of how blockchain-based technologies may be put to use to ensure the genuineness and quality of products. Blockchain technology may find use in the logistics sector. When dealing with logistics, it's common to have to prioritise a number of different objectives at once ([Perera et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r36)).

Operations and sub-activities must be carried out in tandem in a wide variety of settings, including plants, storage facilities, transportation businesses, and governmental agencies. In order to effectively plan, schedule, coordinate, monitor, and validate logistics processes, high-level embedded services inside logistics management programmed are required. Thankfully, blockchain technology can effectively and securely handle these functions. When used to logistics transactions, blockchain technology has the potential to drastically cut down on processing times, administrative costs, and human error. In addition, smart contracts will enhance agreements and contracts between parties involved in a more timely and affordable way. Numerous blockchain-based supply chain and logistics management systems and apps have been created and implemented to take advantage of blockchain's benefits and significant impact on the SCM and logistics sectors. IBM developed a blockchain-based system to trace the provenance of agricultural goods. For a trial run in 2018, retailers and manufacturers including Walmart, The Kroger Co., Driscoll's, Dole, Golden State Foods, McCormick and Co., McLane Co., Nestle, Tyson Foods, and Unilever monitored millions of food items. Using blockchain technology, the Danish shipping corporation Maersk can track the location of each and every item at any given moment. And then there's Provenance, which piloted a seafood tracking programmer in Indonesia using RFID tags, blockchain, and mobile phones. The company has been able to effectively execute fish monitoring so far in 2016 ([Gürkaynak et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r17)).

ADVERTISING AND MEDIA APPLICATIONS

These days, no firm can function without some form of online advertising. Online advertising has several advantages, such as being measurable, interactive, profitable, trustworthy, and inexpensive. However, it also has certain drawbacks. For instance, users worry about data privacy and the ads they see, while publishers and advertisers fret over ad fraud, inefficiency, and the difficulty of measuring and estimating the return on investment. There are three primary challenges facing the world of digital marketing. To begin, there are a lot of middlemen in the advertising sector, which drives up prices for everyone involved. Advertisers and publishers alike are finding that the online advertising process is becoming increasingly inefficient and costly. There are occasions when publishers are unable to sell all of their stock because the price at which most of it is sold is too low. Additionally, advertising must fork up money to middlemen. Data privacy is a second user-related issue that needs to be addressed. Searches conducted by users are frequently recorded and afterwards used to serve advertising that are specifically tailored to the interests of the user conducting the search. Although some people are fine with this, many others see it as an invasion of privacy and are not supportive of it (Suman et al., 2021). Ad fraud is the third problem that has been identified ([Hashemthey Joo et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r20)). Using automated systems to view or interact with advertisements is one common practice that can skew the results of a campaign's evaluation. For every one of these issues, blockchain technology offers workable answers. When it comes to online advertising, blockchain allows for a direct link between parties without the need for intermediaries, and it can offer correct information to all parties involved, such as the actual number of clicks from users and the frequency and timing of ad display. Several businesses have been busy creating advertising networks that are powered by the blockchain ([Mushtaq & Haq, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r35)).

LEGAL-RELATED APPLICATIONS

The legal profession is vested with considerable authority, and as such, adheres to a unique set of rules governing conduct, trust accounting, and client confidentiality. The foundation of these needs is trust. This trust could not have been established with earlier technologies since they lacked the required security and transparency. With blockchain technology, this problem is now obsolete. It fills in the trust gap that previously existed in virtual interactions. Consequently, everyone with a smartphone will be able to seek and get trustworthy legal counsel over the internet. Online identity verification and the execution of legally binding contracts will be made possible. It will make possible, among many other things, the practice of fractional ownership of assets. If you're looking for a sector where blockchain could be useful, look no further than the legal sector. The large number of participants, the sheer volume of papers, information, and transactions, the dearth of confidence, and the substantial friction caused by legal fees are all major factors propelling its growth. Records keeping and financial transactions are the two key blockchain applications in the legal services industry. Information registry, identity management, smart contracts, the transaction registry, and payments are all subsets of these two applications. As blockchain develops and becomes stronger, it will be applied to more areas of the legal sector. Innovators in the field of law anticipate that blockchain and smart contracts will fundamentally alter the way that law is practiced. Intellectual property, real estate, public documents, the land register, and contracts are only some of the domains where blockchain and smart contracts could be used in the legal system. Case in point: Illinois tried out using blockchain to record and store land records in a pilot project ([Francisco & Swanson, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r14)).

REAL ESTATE APPLICATIONS

Brokers, government property databases, title firms, escrow companies, inspectors, appraisers, and notaries public are just a few of the many entities involved in a real estate transaction, contributing to its complexity, obscurity, and high cost. In 2017, there were 9,645 documented victims of real estate fraud. Openledger16 claims that blockchain may improve real estate operations by making them more predictable and transparent, hence making investments safer for all parties involved. Scams like these are only one area that blockchain technology can improve upon. Beyond combating fraud, the benefits of distributed ledger smart contracts include streamlined transactions, fewer middlemen, lower overall costs, and quicker deal closure ([Francisco & Swanson, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r14)). Especially in developing nations with legal systems lacking the idea of trust and openness in dealing with real estate records, or with unreliable land registry systems, blockchain can expedite the registration and transfer of property ownership. In addition, each property can have its own unique digital address, which conveys permanently and keeps track of all past transactions and includes information about the property's occupancy, finances, legal status, building performance, and physical qualities. To address present issues and revolutionize transactions, the real estate sector is well-positioned to adopt blockchain technology. The real estate market has already begun to see these shifts. The City of Rotterdam in the Netherlands, for instance, is collaborating with Deloitte to develop the first real estate-related Blockchain application for recording rental agreements ([Mukherjee & Singh, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r34)).

HEALTHCARE APPLICATIONS

Confidential patient health information is of paramount importance in today's healthcare system. More and more doctors are keeping patient data in computerized files called electronic medical records (EMR). The digitalization of medical data allows for quicker retrieval and exchange, which ultimately benefits patients by improving the quality of treatment and the efficiency with which decisions may be made, but it also presents a significant risk to patient privacy. Not only that, but the healthcare industry suffers from an absence of trustworthy ways for maintaining patients' health records. In order to provide the best treatment possible, several members of the healthcare team need access to separate data sets. At the present time, EMRs are scattered throughout several, unconnected systems. If these documents are still kept in print form, they may also be out of date. While EMR adoption is increasing in healthcare facilities, there has been a lag in the sharing of EMRs across various healthcare providers and healthcare organizations due to concerns about patient privacy and security. With blockchain technology, however, healthcare systems can guarantee that electronic medical records (EMRs) are up-to-date and that EMRs and other healthcare information may be securely shared across several providers. Blockchain technology may have further applications in the healthcare sector beyond the security of sensitive patient information. Medical personnel will have more time to spend with patients as a consequence of less time spent on administrative tasks, and researchers will be better equipped to share their discoveries and create more effective treatments. Improved medication research and less fraudulent claims and billing are two potential benefits. The healthcare industry is beginning to feel the effects of blockchain technology's use ([Cocco et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r8)).

APPROACHES

Public Blockchain

Any implementation of the decentralized principle may be used with these blockchains. They don't limit membership in any way, so long as they have a computer and access to the internet ([Erceg et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r11)).

Advantages

*   • Trustable: No-fraud algorithms already exist. There is no requirement for participants to worry about interfering with other nodes in the network.
*   • Secure: Since this blockchain may be accessed by everyone, it is quite sizable. For larger numbers, the spread of data points is more noticeable.
*   • Decentralized: Because the network is not centralized, each participant has their own copy of the ledger.

Disadvantages

*   • Processing: Due to its enormous size, the transaction procedure moves at a painfully slow pace. This time-consuming process of verifying each node is a major drawback.
*   • Energy Consumption: An excessive amount of power is needed for proof of work. To be an active member of the network, they need high-quality computing hardware.
*   • Acceptance: Governments are unable to integrate the technology quickly since there is no centralized authority.

Use Cases: As public Blockchains can be encrypted using either proof-of-work or proof-of-stake, they can be utilized to completely replace current monetary infrastructures. The smart contract that made this blockchain decentralized is the most complex part of it. Two popular public blockchains are Bitcoin and Ethereum ([Feng et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r12)).

PRIVATE BLOCKCHAIN

Since just a subset of nodes are permitted to take part in the process, these blockchains are inherently more secure than the public blockchain ([Feng et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r12)).

Advantages

*   • Speed: Due to the low value of the transaction, the rate is high. It takes less time to confirm each node.
*   • Scalability: They can alter the scalability. The size of the network can be determined manually.

Disadvantages

*   • Security: There is a potential for manipulation because of the small number of nodes in this type. These blockchains have greater security flaws.
*   • Centralized: One of the major drawbacks is how centralized trust establishment is. This can be exploited by corrupt organizations.

Use Cases: Blockchain technology, if properly protected and maintained, can be a powerful tool for keeping sensitive data safe from prying eyes. Therefore, they are used for voting purposes, asset management, and internal audits. Hyperledger Corda is a private blockchain platform ([Filimonau & Naumova, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r13)).

HYBRID BLOCKCHAIN

It's a blockchain that contains a combination of private and public data, where some transactions are kept under lock and key by a single entity and others are made public for everyone to see (Azzthey et al., 2019).

Advantages

*   • Ecosystem: That this blockchain is hybrid is maybe its greatest strength. Since 49% of users aren't even on the network, it's completely secure.
*   • Cost: Since only a small subset of nodes need to confirm a transaction, the cost per user is low. There is less of a need to verify all nodes, which reduces the computational burden on the network.
*   • Architecture: It's very flexible while yet keeping your data safe, private, and visible.

Disadvantages

*   • Efficiency: Unfortunately, not everyone can take advantage of a mixed Blockchain system. In addition, the company is having trouble with maintenance efficiency.
*   • Discretion: It is possible for someone to withhold data from the user. The organization's willingness to grant access to its hybrid blockchain will determine whether or not a user is granted access.

Use Case: It's a better answer for hospitals, governments, and banks, among other sectors. It's a solution for situations where people need public access to protected information. Ripple's network and XRP cryptocurrency are two working examples of a Hybrid Blockchain ([Yaeger et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r51)).

CONSORTIUM BLOCKCHAIN

This is an original strategy for meeting the requirements of the business. This blockchain does more than just verify trades; it can also start or receive trades ([Scholl et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r42)).

Advantages

*   • Speed: With a small user base, verification times are reduced. With such a fast transfer rate, this is more practical for use in businesses.
*   • Security: the data contained within the marked blocks is hidden from view. However, it is accessible to anybody who is a part of the blockchain network.
*   • Flexible: The blockchain's adaptability varies greatly. Given the low stakes, a quicker choice can be made.

Disadvantages

*   • Approval: Since the protocol has been approved by all of the members, it is less open to change. Since more than one group is involved, competing priorities may emerge.
*   • Transparency allows for hacking if the institution itself is corrupt. It's possible for businesses to withhold data from customers.
*   • Exposure to Risk: The blockchain is more likely to be vulnerable if only a small percentage of nodes are compromised.

Use Cases: It could be very useful for merchants, banks, and other types of payment processors. Organizational food tracking is best served by a federated solution because of the frequent cross-sector collaboration that occurs within it. Consortium Blockchains include Tendermint and Multichain ([Xu, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch010.xhtml#r50)).

CONCLUSION AND RECOMMENDATIONS

Blockchain is gaining traction as a potentially game-changing technology in the fields of distributed computing and data assurance, where it might be used to build reliable and secure systems for sharing and transferring data. Blockchain has a lot of potential when it comes to cybersecurity, but it also has a lot of unanswered questions about things like security, scalability, and performance that limit how well it can be applied to real-world business models. The decentralized and peer-to-peer nature of blockchain's underlying architecture has led to its broad recognition and adoption as a promising new technology. Blockchain's architectural elements have shown the ability to streamline laborious processes including transaction verification, difference reconciliation, and dispute resolution. Blockchain also has the potential to disrupt the way conventional companies function because to its basic qualities like as distribution, privacy, immutability, and audibility. To achieve its goal of eliminating middlemen, particularly in financial transactions, blockchain relies on a decentralized consensus system for processing and validating transactions. Proof-of-Work (PoW), Proof-of-Stake (PoS), Proof-by-Fee-Transaction (PBFT), and Distributed Proof-of-Stake are the most common consensus mechanisms used by active blockchain systems (DPoS). Although blockchain was first envisioned as a public distributed ledger, there are now several different blockchain implementations. Public blockchains, private blockchains, and consortium blockchains are the three main categories of distributed ledger systems. Considerations such as investment funds, the need for complete anonymity, and the intended consequences are crucial in deciding which blockchain technology to use. Financial institutions, for instance, put a premium on anonymity, and this is why they are more interested in private blockchains. Concurrently, organizations who participate in similar activities and want to achieve similar outcomes are ready to share expenses and data, and thus may choose to utilize consortium blockchains.

Until recently, blockchain's only use was in the Bitcoin sector; however, new innovations have greatly widened this technology's applicability. Findings from this study suggest that blockchain technology has broad potential for use across numerous sectors, including but not limited to those of advertising and media, energy, real estate, healthcare, and many more. Several industries are taking an interest in blockchain and doing research into its capabilities and uses as they realize they can no longer ignore blockchain's fundamental benefits, which include, among others, transparency, business continuity, disintermediation, and trust. As smart contracts may now be implemented with the help of blockchain technology, there have been several suggestions for their use. It is common knowledge that blockchain technology has the ability to solve several issues. However, they are only now becoming aware of some of the obstacles that blockchain will have to overcome, such as those related to scalability, security, and law. For blockchain technology to become more trustworthy and effective, it is crucial to address the current limitations it faces.

Future Scope

It will be crucial for the future of the Digital Transformation that many essential areas be prioritized. The goal is to facilitate simpler use of remote features. In our opinion, the company's first priority should be the welfare of its employees. The processing capacity of digital twins might be used in data mining and forecasting. The emergence of no-code platforms might make it easy for anybody to create their own paid app. The marital of quantum and edge computing might usher in a new era of digital transformation. All photonics networks must provide end-to-end data integrity. Information sent between a client device and a server is crucial to the growth of any functional digital system. In all the above applications block chain is expected to play a crucial role in years to come.

SUMMARY

*   • The significance, benefits, advantages, and limitations of blockchain technology have been covered in the introduction. It has been noted that one major shortcoming of blockchain technology is that it might be difficult to alter or update information after it has been recorded.
*   • Impacts on the Global Economy and Economic Benefits of Blockchain, Theatrical Concerns of Blockchain, and Business Applications of Blockchain have all been covered in this literature review.
*   • On the Blockchain Taxonomy side of things. The Consortium blockchain, as they've seen, has a wide range of applications in the business sector, and have established that profiling focuses on responsibility and authority.
*   • In the section under “Blockchain Application,” they covered the many different ways that blockchain technology may be put to use in several industries, including but not limited to finance, accounting, insurance, supply chain management, and many more.
*   • There are three major obstacles on the path of digital marketing. To begin, the advertising industry is notoriously saturated with intermediaries, who inflate costs for all parties. Whether it's the advertiser or the publisher, everyone involved in internet advertising is starting to notice that it's become more difficult and expensive to do their jobs effectively.
*   • At the end of the advantages and disadvantages section, we discussed the differences between private, public, hybrid, and consortium blockchain.
