# Chapter 11

*   Aniket Ghorpade  
    Lovely Professional University, Phagwara, India
*   Uma Pandey  
    Lovely Professional University, India

ABSTRACT

Blockchain is a technology that allows for the open storage of every transaction that a given network generates. The term “blockchain” refers to a sequence of blocks made up of encrypted copies of each register. The main feature of blockchain technology is decentralized data, which means that data is spread across the many nodes or computers that make up the network. Blockchain secure database platform has been established in the past decades. Blockchain can be extensively and effectively implemented in the preservation, recognition, and development of ICH globally. It can be used as a secure database platform as well as an efficient and easy market platform for authentic ICH practitioners and products and services. The present study will evaluate the use of blockchain technology in preserving the ICH of India and also highlight the advantages of the said technology. The data is collected from secondary sources. This study will offer a reference for the network security research related to the digital protection of the ICH.

INTRODUCTION

Culture is not reducible to material objects since it is dynamic and ever-changing; instead, it consists of the indefinable qualities that embody the human culture and are collectively known as intangible cultural heritage ([Goetcheus & Mitchell, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r21)). This encompasses a broad spectrum of cultural values and norms such as arts, social behaviours, rituals, beliefs, and events, and incorporates diverse components of cultural heritage such as traditions and live expressions acquired from ancestors. The meaning of the word “cultural heritage” has changed because of the various tools created by UNESCO. Cultural heritage extends beyond monuments and artifact collections. It also includes customs or living experiences that have been passed on from generations, such as oral traditions, performing arts, social customs, rituals, holiday celebrations, and knowledge and customs related to nature and the cosmos as well as the know-how required to make traditional crafts which are considered intangible. This Intangible Cultural Heritage (ICH) has been the focus of tourism organizations for the past two decades because of its valuation and rareness across the globe. Organizations like UNESCO and UNWTO have been extensively active in recognizing ICH globally.

UNESCO has identified 631 intangible cultural heritages corresponding to 140 countries till now. The disappearance of intangible cultural heritage is a persistent concern, mostly as a result of globalization. To address this issue, UNESCO created the convention for the safeguarding of Intangible Culture Heritage in 2003.

To date, many countries have signed on to the Convention and enrolled close to 500 ICH essentials on the lists of Intangible Cultural Heritage and the Register of Good Safeguarding Practices. Cultural heritage is the metaphorical presence that integrates a country's history, traditions, and culture. It is an extraordinary source of pride for the people of that country, but it also has universal value that can touch people all over the world. There are countless properties of valuable intangible and tangible cultural heritage that are currently endangered. This paper investigates the possibility of preserving and making available Indian cultural heritage reserves in a digital world.

It is challenging to gather, identify, and manage intangible cultural resources at present. Thus, there is a growing interest in studying about safeguarding ICH. As a way to safeguard ICH many diverse facets of ICH digitization have attracted the interest of various scholars. These range from the three-dimensional digitization of artworks and archives, ethical and legal considerations involved in the revitalization of cultural artifacts, management, and preservation of intangible cultural heritage.

Management of intangible cultural resources has benefited greatly from the proliferation of computing technology and the rise in living standards around the world ([Cisneros et al., 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r8)). Using paper archives, for example, to manage intangible cultural material has the drawbacks of sluggish search speed and a complex administration process. Through database queries, the digital ICH management system can expedite the retrieval and monitoring of ICH, streamline the management of ICH through online approval, cut down on labour-expenditure, as well as boost workplace productivity ([Jun & Li, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r25)).

However, digital intangible cultural heritage creation has recently grown the question of safeguarding the validity and authenticity of intangible cultural heritage documentation against theft, tampering, and destruction. Nowadays a lot of countries are exploring different technologies to safeguard intangible cultural heritage and blockchain is one of them. Blockchain is a decentralized, trustless, and highly anti-calculation-modification dispersed ledger technology. Cryptography, probability speculation, agreement procedures, and circulated networks are only a few of the computer technologies utilized ([Ferdous et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r19)). The Crypto blockchain was the first function of blockchain tools, and its thousands of distributed nodes have been up and running for the utmost with no major disruptions. When it comes to protecting sensitive digital data, the reliability and traceability characteristics are superior to those of conventional network security. The emergence of blockchain technology has revolutionized the way we manage intangible heritage. This technology has the potential to revolutionize the way we store, protect, and share intangible heritage. It can also provide a secure platform for the preservation and transmission of cultural heritage. In this study we will discuss the implications of blockchain technology in intangible heritage management and how it can be used to protect and preserve our cultural heritage.

Blockchain Technology (BCT)

In this technology-savvy world, many countries are trying to use blockchain to preserve intangible cultural heritage. The best way to describe blockchain is as a virtual live database that has some special characteristics ([Ouaddah et al., 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r32)). First, the capacity to make a distinct ledger entry for every and every commercial transaction is a feature of blockchain technology. Once a transaction is recorded, it cannot be changed in any way, including being updated, deleted, or added. This has a big advantage because it lessens the chance of fraud and/or human error. Furthermore, because every record in this ecosystem is permanent, has a date and time stamp, and makes the recorded transaction visible to all users of the Blockchain system, it facilitates total and unadulterated transaction transparency. Third, these transactions are recorded in precise chronological sequence and are posted in real-time with no time gaps ([Dai & Vasarhelyi, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r10)). As each transaction is parked into its own “Block” and automatically linked to the one before it and the one after it, a “Block” of transactions is created; “Blockchain” ([Williams, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r51)). The tourism sector could undergo a significant transformation thanks to blockchain technology. Its key characteristics, including immutability, transparency, programmability, and decentralization, enable novel organizational structures and processes and make room for new types of inter-organizational collaboration ([Treiblmaier, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r50)). Blockchain, according to Angus Hervey, “is the latest model that renders the active model obsolete.” Bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies are frequently linked to the blockchain. Blockchain is a decentralized database system with robust security built on several data protections, and high capacities. It can be described as a sizable distributed public ledger that keeps track of transactions across a network. Blockchain technology eliminates the requirement for travellers to present their physical IDs by using digital IDs, which speeds up travel and decreases waiting times. Businesses that are affected can establish safe digital records and store sensitive data securely thanks to the potential use of blockchain technology (BCT) ([Rashideh, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r37)). All sorts of businesses will be affected by BCT because of its ability to cut costs, boost efficiency, lessen the risk of data falsification, intensify confidence among business partners, and decrease the need for intermediaries ([Yin et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r53)). To better anticipate future developments in the hospitality business, it is crucial to research all facets of BCT and its relationships within and between industries. High levels of competition are a hallmark of this sector, which is characterized by its heterogeneity and the complexities of the business interactions among partners, persons, travel services, and business processes ([Maratovna et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r30)). It is necessary to adopt the most recent technologies in order to constantly adapt to these conditions. These technologies will supply the information that the industry needs in order to quickly change, adapt, integrate, and link the stakeholders involved in the industry. This must be done while taking into consideration the varied needs of travellers as well as the growing demand for speed in the provision of services.

Intangible Cultural Heritage

ICH refers to a wide range of social behaviours, conceptual expressions, knowledge, skills, tools, handicrafts, and cultural locations that are seen as part of a community's or group's or an individual's cultural heritage by that community or group or by the individual in question ([Baixinho et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r4)). The safeguarding of ICH is an organized cultural undertaking whose foundational components include data mining, data administration, data transmission, etc. ([Alivizatou-barakou et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r1); [Cominelli & Greffe, 2012](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r9)). It is widely known that anything that can only be described as an “intangible cultural heritage” (ICH) bears evidence to an essential component of human civilization. As a consequence of this, everyone agrees that its safety is of the utmost importance. However, doing so would require enormous financial investment, and the funds from the administration and larger society have to spare are paltry ([X. Su et al., 2020a](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r45)). Recent decades have seen a significant change in the meaning of the phrase “cultural heritage,” in part because of the tools created by UNESCO. Cultural heritage extends beyond monuments and artefact collections. It also includes customs or livelihood expressions that have been passed down from our family to our descendants, such as oral customs, performing arts, social customs, rituals, holiday celebrations, and knowledge and customs related to nature and the cosmos and the know-how necessary to make traditional craft ([Roszczynska-Kurasinska et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r38)).

It is acknowledged that “intangible cultural heritage” (ICH) is a sign of a significant attribute of human civilization. Therefore, the growth of tourism related to ICH has emerged as a crucial new avenue for raising money for its defence ([X. Su et al., 2020a](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r45)). Even though it is vulnerable, intangible cultural heritage plays a fundamental role in safeguarding cultural diversity in this time of accelerating globalisation. A better understanding of the intangible cultural past of the various groups enhances intercultural communication and creates value for a variety of diverse living styles (UNESCO). This is not so much about the cultural expressions themselves as it is about the vast amounts of information and skills that are passed down through generations as part of intangible cultural legacy. This is why it is of such critical significance. This information transmission is essential for both well-established nations and those that are still in the process of emerging, and its sociological and financial value extends to both marginal and mainstream socioeconomic groups within a given state. The preservation of intangible cultural assets is becoming increasingly important to the expansion of the tourist industry as a result of the extensive cultural and aesthetic value that it possesses. Authenticity is essential to the expansion of the tourist industry centred on intangible cultural assets. Cultural identity places a significance on the affirmation of a common culture among individuals and groups. This relevance arises in connection to cultural history as well as the natural environment. Innovative possibilities in the area of teaching about and preserving intangible cultural heritage (ICH) have been made possible by recent technological advancements. This paper's main aim is to provide a study whose focus was to determine whether and how much technology may contribute to ICH protection and education ([Pozzi & Katos, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r34)). A sense of value and acknowledgment are at the heart of cultural identity. The research into cultural identity within the background of tourism has drawn progressive attention as the travel industry continues to grow. When seen in the perspective of globalisation, intangible tourism based on culture gives visitors access to a diversity of identities for places that emphasise the significance of cultural identity ([Tian et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r48)). Since the beginning of cultural heritage tourism, authenticity has been linked to cultural tourism. This association continues to this day. Authenticity has emerged as a central concept due to the fact that the protection of intangible cultural assets is presently the most important aspect of heritage conservation. It has been extensively used to refer to the potential of culture and heritage to function as a source of economic growth. This is particularly evident in the process of developing economies to serve their strategic interests, and it is one reason why cultural and heritage tourism is becoming increasingly popular. This potential is in adding up to it being acknowledged as a method to promote and disseminate heritage elements and forms more widely. In order to strengthen the sense of nationalism at the national level and to spread specific ideology abroad, several nations also use cultural creation and heritage tourism. Heritage tourism does, however, operate as a source of causing changes in heritage forms, which is something to take into consideration. Concerns concerning historic forms' sustainability have regularly been raised by tourism ([Ranwa, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r36)). However, due to the confused definition of ICH tourism, the massive and dispersed structure of ICH data, and the general acceptance of ICH tourism among academics, it is difficult to investigate and make conclusions on the most recent research stance on ICH tourism. To conclude the trends and patterns and future scope of ICH tourism, more extraction and summarization are required. Reviewing the state of ICH tourism research now reveals the theoretical worth and practical significance of this work ([Qiu et al., 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r35)). Since the 1990s, the sector of international cultural heritage has been in the front of the “digitization” trend in the cultural archiving and preservation world. In the years that followed 2003, a large number of countries started their own digital ICH programmes. Studies conducted by ICH have, for some time now, shifted their attention away from cultural forms of inquiry and toward informational forms of inquiry on a global scale. The majority of research on ICH that is carried out by academics in the United States focuses on either ICH technology or ICH in digital format. Digital imaging technology, three-dimensional technology, virtual reality and augmented reality technology, three-dimensional spatial positioning technology, and other facets of technology are the primary areas of study in the field of ICH technology research. Studies in digital morphology at ICH are conducted primarily from the vantage points of the ICH database, the digital museum, and animation games ([Dou et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r13); [Kong & Zhang, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r26)). One way is that the knowledge economy offers intangible elements a deciding role in the development and manufacture of new products. In two ways, this relates to artistic traditions and methods. They foster creativity and provide a huge number of inspirations in provisions of signs, forms, colors, and symbols for all sectors of the economy (from crafts to style and furnishings to the industry) as a source of legacy that is always renewing itself. Arts and heritage can establish guidelines or standards for creativity that are capable of being applied in other industries because they are inherently creative activities ([Cominelli & Greffe, 2012](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r9)). There are indeed benefits to safeguarding ICH through digitization, except there are also risks to data security that should not be ignored. A firewall can be installed in the structure to keep an eye out for malicious IP packets and prevent unauthorized users from logging in. Aside from the foregoing approaches, other areas of international network security study include cryptographic algorithms, system design, security protocols, wireless sensor networks, and smart grids. As a science, technology, as well as the economy have progressed over the past few years, a new system known as “blockchain” has arisen ([Chang et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r6)). Decentralization, immutability, traceability, and high credibility are all features of blockchain-based network information security ([Chen et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r7); [Dwivedi et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r15)).

METHODOLOGY

To meet the objective of this paper extensive literature review was done. By doing a literature review, you may get acquainted with the state of knowledge in your subject and the limits of that knowledge. Reviewing the relevant literature can also shed light on the underlying theories in your subject, which will help you better frame your research topic.

In this section, we have presented the review analysis of 25 research papers. Based on our analysis we have identified three key themes which are presented in Table 1.

Implications of Blockchain Technology in the Tourism Sector

Many advantages of blockchain can be utilized in the protection of the ICH. The appeal and beauty of BCT are further bolstered by its underlying concepts and guiding ideals. They include 1) a distributed ledger format that gives each system member access to a “public” record of transactions, 2) a distributed consensus, wherein all participants concur that the records are accurate and genuine, 3) immutability since each participant is a component of the structure and is unable to operate on their own, 4) provenance, as each transaction has a digital history that can be tracked and validated as to its provenance, characteristics, and ownership and 5) native cryptography since the blockchain has strong security features built in to keep unauthorized intruders out. The hospitality services and tourism sector, like other sectors, is constantly looking for cutting-edge solutions to assist boosting competitive edge, elevate customer fulfilment, and enhance bottom-line performance. To this aim, the lodging, travel agency, and food service industries can strategically use blockchain to achieve this purpose. Other benefits of using Blockchain for the travel agency/agent industry include, but are not restricted to 1) secure transfer and sharing of client/passenger information, 2) online dependability, 3) better transaction security, and 4) the effective mitigation of lost or stolen records and transactions. Tourism is heavily reliant on culture, current conditions, and historical events. Any site can attract tourists thanks to its unique cultural experiences, so it is essential to protect that heritage to preserve its legacy.

Intangible Cultural Heritage (ICH) and Digitalization

The valuation of cultural heritage is derived from its authenticity, quality, and intangible values. These elements contribute to the cultural asset's uniqueness and non-replicability. Digitization has created intriguing threats to cultural heritage authentication because it can be conveniently modified, recreated, and distributed at low cost if not properly managed ([Gipp et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r20)). Technology enables us to overcome issues concerning the authenticity and origins of works and ethnic content. As Angelova (2019) proposes, blockchain may be utilized to create an unchanging registration that contains the form and categorization of cultural property, as well as all activities performed and authorised on cultural heritage assets. The preservation of documentary heritage must be considered in terms of organising what we did inherit from the history in such a way that we can all give it over to the coming years. Documentary protection makes perfect sense only when we take responsibility for the preservation of objects, built-in environments, and landscapes. Digitization is now a matter of practicality and reality, with technological interventions providing improved access to data resources, preservation, and dissemination as needed, at any time and from any location. This research contributes much to the discussion of blockchain's potential applications in heritage conservation by outlining a path toward further disintermediation. It also adds to the ongoing conversation on the qualities and advantages of blockchain technology as they pertain to safeguarding intangible cultural treasures. Finding ways to preserve our cultural heritage through digital preservation of resources. If the manuscripts are digitised and made available on the internet, they will be easily available from anywhere in the world, and users will not need to travel to the locations where the documents are physically preserved. The significance of digital preservation could be overstated. Culture and heritage are valuable legacies that all people share across national boundaries and must be passed on to succeeding generations.

Blockchain technology provides a method for linking intangible cultural assets with science, tourism, and heritage preservation ([Shuai & Yu, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r41)). One of the greatest ways to put the concept into practice is with the use of blockchain technology, which can also be utilised to better protect the ICH travel experiences ([Hu et al., 2015](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r23); [Zhang et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r55)) as stated by [Anagnostakis (2019)](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r2). The introduction of Blockchain in cultural heritage documentation will provide an invaluable, universal, and immune timeline of cultural heritage documentation records, safeguarding the validity of data and the value of tokens and facilitating in the most effective way the resolution of ownership and enhancement problems for future generations.

If the blockchain strategy is properly used, it can be used to a degree that has yet to be investigated. An exceedingly delicate and successful strategy for drawing tourists to India's culturally extraordinarily rich country is the preservation of its intangible cultural heritage. In this process, blockchain technology could be an extremely helpful tool. When examining the benefits and repercussions of blockchain for the tourism industry, it becomes abundantly evident that this technology has the potential to advance the concept of intangible cultural heritage ([Panasiuk, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r33)).

Blockchain can be incorporated into the ICH tourism sector for encrypted data storage about the various ICH practices and performances ([Pozzi & Katos, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r34)). For booking lodging, services, and ICH items in several locations, blockchain can offer a very safe platform. At ICH destinations, blockchain can be employed as a carrier of protected tourist data and procedures. For safe payment methods like cryptocurrencies and other flexible payment options at ICH locations, blockchain can be suggested. Blockchain technology may be used as a strategy to win over tourists to an ICH destination by ensuring that services are delivered in a secure manner. The blockchain's core characteristics can be utilised to deduce other attributes. One of the most important is (dispersed) trust, or the replacement of faith in people or organisations with trust in blockchain-based systems where information is immutably stored while algorithms are automatically performed out However, care must be taken to prevent erroneously believing that BCT can solve every trust-related issue. Potential problems include programming errors, malicious network intrusions, or powerful entities working behind the scenes to affect events ([Degbey & Pelto, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r11)). Another advantage of utilising asymmetric encryption is that data may be encrypted and decrypted using a pair of keys, which ensures authenticity. A so-called public key could be given to anyone, unlike a private key, which must be safeguarded by the owner. To decrypt a communication that has been encrypted, only the pair's first key can be utilised. Encryption algorithm can be employed for both confidentiality and authentication. This is accomplished by simply adding a private key to a message. Anyone who possesses the corresponding public key can immediately determine that a specific transaction might only have been made by the owner of the corresponding private key ([Dubey et al., 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r14); [Treiblmaier, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r50)).

Blockchain Technology and Indian ICH Preservation

The diverse and everlasting culture that has been passed down through the generations in India is without a doubt the most significant contribution that the country has made to the rest of the globe. This account of history includes almost the entirety of mankind's search for knowledge. This history is presently spread out among several different archival collections as well as private collections. Cultural heritage sites must respond and adapt to shifting contexts in a coherent manner, that is, in a way that is consistent with the memory and character of the place, in order to stay alive and relevant. The long-term viability of urban cultural assets can be negatively impacted by incoherent changes or transformations that, in the opinion of the local community, are not consistent with the character of a place ([Roszczynska-Kurasinska et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r38)). Since UNESCO first explicitly recommended protecting ICH in 2003, it really has progressively gained academics' attention and sparked lively academic debates. The aforementioned works have concentrated on the digital gathering, archiving, and presentation of particular kinds of ICH. The utilisation of digital technology (such as databases and motion capture technology) and digital presentation techniques have been the key topics of discussion (such as serious game design and interactive narratives). The monitoring of digital data over a period of time is known as digital preservation. It consists of procedures and operations that ensure ongoing access to data and all types of digital data, both technological and cultural heritage. The goal of digital conserving is long-term, error-free repository of digital information with retrieval and interpretation capabilities for the duration of the information's requirement. The subjects of ICH exhibitions are people and activities. Designers must interact with ICH's inheritors and communities, enter their lives, and gather new information on ICH. They must also take into account moral dilemmas and challenges with value orientations ([Kong & Zhang, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r26)).

Unfortunately, many aspects of India's physical past have been mutilated and obliterated as a result of some invasions; furthermore, some of this history is continually being taken, and books and manuscripts are degrading. The maintenance and protection of Indian culture is a challenging endeavour ([Ruhanen & Whitford, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r39)). The excellent thing is that the rapidly developing information technology field may be able to provide not just a resolution for preservation but also for enhancement and increased accessibility. The bad news is that information technology hasn't advanced very quickly. If Indian libraries digitised and disseminated their vast, ancient, and timeless cultural heritage, it might have a significant impact on the rest of the globe. The Indian government has started developing national policies and initiatives to protect the rich cultural legacy of the nation. Since these unique works of art are housed in so many different institutions across the nation, it is the duty of every individual library, museum, and archive to embrace digital preservation techniques to guarantee their accessibility in the future. A new analysis of the relevance, importance, and advantages to society and the global community of many of the initiatives currently being carried out by a range of organisations, institutions, and people all over the country of India is required ([Sarabia-Molina et al., 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r40)). Because it gives us a tangible connection to our past and a sense of who we are as a people, cultural heritage preservation is vital to the success of the tourism industry. Art contributes to the preservation of culture, along with language, music, and craft, through the inheritance of traditions ([Singh, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r42)). In light of recent developments in information technology, it is critical to give serious consideration to the use of technologies such as blockchain for the preservation of cultural heritage to offer an additional layer of safety.

CONCLUSION

Thanks to the fast-paced growth of IT, digital protection of ICH is making strides in India and elsewhere, with more significant ICHs adopting this strategy. Research into digital security at the worldwide level is driven by three key areas: digital acquisition, digital preservation, and digital development. From the point of view of managing and utilizing digital information, research into digital protection focuses primarily on three facets: digital information organization, retrieval, and administration. The proliferation of connected devices and the Internet, however, has led to an explosion in the volume of data available online.

Despite its usefulness, the exchange of such vast amounts of data also poses security concerns. As the ICH continues to digitize its resources, more and more of those resources will become available online. Cyberspace poses a security concern for digital data. The preservation of digital information resources is a new concern that has arisen as a result of ICH's digitization ([Bilgiçli, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r5)). Integrity and encryption of ICH's digital information resources based on blockchain technology have been the topic of several investigations. The tourist industry is only one of several that might benefit from the increased disintermediation made possible by blockchain technology. The business model, monetary transfer mechanisms, security, performance, trustworthiness, etc. of the travel industry might all be affected by this technology ([Latifi et al., 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch011.xhtml#r27)). Digitization projects involving artifacts from other cultures, rare literature, and historical archives have sparked interest in conducting digital studies across a wider range of disciplines. Currently, India should compete significantly with other Asian governments, such as China, in researching the implications of blockchain technology for ICH protection.

DISCUSSION

There are benefits to using block technology for the digital security of ICH. Comprehensively evaluating the digital preservation of ICH is one way to better achieve the transformation and inheritance of ICH information resources. This includes looking at it from the viewpoints of government policy direction, market deployment, and social support. The beneficial effects that blockchain technology may have present a promising option for the preservation of historical records. Researchers will profit from this study by learning more about the features and advantages of blockchain technology and how they might be used for the preservation of cultural artifacts as well as intangible cultural heritage. Blockchain can be included in models of heritage conservation to lessen the need for middlemen and encourage new entries into the field.

LIMITATIONS AND FUTURE RECOMMENDATIONS

This chapter is based on secondary data and a critical study of the same. We only considered papers published after 2010 when compiling our sources, and we limited ourselves to the Google Scholar database. Future research utilising a different methodology could be able to address the problems we encountered. A deep study using primary data from various stakeholders and a different quantitative or qualitative approach for the study of the implications can be done in the future for more enhanced and calculated results.

The current study stresses mainly on advantages of using blockchain in ICH enhancement and preservation, including how it will improve corporate operations, the standard of offered goods and services, data handling and security, as well as the national economy. However, several experts in other domains contend that blockchain technology itself has several security flaws. Future studies should therefore look into the main difficulties or disadvantages presented by blockchain technology and analyse their implications for ICH conservation using blockchain technology.

# Chapter 12

Worldwide Supplier of Digital Services and Technology – Tactics, Methods, Limitations, and Performance

*   Snehlata Barde  
    MATS University, Raipur, India
*   Aaryan Barde  
    Lakshmi Narain College of Technology, India

ABSTRACT

Blockchain is an emerging technology and has gained trust in the world of tech in no time. Data is the most important asset for any organization or institute as it holds the base of all information. Every now and then, cases of data leaking, piracy, and stealing could be found in many companies, businesses, industries, and markets ruining the database and causing hindrance to growth. This is where blockchain acts as a savior for these business and industrial organizations by protecting their data and transactions. Blockchain is a method of recording and handling data that makes it tough for the system to be altered, hacked, or manipulated by illegal sources. “Block” of public or the “chain” is the assembly that keeps transactional records which is connected by peer-to-peer nodes. This storage is also known as a “digital ledger.” It can be defined as a distributed ledger that replicates and allocates the transactions across the network of computers contributing to the blockchain.

CONCEPT OF BLOCKCHAIN WITH DIAGRAM

Blockchain further has various advantages over old-school methods of storing data. Blockchain networks are scalable that is; they have the capability to support the increasing load of a transaction, as well as the data-storing nodes are flexible and can be reduced and increased as per the requirement. That is why it is a very crucial and major leader in the field of business because it increases the growth of the business to an unmarkable level ([Barde,2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#r3)).

Any application development is a procedure that eliminates the need for people to perform tasks like banking and ticketing manually. A blockchain, according to Satoshi Nakamoto, is a group of records that are connected together and employ cryptography for safety. These records are known as blocks and are constantly expanding. Blockchain technologies provide a future-proof solution for a wide range of transactions and address the issue of record storage for the following group. The core idea behind the development of this technology by Stuart Haber and W. Scott Stornetta was between the 1980s and 1990s. There are many blocks in the block chain. [Figure 1](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#f01) depicts a blockchain diagram with three morals: data, pre-hash value and hash value (Bresnaha, 1995).

| Figure 1. Blockchain |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.f01](i/ch012.f01.png) |
| --- |

The majority of senior executives (63%) and business owners continue to be confused about what blockchain technology is and why it matters, according to a Global Blockchain Business Council poll that was published in Coin Telegraph. As a result, the need for blockchain has become essential. However, many service-providing, automotive, and product managing industries have become the primary database controller, making the need for blockchain even more critical.

Businesses deal with a lot of data on a daily basis, but many business owners are still unaware of the advantages that blockchain technology can bring about for the growth of their companies, which has a significant negative impact on the service.

Blockchain is a useful and essential tool for enhancing firm security for a number of such whys and wherefores.

Advantages of employing blockchain technology over conventional manual techniques for small enterprises' growth:

*   • Strong Security: The technology is equipped with the ability to shield and safeguard data from threats to its security.
*   • Wealth Creation: By reducing the costs associated with using third-party vendors, adopting blockchain technology can help the company save a significant amount of money.
*   • Immutability: Once the information has been saved in a node, it cannot be changed or removed. Data is safe and secure since all transactions are accurately recorded and marked with their time and date, which cannot be changed.
*   • Greater Effectiveness and Speed: By eliminating many pointless middlemen, transactions can be carried out more quickly and creatively. eliminates the need for pen and paper and saves a tonne of documentation and paperwork.

EVOLUTION OF BLOCKCHAIN

In general, there are two stages in the development of blockchain technology. The first phase spans the years 1991 through 2013.

In reality, the cost of Lehman Brothers' 2008 bankruptcy to the United States is believed to be in the trillions, and regrettably, it sparked a chain of events that caused an economic downturn or depression in a number of countries.

As the first implementation of Blockchain technology, Bitcoin was created in 2008. With the use of cutting-edge technology known as Bitcoin, banks might be able to settle their own accounts without the need for centralized authorities. Bitcoin is regarded as the first globally functional decentralized money system built on Blockchain technology. It uses peer-to-peer networking, digital signatures, and cryptographic proofs of work to establish a distributed ledger. The second phase of the blockchain, dubbed Contracts, spans the years 2013 through 2015. In 2013, Vitalik Buterin created Ethereum, a brand-new public blockchain with more features than Bitcoin (Barde S., 2020).

This invention has since become recognized as a turning point in the history of the Blockchain. By introducing a feature that enables for comparison with Bitcoin Blockchain, he distinguished Ethereum from persons who keep track of other assets like contracts. With new features, Ethereum's capabilities are broadened from that of a coin to include a stage for creating decentralized apps. Additionally in 2015, the Linux Foundation released the Hyperledger open-source blockchain umbrella project. No cryptocurrency, including Bitcoin, is supported by Hyperledger. The goal of Hyperledger is to promote.

REAL-LIFE APPLICATION OF BLOCKCHAIN

Here is a list of real-world problems where we can use blockchain (Franca, 2021):

*   1\. In a secure and full-proof voting management system.
*   2\. To supply chain management.
*   3\. In healthcare management.
*   4\. Real estate project.
*   5\. NFT marketplace.
*   6\. Avoid copyright and original content creation.
*   7\. In the personal identity system.
*   8\. To make an immutable data backup.
*   9\. Internet of things.

KINDS OF BLOCKCHAIN

These are of four kinds:

*   • Public blockchain.
*   • Private blockchain.
*   • Hybrid blockchain.
*   • Consortium blockchain.

Public Blockchain

These type of blockchains are completely amenable to implement the decentralization concept. Anybody with a computer and internet access can connect the network; there is no limitations. This blockchain is available for every user because its name is public, which infers it is not measured by anyone. Anyone with internet access and a computer equipped with adequate hardware can participate in this blockchain shown in [Figure 2](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#f02).

Nodes or network blocks are copies of themselves on every machine in the network. We can also carry out transaction or record verification on this open blockchain.

| Figure 2. Public blockchain network |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.f02](i/ch012.f02.png) |
| --- |

Advantages

*   • Trustworthy: Algorithms exist to identify any scam. Participants don't need to worry about the additional nodes in the network.
*   • Secure: This blockchain is significant because it is open to all users. A larger variety of records come in enormous sizes.
*   • Anonymous Nature: You can join without disclosing your name or identify, making it a secure platform for your transaction.
*   • Decentralized: Each user has a copy of the ledger; there is no single platform that manages the network.

Disadvantages

*   • Processing: As a result of its magnitude, the transaction procedure moves at a fairly slow pace. The procedure of verifying each node takes a long time.
*   • Energy Use: Producing a proof of work requires a lot of energy. To join the network, quality computer hardware is required.
*   • Acceptance: Governments are forced to cope with the difficulty of incorporating technology more quickly since there is no centralized authority.

Private Blockchain

These blockchains are not as dispersed as the public blockchain, it is more secure than the others because only a minor number of nodes can contribute in the process. These are less transparent than a public blockchain. They are only accessible to specific authorized users. On a private network, these blockchains function. In this, only a small number of people are allowed to join a network inside a company or organization shown in [Figure 3](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#f03).

| Figure 3. Private blockchain network |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.f03](i/ch012.f03.png) |
| --- |

Advantages

*   • Speed: The rate is high because the transaction is so small. Confirmation of each node requires less time.
*   • Scalability: The scalability can be changed. The network's size can be by hand selected.
*   • Privacy: The level of privacy has increased as a result of rising commercial expectations for confidentiality.
*   • Balanced: The transaction is more balanced because it is only accessible by a small number of users, which improves network performance.

Disadvantages

*   • Security: This type of node is rarer, thus there's a chance that someone may alter it. There are greater security dangers with these blockchains.
*   • The difficulty of building trust is one of its main problems as a result of centralization. Businesses can use this technique to punish wrongdoing.
*   • The blockchain system as a whole could be in jeopardy if any nodes go offline due to the limited number of nodes.

3\. Hybrid Blockchain

It is a mixture of private and public blockchain content, where some portions are under the authority of a single company and others are made openly accessible. It combines blockchains that are both public and private. Both permission and non-permissioned systems are used.

Despite being the owner of this blockchain, a major entity cannot alter a transaction. Smart contracts enable users to acquire information shown in [Figure 4](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#f04).

| Figure 4. Hybrid blockchain network |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.f04](i/ch012.f04.png) |
| --- |

Advantages

*   • Ecosystem: The hybrid character of this blockchain is its greatest strength. The network cannot be hacked because 51% of users are not connected.
*   • Due to the fact that just a small number of nodes verify transactions, they are cheap. Because not every node performs the verification, the computational cost is reduced.
*   • Architecture: It offers a great degree of adaptability while upholding integrity, security, and transparency.
*   • Operations: It has the power to choose which blockchain users to include and whose transactions should be made available to the public.

Disadvantages

*   • Effectiveness: Not everyone can put a hybrid Blockchain into practice. Additionally, the business has certain issues with maintenance efficiency.
*   • Transparency: Someone could choose to hide some information from the user. It depends on the organization whether it will give access to someone requesting it via a hybrid blockchain.
*   • This blockchain ecosystem is lacking incentives for network membership because of its closed ecosystem ([Barde, 2022](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#r4)).

Consortium Blockchain

It is a novel approach that satisfies the demands of the company, which also sends or receives transactions, validates the transaction. It is also known as Federal Blockchain. It’s a novel approach as how it addresses the organization's needs. A part of it is both public and private. This blockchain is managed by multiple organizations shown in [Figure 5](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#f05).

| Figure 5. Consortium blockchain network |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.f05](i/ch012.f05.png) |
| --- |

Advantages

*   • Speed: Verification is quick because there aren't many users. Organizations can use this more readily because of the fast speed.
*   • Authority: It can be dispersed at every level and involve multiple entities. Decentralized power increases security.
*   • Privacy: The checked blocks' information is hidden from the general public. But everyone who uses the blockchain can access it.
*   • Flexible: The blockchain's degree of adaptability varies greatly. Due to the decision's small size, it can be made more rapidly.

Disadvantages

*   • Approval: The procedure is less flexible because it has the support of all the members. Since one or more entities are engaging, there might be differences in the vision of interest.
*   • Transparency: The company could be hacked if it becomes corrupt. Organizations may hide information from users.
*   • Vulnerability: This blockchain is more likely to be vulnerable if a few nodes are compromised.

BLOCKCHAIN AND BITCOIN TRANSACTIONS

A bitcoin transaction is the transfer of bitcoin from one address to another. For the transaction to be regarded legitimate, the sender must sign it.

Accounts do not exist with Bitcoin. Instead, Bitcoins of any size are all linked to a specific address that is managed by the bitcoin's owner. They are referred to as Unspent Transaction Outputs (UTXOs).

Each Bitcoin transaction is published to me pool, where it is treated as “pending.” A transaction is regarded as confirmed once it has been included in a block by a miner ([Evans, 2014](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#r11)).

BLOCKCHAIN'S EFFECTS ON THE WORLD ECONOMY AND ITS ECONOMIC BENEFITS

Ledger is a distributed technology where blockchain treated as a protocol that allows the money transmission, assets, and information in secure internet without middleman, such as a bank The integrity and authenticity of private documents and records, including those pertaining to voting, contracts, wills, patents, drivers' licences, passports, visas, and medical data, can also be preserved using a blockchain.

Digital blockchain using as a registry can be used to track, confirm the ownership, and transfer, of assets, including ownership of homes, cars, stocks, bonds, mortgages, and insurance, among other things. Financial and governmental services that are specifically adapted to each person's needs may be more effective in a network economy with asset transfers based on blockchain technology. Numerous routine transactions involving funds, assets, and paperwork could begin using blockchain technology. With the use of cryptographic security algorithms, many regular financial, assets, and with the use of blockchain technology, document processes could begin to be conducted on digital networks ([Melanie, 2015](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#r13)).

The phrase “Blockchain economy” is the substitution of for traditional national hard currency and outdated legacy ledger systems, there are cryptocurrencies and digital ledger systems rather than using conventional software application programs to administer existing national currencies blockchain and other similar technologies have become the norm for financial management. Additionally, In a scenario and likely future setting called the blockchain economy, cryptocurrencies would displace the world's existing monetary systems.

The insurance industry, the financial sector, and specifically the design of its financial markets industry have benefited greatly from blockchain technology. The principles of the new decentralized transactional model made possible by blockchain technology, which is based on a White paper on peer-to-peer communication by Satoshi, is at the center of this enthusiasm for the technology. It is generally acknowledged that the distributed ledger technology (blockchain technology computational)'s design opens up an extensive range of probable applications. For example, Peer-to-peer transactions can be made easier by using an immutable distributed ledger, however, payments also storing records, tracking physical things, and transferring charges via smart contracts are deprived of the need for an intermediary or human reconciliation (Reijers, 2016).

ROLE OF BLOCKCHAIN IN BUSINESS AND INDUSTRIAL ORGANIZATIONS

Blockchain applications in the realm of business. In many international industries, blockchain is contributing to the change of companies. Trust builds relationships and reduces effort duplication, increasing efficiency. Many industries are being transformed by the blockchain, including supply chains, food delivery, financial services, government, and retail.

Blockchain helps multi-step transactions that call for such functions to be checked and followed. It can speed up data transmission procedures, protect transactions, and lower compliance expenses. Blockchain technology can verify a product's provenance and streamline contract management ([Barde, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#r3)).

Blockchain technology is becoming more widely available across businesses thanks to its capacity for data organization. By creating a shared, digital record for your firm, blockchain allows employees to see how the business operates. Using blockchain technology, companies may make and receive payments. using “smart contracts,” which are computer-based rule sets that are encoded in code and saved on a blockchain. When a preset condition is satisfied, the smart contract automatically initiates the following relevant activity.

More generally, blockchain reduces expenses for businesses by handling the processes that it can perform without the need for intermediaries like suppliers and third-party providers. Among other things, the particular characteristics of blockchain can benefit enterprises by boosting trust, security, and transparency.

THE BENEFITS OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNIQUES THAT MAKE THE BUSINESS MORE SECURE

Decentralized Structure

Blockchain actually proves to be effective, when there isn't a single party that enables trust, in Daniel Field's opinion, when there isn't a central party that enables trust, the UST blockchain's director Consequently, as a leading provider of digital technologies and services, blockchain permits for data sharing among a variety of businesses in an ecosystem where no single entity has complete control.

It also helps to build trust amongst participants who might not otherwise be able to do so because of their lack of familiarity. The supply chain is a prime illustration: Nobody is responsible for organizing all of that information sharing, though. Information from other companies in the chain is needed or desired by a variety of organizations, including producers, distributors, retailers, suppliers, and transportation companies. The blockchain's decentralized organization solves that issue ([Wolfson, 2015](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#r15)).

Trust

Blockchain creates where it is either absent or untested, there is faith among many entities. These organizations are therefore willing to conduct business in ways that they might not have otherwise or that would have required an intermediary, such as transactions or the sharing of data. Facilitating trust is one of the most commonly cited benefits of blockchain. Early blockchain use cases that made it easier for companies without a direct link to trade while still needing to communicate data or make payments serve as examples of its usefulness. The most basic illustrations of how blockchain promotes trust between strangers are bitcoin and cryptocurrencies in general.

Enhanced Privacy and Security

The security of systems that employ blockchain technology is another significant benefit of this new technology. Due to the way that blockchain technology works, which results in greater security, fraud and undesirable behavior are prevented by creating an unalterable record of transactions protected from beginning to conclusion. The blockchain uses a grid of computers to store data, creating it virtually difficult to hack (unlike conventional computer systems that store data together in servers). Furthermore, by anonymizing data and requiring permissions to limit access, blockchain can better handle privacy concerns than traditional computer systems.

Costs Reduction

Blockchain's characteristics cannister enable businesses to save money. Transaction processing becomes more efficient. Additionally, it streamlines auditing and reporting procedures and eliminates manual activities like data collection and update. According to experts, blockchain's capacity to streamline clearing and settlement directly translates into decreases in process costs. They also cited financial institutions' savings as proof of the technology's success. More generally, blockchain reduces expenses for businesses by handling the processes that it is capable of performing without the need for intermediaries like suppliers and third-party providers.

Speed

Since there are no intermediaries and no longer any human procedures involved in businesses, blockchain executes transactions considerably quicker than conventional techniques. On rare occasions, blockchain enables transactions to be finished in a few seconds or less. Each data block's size and network activity are only two examples of the many factors that might a blockchain-based system's ability to process transactions swiftly. However, researchers have discovered that blockchain frequently beats conventional methods and technology in terms of speed.

In one of the most well-known blockchain applications, Walmart utilized the technology to quickly identify the supplier of mango slices, a procedure that had previously required seven days.

Visibility and Traceability

Beyond only speed, blockchain technology allows Walmart to suggested source of the other products. Thanks to this, Walmart likes retailer that can manage their inventories, responses or questions that confirm the origin of their products. If a retailer using blockchain needs to recall some of its items due to contamination, it may identify and remove the food that comes from that farm while keeping the other products from that farm on the shelves. According to experts, blockchain can help track the pedigrees of a variety of commodities, such as medications, in order to ensure that they are authentic and not counterfeit, and organic products, in order to ensure that they are organic.

Immutableness

Immutability basically means that once a transaction is put to a blockchain, it cannot be changed or deleted. On the blockchain, every transaction has a timestamp and a date stamp that creates a permanent record. Therefore, blockchain can be used to track data across time, enabling a secure, reliable audit of data. Omar cited This value is demonstrated by Sweden's usage of blockchain to track property titles as they are transferred and digitalize real estate transactions.

Individual Control of Data

Blockchain, according to experts, offers unparalleled levels of personal sovereignty over one's own digital data. According to Michela Minting, research director at ABI Research, in a world where data is a valuable commodity, technology inherently secures the data that is yours while giving you control over it.

Individuals and small groups of organizations can decide which parts of their digital data they want to share, with whom, and for how long, subject to restrictions set by blockchain-enabled smart contracts.

Tokenization

The process of tokenization includes transforming an item's worth, it may be physical or digital, token that is then saved on a blockchain and distributed through it. other virtual materials such as digital artwork have had success with tokenization, but the concept has broader applications that might simplify corporate transactions, according to Joe Davey, head of technology at international consultancy firm West Monroe, explains that this process entails turning the conversion of a physical or digital object's value into a digital token. Utility companies might employ carbon emission trading through tokenization permits with a carbon cap schemes. The traded carbon emission permits are then stored on blockchains and made available for sharing.

Innovation

Blockchain-based systems are being tested and implemented by leaders in a range of industries to address intractable problems and improve antiquated, time-consuming processes. Field offered the blockchain uses to validate the data on resumes supplied by trade searchers as an illustration of such revolution. Studies show that many people fake hiring managers the time-consuming chore of manually verifying the material on their applications. However, pilot projects that allow participating colleges to deposit information regarding their graduates' degrees and certifications on the blockchain, then provide approved hiring managers access to that data help to find Finding the truth will lead to solutions for both challenges and doing so quickly and effectively.

BLOCKCHAIN IMPLEMENTATION INCLUDES THESE STEPS

Because blockchain is completely decentralized, servers or machines that have nodes can manage the network. The blockchain network is under the control of just one stakeholder. By allowing the stakeholder to either add to the blockchain or view it, the blockchain can be completely permissionless if the stakeholder so desires. By limiting changes and viewing options, they can also grant access to blockchains.

To implement blockchain, you must take the following actions:

Identify a Use-Case

Because blockchain is a disruptive technology, enterprise customers need to be aware of its special strengths as well as the challenges and requirements associated with its implementation. Implementing blockchain requires a lifetime commitment, but it is worthwhile. Considering use cases with an open mind might completely alter the game as blockchain alters how it works.

The following are a few crucial use cases to consider:

*   • Secure: A blockchain does not connect any of the cited parties. It is perfect for them that cryptography makes it simpler for participants with and without permission to view the data.
*   • Shared and Replicated: All ledger data, including transactional and data-driver data, is effortlessly shared throughout the network. Participants in blockchain are also given a copy of their ledger.
*   • Immutable: Once the data is uploaded to the blocks, it cannot be changed or erased; it is irreversible. With blockchain, a record-keeping system can be attached. Verifying an audit is also a crucial use case for blockchain networks, concurrently.
*   • Verifiable: The blockchain connects all crucial corporate regulations to monitor network transactions. When business rules are implemented, the trades are also approved across the entire network. A mining derivative called Proof of Work (PoW) chooses endorsements constantly.

Enterprises must select the use cases that best suit their needs.

Developing a Proof of Concept (POC)

The blockchain's revolutionary Proof of Concept (POC) approach checks how well real-world applications are used in practice. PoC evaluates whether a project is practical and able to complete a task. Creating a solid PoC will naturally lead to automation and streamlining intermediary and repeated actions. Compared to other technologies, the blockchain Proof of Concept offers end users and customers a lot more value.

Today, numerous sectors use blockchain in their everyday work and rely on PoCs for best performance. Among the industries where POCs have completely changed how things are done are Telecom Management and FinTech KYC Insurance, among others.

Choosing the Blockchain Carefully

Organizations and corporations form a false belief when given the option of using blockchain. Enterprise users frequently believe that blockchain is the all-encompassing solution to their problems. It is undeniable that blockchain technology has the power to fundamentally alter how organizations operate. Before they decide that blockchain isn't the best option, it is crucial to carefully consider it.

Although it isn't a universal technology, blockchain adds value for companies. When selecting a blockchain network, take into account the following actions:

*   • Analyze whether blockchain technology is a good fit for the business's challenges.
*   • Pick a strategy for integrating blockchain.
*   • Consider some existing solutions, no matter how difficult they may sound.

The following are the names of the most advanced blockchain platforms:

*   • Stellar Hyperledger Fabric Corda Multichain Quorum Ethereum Unlock chain.

Businesses that conduct appropriate research can learn how to avoid being duped by marketing strategies. When deciding whether to rely on a budget, it was established that the technology was available. Blockchain can be either open or closed, permissionless or granted, centralized or decentralized. You will never be let down by blockchain's quickness or dynamism.

Initializing the Blockchain

Initializing blockchain requires the first-ever block to come into being curated manually. Remember, the block should comprise all the characteristics of the said chain. Then the features are shared through network nodes. Moreover, a JSON format file is created for defining the block. There are numerous parameters like Nonce, and Timestamp, which have to be specific. When the JSON file is further filled in, businesses could create folders that comprise the blockchain and the initialization.

Choosing the Right Consensus Protocol

Distributed systems utilize consensus methods to make sure that every node in the system agrees on a specific value or state. A few of the key comparisons between the various consensus protocols are as follows:

*   • Proof of Work (PoW) vs. Byzantine Fault Tolerance (BFT): BFT and PoW are two of the most used consensus techniques. PoW is often utilised in networks without permission, whereas BFT is typically used in networks with permission. Unlike PoW, which relies on a large number of nodes, BFT relies on a limited number of trusted nodes to validate transactions. Blocks must be mined using a lot of computational power in PoW, whereas BFT does not.
*   • Proof of Work (PoW) as opposed to Proof of Stake (PoS): In networks without permission, PoS and PoW are both employed. PoW needs nodes to execute a particular amount of computational labour in order to validate transactions, whereas PoS requires nodes to stake their own coin as collateral. PoS offers a lower entry barrier and is more energy-efficient than PoW.
*   • Raft vs. Practical Byzantine Fault Tolerance (PBFT): BFT-based consensus systems used in permissioned networks include PBFT and Raft. In comparison to Raft, PBFT is more fault-tolerant and depends on a higher number of nodes to validate transactions. Compared to PBFT, Raft is simpler to use and comprehend.
*   • The Difference Between Delegated Proof of Stake (DPoS) and PoS: Is that DPoS uses a smaller group of nodes—referred to as delegates—to validate transactions. The network chooses the delegates, and the more cryptocurrency a node invests, the more voting power it has. Although DPoS is less decentralised than PoS, it is faster.
*   • Federated Byzantine Agreement (FBA) in Comparison to Proof of Stake (PoS): FBA is a consensus technique used in permissioned networks that depends on a group of nodes that trust one another to confirm transactions. Although FBA requires greater node trust than PoS, it is more fault tolerant.

Generally, the choice of consensus protocol is determined by the unique needs of the network, including the required degree of decentralization, network size, and desired amount of fault tolerance.

Table 1. Major comparisons of consensus protocols

| Consensus Protocol || Type || Trust Model || Energy Efficiency || Speed || Decentralization || Fault Tolerance || Example Use Cases |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Byzantine Fault Tolerance (BFT) || Permissioned || Small set of trusted nodes || High || Medium || High || High || Financial systems, government networks |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Proof of Work (PoW) || Permission-less || Majority of nodes || Low || Medium || Low || High || Cryptocurrency networks |
| Proof of Stake (PoS) || Permission-less || Staked nodes || High || Fast || Medium || High || Cryptocurrency networks |
| Practical Byzantine Fault Tolerance (PBFT) || Permissioned || Larger set of trusted nodes || High || Medium || High || Very High || Financial systems, government networks |
| Raft || Permissioned || Leader-based || High || Fast || Medium || Medium || Distributed databases, cloud systems |

The blockchain allows the first member of the network to produce the answer to write the following block. Additionally, this enables routine writing of the blocks. The consensus process also guards against hacking and spam attacks.

Designing Deliberately

Blockchain architecture demands meticulous curation. The only goal of integrating blockchain technology into business is to enable it to address any problem that may arise in any organization, regardless of its size.

Additionally, blockchain must get along with the established procedures. Further analysis would be necessary for a blockchain that is incredibly powerful yet unable to satisfy the conditions and criteria. Additionally, the evaluation would choose the basic framework for blockchain implementation.

Navigating Uncertainty

Since the 1980s, blockchain has been around. However, before the introduction of Bitcoin, the technology never really gained traction. Blockchain is subject to a number of rules because it is essentially connected to cryptocurrencies.

In the near future, regulatory practices will change. Organizations must keep track of the regulatory framework's constant change because of the regulations. Blockchain would need to be shaped by organizations as well. For integrating blockchain, several nations have different rules and procedures.

Businesses and organizations should cooperate with regulators. The partnership will help to improve how blockchain is used all around.

EXAMPLES AND USES OF BLOCKCHAIN

Before you begin to implement blockchain, don’t you want to see some blockchain implementation examples that will inspire you? Let's have an overview of different industries.

Banking

The most well-known industry to uses blockchain at its heart is banking. The crucial component of the financial vertical is security. Therefore, the use of blockchain to incorporate security into banking could not only transform banking but also cause a paradigm shift in the industry as a whole.

*   1\. Improved International Payments: The banking and financial systems depend on payments in one way or another. Global commercial and central banks now have access to the latest payment processing technologies thanks to blockchain. The current trend also makes it possible to make cross-border transfers without using Western Union or Swift.
*   2\. Stock Share and Exchange Prior to Blockchain: Third parties were involved in the purchase and sale of stocks and shares. Blockchain trading will cut down on information redundancy in general. Additionally, it would raise performance as a whole. Blockchain gives both trading parties the ability to conduct smaller transactions outside of the blockchain. The blockchain will only contain records of the final transactions. The best part is that no additional steps are necessary.
*   3\. Trade Finance: The pursuit of money combines international trade and business (not only the stock exchange). Even though the modern world mainly relies on technology, economic operations still involve a lot of paperwork, which results in bills, credit letters, and invoices, among other things. Blockchain-backed trade finance has the potential to completely automate the trading process. It eliminates the burdensome paperwork and bureaucracy.
*   4\. Identity Checking: Digital verification is necessary for online transactions to be completed. Additionally, there are many processes in digital verification, including: 
    *   • In-Person Verification: Banking client authentication to confirm individuals' identities
*   5\. Bookkeeping, Accounting, and Auditing Blockchain: Uses standardization to enable automated data verification by auditors. The method saves time and lowers total expenditures. Additionally, the general integrity of electronic files can also be determined by simply using blockchain. The methods will help the hash string, which further serves as the digital fingerprint of the file. By writing directly into the blockchain, it also aids in producing an accurate timestamp. In terms of finance, blockchain use cases also include: • Credit Reports for both individuals and corporations Hedge funds, Syndicated Lending, P2P or Peer-to-Peer Transfers, Crowdfunding, or Initial Coin Offerings.

Insurance

Approximately 80% of insurance businesses globally claim to have either adopted blockchain technology or have plans to do so soon. Numerous insurance businesses have either started embracing blockchain technology or have intentions to do so for the better part of the past two years. Due to the Proof-of-Concept stage, several blockchain insurance projects are giving it a shot. In addition, a lot of businesses decide to work together or develop alliances with B3i (Blockchain Insurance Industry Initiative).

The alliances are ground-breaking, and the blockchain-based platforms leverage the following use cases:

Stop Fraud and Abuse

Insurance companies lose a lot of money as a result of fraudulent activity. It is expensive and difficult to identify fraudulent activity involving data from both public and private sources. There is a possibility that data may occasionally be found in fragments due.
