Management That Is Compartmentalized

Selling and managing insurance policies requires a significant amount of labor. The insurance industry must also deal with an intense rivalry, bureaucracy, and traditional methods.

Blockchain's cutting-edge ledger and intelligent contracts are combined to enable seamless processing. A single trustworthy technology can help the payout procedure cut down on time-consuming delays.

Most significantly, blockchain is changing the foundation of the insurance sector. It promotes the need for ongoing innovation and helps people break free from strange traditions. Customers need the cost flexibility, speed, and transparency that blockchain can deliver.

Healthcare

Healthcare is the most undervalued sector in the global economy, although it outperforms all other verticals in terms of revenue generation. Everyone is now aware of how crucial it is to have a strong healthcare facility thanks to historically difficult periods like COVID-19. Blockchain opens up fresh possibilities in the health sector. For instance, it makes sure that patient data is stored. Blocks can be used to transport electronic health records for storage purposes. All of the information from tens of thousands of patients can be kept in the ledger. The technique also permits complete information duplication and storage with excellent encryption as a backup.

By making the replacement, any prospective DDoS attacks are further eliminated from precedent. Hackers cannot modify or delete health records using blockchain.

Blockchain Tech in Healthcare Can Initiate

Drug Monitoring

Blockchain technology can significantly reduce the problem of product counterfeiting. In some networks, transactions between distributors and manufacturers were simple to track. The system would make it simple to verify authenticity, track momentum through time, and quickly learn about makers.

Electronic Health Records

The single-most patient registry is considerably created by blockchain. Higher security standards, dependable operation, and remote accessibility are all provided by EHR. Patients typically worry about data synchronization, but the infrastructure makes it possible. Once patients give their consent, doctors can easily evaluate the whole medical history using blockchain. The strategy would drastically lower clinic hardware expenditures. As soon as data is updated in real-time, processing will cease. Doctors will be able to quickly learn specifics about blood types, allergic reactions, and chronic conditions.

Standardization

Blockchain technology enables the use of EHR and conventional protocols. Additionally, blockchain greatly facilitates the establishment of global standards for patients.

Blockchain technology might enable efficient operation at what appears to be a minimal cost in healthcare and medical facilities.

Real Estate

A significant part of global income and assets come from real estate. Real estate is a significant means to earn an income due to the transactional nature of the industry. Independent networks are now widely used in the real estate industry. Blockchain offers advantages like tokenizing real estate assets; processing industry fundamental operations efficiently; reducing costs associated with automation processes; granting access to assets distributed globally; creating investor-specific pools due to the fractionalization of total ownership; gaining access to secondary market opportunities; and increasing data accessibility and transparency.

THE THEORY OF ELECTRONIC MARKETS

The Theory of electronic markets is based on the research done by economists Ronald Coase and Oliver Williamson, both of whom have won Nobel Prizes. Why do we see businesses in a free market economy, wondered Coase? One purported advantages of the free market economy is that, it “self-regulates,” but in practice, we frequently see situations when management directs economic activity rather than the economy acting on its own. In order to resolve this paradox, Coase pointed out that using the price system had cost, and that for particular types of economic activities, those costs were larger in the market than in a corporation ([Benson, 1989](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch012.xhtml#r7)).

As a result, we would anticipate seeing few economic activities occurring within enterprises and few other occurring within markets.

By imagining a make-or-buy choice, Williamson operationalizes Coase's concept. A company can either purchase a component from the market or manufacture it internally to employ in its own production process. Williamson provides a thorough explanation of the circumstances that could influence a decision to make or buy. For our instantaneous goals, it is crucial that private economic activity occurs either in the enterprises or in the hierarchical structure, i.e., firms. Transaction costs are a major factor in determining where that economic activity occurs.

Malone argues that computers and information technology have significantly lowered the cost of processing and transferring information. outlining three potential implications on industrial organization. An improvement in communication efficiency that has a beneficial influence on markets and hierarchies is the first definition of an “electronic communication effect.”

AN ORGANIZING ELEMENT

Any civilization had to deal with the economic issue of distributed information. Because of this, it was unknown which resources did society had access to, what further uses they may have, and what prices had to be established in the market since they were not just handed. This understanding was crucial in the context of the argument between socialist economists, particularly Oskar Lange and Ludwig von Mises, and F.A. Hayek, and that raged in the 1930s. In the absence of market pricing, opportunity costs, according to Mises, could not be determined, and resource allocation would therefore be inefficient. Lange had retorted that market prices might be either computed using econometric approaches or simulated through an accounting process. No central planner could ever have access to enough information to perform the computations Lange envisioned. This was Hayek's contribution to the discussion. Furthermore, Mises maintained that even if central planners had all the essential data, they would not be as motivated to make wise judgments as businesspeople. The socialist calculation controversy has now been settled, but Mises' and Hayek's arguments are still crucial. In 2003, What is the relation between decreasing communication costs over time and the economics of various decision-making structures? is Malone's restatement of the original hypothesis. Now he makes the case with respect to centralized vs. decentralized decision-makers, rather than advocating for a choice between hierarchies and markets. He outlines the elements that will influence the decision between decision-making frameworks, which is crucial for our objectives.

These three components are motivation, trust, and decision information.

He contends that IT can increase reliability through greater monitoring, likeness, and socialization of remote decision-makers.

According to Sundararajan, trust have five indicators in a digital environment which are: prior interactions, peer experience learning, blazing, digitised social capital, and external authentication. He also made the crucial argument that the later two indicators, digital social capital, and outside authentication Networked services which can offer these cues at scale have grown significantly and quickly.

BLOCKCHAIN AS A FOUNDATION FOR THE ECONOMY

Similar to how industrialized energy was distributed at scale during the industrial revolution, industrialized trust will be distributed at scale during the digital revolution.

Following the industrial revolution, the industrial organization saw a shift from U-form organization to the M-Form organization, according to Chandler. We envision the establishment of a “V-form” organization with the industrialization of trust (Allen, 2016).

Organization in V-Form

The V-form organization will be made up of number of completely autonomous businesses that work together as if they were a single vertically integrated company, using blockchain technology to coordinate and audit tasks that were previously handled by the corporate office or by the market. A future digital economy would have a trading infrastructure provided by these V-form companies. Currently, economic infrastructure includes not only the legal framework and social norms that support commerce and economic activity, but also the physical infrastructures like ports, highways, rails, and other similar facilities. It is simple to envision an additional digital (blockchain) layer of economic infrastructure that both supplements and replaces current forms of economic infrastructure in a world where trust has been industrialized (Allen, 2011).

A future digital economy would have a trading infrastructure provided by these V-form companies. Currently, economic infrastructure includes not only the legal framework and social norms that support commerce and economic activity, but also the physical infrastructures like ports, highways, rails, and other similar facilities. It is simple to envision an additional digital (blockchain) layer of economic infrastructure that both supplements and replaces current forms of economic infrastructure in a world where trust has been industrialized. The Aragon Network is an example of a V-form organization; virtual organizations can be built as a collection of smart contracts, and the network offers a mechanism for resolving disputes. This organization can be characterized as decentralized and fully congruent with the concept of the electronic market.

On the other hand, “centralized” V-form organizations are also visible. An example would be the Tradelines platform that IBM and Maersk created. Here, traditional hierarchical organizations have given way to a V-form organizational structure.

BLOCKCHAIN ANARCHY AND PRIVATE GOVERNANCE

Through DAOs, A blockchain-based economic system can automate and carry out smart contracts entirely, however there is still a problem with contractual enforcement, especially given that these voluntary contracts may not be approved by a centralized authority and are thus not always carried out in secret. Noting that private agents may desire property of not being observable by the government may be the source of the efficient advantage, since it would allow one to avoid government predation in relation to taxes, regulations, or other constraints. This is comparable to instances where private ordering principles emerge on their own in anarchic or shadowy economies.

CONCLUSION

According to Malone, a dramatic rearrangement of the industrial organization will result from information technology commencing in the late 1980s. Costs of communication have decreased. Viable business models started to appear in the second half of the 20th and the first decade of the 21st centuries. The major internet platforms we use today were founded at that time. This is an example of the electronic brokerage effect. We still haven't seen the electrical integration effect in operation on a significant scale. The electronic markets theory is based on the cost of trust, not a general decrease in communication costs. We might infer that blockchain technology offers the structure for digital.

# Chapter 13

*   Ashish Kumbhare  
    ICFAI University, Raipur, India
*   Piyush Kumar Thakur  
    ICFAI University, Raipur, India
*   B. Ravi Kiran Patnaik  
    ICFAI University, Raipur, India
*   Kiran Midiyam  
    ICFAI University, Raipur, India

ABSTRACT

Services for online storage have expanded quickly with the quick rise in cloud computing technology. The fact that online storage servers are insufficient to properly store and retain data has drawn specific attention in relation to such distant storage solutions, which significantly undermines consumers' trust in acquiring and using cloud storage solutions. The centralised nature of conventional information integrity auditing methodologies for cloud-based storage creates significant security concerns owing to only a few failure points and server flaws. Customers constantly have good standards for the integrity and protection of personal data. This crucial guarantee of data integrity is provided by blockchain technology thanks to its advanced security capabilities. Integrity of data, privacy protection, accessibility of the information, security, and effectiveness are all guaranteed by the blockchain solution's cryptography encryption, which shields data from intruders, thwarts fraudulent activity and exploitation, and lessens the likelihood that information will be stolen or damaged.

INTRODUCTION

Data integrity is the quality that the information utilized in a solution is accurate, trustworthy, and beneficial to all parties. The term “data integrity” is used in this context to refer to the completeness, timeliness, and correctness of the data throughout its entire lifespan as well as a resistance to unintentional data alteration, which is a broad concept that is widely used in the supply-chain business. Today, data is a valuable resource. In a wide range of industries, from finance and insurance to health, education, and public administration, strategy should inform all corporate decisions. Trusting data has therefore become essential as more and more human activities helped by computers rely on it (Boureanu et al., 2020). The importance of data has also made it a prime target for cyber-attacks that seek to undermine the key CIA qualities (Confidentiality, Integrity, and Availability) that data must possess in order to be trusted.

| Figure 1. Prime target for cyber-attacks |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.f01](i/ch013.f01.png) |
| --- |

Globally, data security has grown to be a serious problem. There are many data threats out there. These security risks, which might take the shape of flaws, hacks, data breaches, insider threats, staff mistakes, etc., certainly exist, but they must be minimized to prevent harm. Although it might be difficult to stop the risks, they can actually be reduced. Depending on which CIA properties are attacked online, distinct data trust impairments result. In particular, destroying availability only temporarily prohibits data from being obtained; however, processes can be restarted as soon as data are once again accessible. While breaching confidentiality results in the disclosure of private data that cannot be undone, the original data are still accessible and usable, at least to the extent permitted by the damage that was caused (i.e., an organization victim of data leakage may have to face economic consequences) ([Hasanat et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r18)). In contrast, altering data integrity is a seriously harmful attack that almost always creates serious problems for data trust. In fact, tampering with data can go unnoticed and fuel malevolent operations by removing individual entries (i.e., to erase bothersome traces) or altering specific chunks of data (i.e., to change the behavior of data consumers). When integrity is breached, unlike with confidentiality and availability, the original data cannot be recovered and is permanently destroyed. Integrity attacks are therefore difficult to detect and highly effective ([Bumblauskas et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r7)).

Data integrity problems are made worse in cloud computing environments because data owners have no control over how, where, and by whom their data are accessed. However, a growing number of both public and private organizations are outsourcing their data because “It reduces the expense of local data storage as well as the weight of maintenance costs.” Therefore, it has become necessary to address the issue of assuring data integrity features in cloud computing systems.

ANALYSES OF CURRENT METHODS FOR DATA INTEGRITY ASSURANCE

By computing checksum values and comparing them to reference values and methods based on cryptographic techniques, such as key and keyless hashing, means of electronic signature, there are various known ways to ensure data integrity. The drawback of these approaches is the inability to guarantee their integrity without adding an additional mechanism for data recovery. The use of several sorts of reservations employing RAID technology (Redundant Array of Independent Disks) (RAID arrays) implemented in hardware or software, duplication techniques, and redundant coding techniques are further ways to guarantee the integrity of data. These techniques' significant redundancy is a drawback ([Cristofaro, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r11)).

Request for Cryptographic Approach

Data integrity is typically ensured by employing both proper data replication mechanisms and cryptographic technologies (such as digests and asymmetric keys). In fact, individual pieces of data are signed using cryptographic methods so that any attempt at forging may be quickly identified through the verification of cryptographic signatures. In fact, for an attack to be successful, the secret keys must be compromised in order to change data signatures and get around the cryptographic integrity tests. Although these attacks are difficult to execute, once they are, they are essentially undetectable. In order to guarantee data integrity in any case, it is strongly advised to utilize appropriate data replication mechanisms.

Data replication and distribution across a number of nodes severely hinder data integrity violations since an attacker would have to compromise every piece of duplicated data without being noticed. This replication strategy is commonly used in real-world settings, such as cloud computing systems with their wealth of dispersed storage resources. Although replication undoubtedly makes it more difficult for an attack to succeed in a cloud environment, cloud providers themselves are sometimes complicit in data integrity violations ([Garaus & Treiblmaier, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r14)).

It reduces the possibility of data manipulation and encourages data confidentiality thanks to its cryptography and encryption strength. Users of cloud systems frequently believe that the technology is safe enough to encrypt data before sending it to the cloud. Although it was believed that encryption might offer strong secrecy against internal attacks, it does not guard against data corruption brought on by incorrect configuration and software faults. In order to conserve bandwidth and computing power, a technique for checking data integrity was needed. Remote data auditing has recently attracted more attention in order to confirm the data accuracy or integrity. Users of the data system define data integrity as wholeness, the state of not being damaged, altered, or impaired. Additionally, data integrity refers to information that hasn't been altered by a hacker or other unauthorized invader. Data integrity is the quality of being undamaged, meaning that it has not been altered by a hacker or other unauthorized intruder. Additionally, it refers pristine data that has not been harmed by a system flaw or other shortcoming. Human error, data manipulation, system failure, and environmental events can all lead to data corruption and system data compromises. Methods and procedures for data assurance should be used by organizations and system users who depend on data integrity. Data governance and integrity are essential components of any data management service ([Goldberg et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r15)). Consumers who use reliable data can be satisfied that their personal information is secure, that their data is confidential, that their data is accurate, and that their data is used to achieve appropriate planning and decision-making objectives.

Without a strong infrastructure for detection, it might be tough to spot unintentional or scientific fraud. The integrity of cloud data has become a crucial concern with the widespread use of cloud storage. There have been various methods suggested, however they all have drawbacks. User data is centrally collected and kept on the cloud, where a variety of applications are implemented. Cloud computing provides users with sharing and storage resources through a range of deployment methods, including private cloud, community cloud, public cloud, and hybrid cloud. Many advantages come from storing data on the cloud, including lower system investment costs, less local storage requirements, and support for remote access. Cloud storage does, however, provide equivalent difficulties. User data is vulnerable to a variety of dangers because of highly centralized data and sophisticated computing settings. The likelihood of an assault on the cloud increases due to the complexity of the systems' many components. The vulnerabilities get worse as complexity rises. Additionally, the multitenant sharing capability of cloud computing services increases the risk of data loss. Finally, cloud service providers might purposefully conceal user data or keep information that customers hardly ever access offline ([Statista, 2021a](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r48)). Verifying outsourcing data integrity has become a crucial issue in cloud storage in light of these difficulties.

Request for Third-Party Auditor

Although encryption offers strong protection from internal threats, it does not shield data from damage brought on by incorrect configuration and software flaws. Third-party auditing is a fundamentally established method of demonstrating the integrity of data outsourced to a remote server. A third party may examine the accuracy of the data. The Third-Party Auditor (TPA) program employs a person with the training and experience necessary to perform all data integrity auditing procedures. The architecture of the TPA scheme achieves data integrity and provides reassurances regarding data security to the data owner. The strategy maintains data integrity for all owner resources in the cloud and involves the data owner in the auditing process. The requirement for a third-party communication channel and the exposure to man-in-the-middle attacks are drawbacks of this data integrity technique. The introduction of a third party for data processing raises the possibility of implementing a number of hacker threats. One of the hot themes is how to safely and effectively store massive amounts of data on the cloud with data integrity verification and assurance ([Statista, 2021b](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r49)). Utilizing modern technology replaces the conventional methods of data integrity verification that relied on trusted Third-Party Auditors (TPAs) and encryption to protect data in the cloud.

DATA INTEGRITY IN BUSINESS

The worry of prying into private lives is made worse by advances in networking and computing. Sniffers and cookies are used to capture data as it is being transmitted, which raises serious privacy concerns when used to monitor user browsing and shopping habits. All of these anonymous data mining violate people's online privacy, even if they might not necessarily cause customers any harm. On this subject, Mayor Schonberger outlines the many phases of drafting regulations pertaining to the right to privacy and characterizes the way privacy is currently understood in cyberspace as the right to information self-determination. At this point, the person can choose how they want to take part in the gathering, handling, and use of their personal data.

The importance of information in the global economy is growing as a result of new technologies, more data collection, shifting market trends, and the creation of a new worldwide market for electronic commerce. Due to this, knowledge in particular has grown to be a valuable resource that can lead to the creation of jobs, enterprises, and consumer services ([Stix, 2021](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r50)). As a result, these forces have increased the pressure to gather, keep, process, and use personal data. These elements have also decreased the amount of privacy, and in such a setting, customer confidence is inadequate.

Blockchain and Online Shopping

It required many years for the online world to transform from a system which was mainly use in communicating inside and between defense and academic organisations into a platform which could store as well as run business applications. Nonetheless, it only took a few more years after the World Wide Web was launched for commercial websites to flourish and e-commerce to become a widely used business model ([Stoll et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r51)).

One of the early reviews of e-commerce divides the field into three categories: (a) applications (such as marketing, payment, and inter-organizational systems); (b) technological obstacles (such safety, connectivity, and assistance systems); and (c) assist and deployment (e.g., corporate strategy, governmental policy). Later e-commerce review papers have concentrated on issues like consumer relationship trust building, e-commerce in particular geographic locations, research on online consumer behavior, recommendation systems, and reference architectures. These models are intriguing to both researchers and practitioners since they provide guidelines for developing potential application as well as assist identify factors that can encourage the general adoption of e-commerce. They aid in identifying the main e-commerce success elements as well.

Numerous academic publications thoroughly research the origins of prosperous e-commerce. Some provide examples of how information technologies have altered how e-commerce looks through the business network-based value creation and come to the conclusion that the sector underwent a digital transition ([Gramoli, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r16)). Uncertainty in the market and in technology actually has a moderating effect on performance outcomes. The regulatory environment, business size, power inside the supply chain, which also covers supplier power, and the mechanisms for e-government compliance are included to the extended model as new antecedents ([Iinuma, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r20); [Iman, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r21)).

Features of Blockchain Technology

There are three ways to define blockchain. It is a repository that maintains a publicly auditable shared ledger. It is a peer-to-peer transfer network for business transactions that allows users to transfer assets, value, and payments without the need for middlemen. Legally speaking, it validates transactions and takes the place of previously trusted entities. The word “DLT” refers to a group of technologies that share data among numerous nodes in either private or public ledgers. So-called trustless systems really reduce the amount of trust required from any one participant rather than completely eliminating the requirement for confidence. This is accomplished by offering different forms of cooperation incentives to actors that encourage actions that benefit the system as a whole. The audience must be conscious, however, that such a name does not precisely delimit other terms but rather reflects the term's common usage. With the use of blockchain technology, users can maintain a shared database without the requirement for a reliable central authority or shared confidence, allowing any participant to join or leave the system at any moment ([Jevans et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r22)). Techniques that cryptographically link several activity sets to each other establish the chronological sequence of time-stamped data. The sequence of blocks is provided both order and integrity by employing a hash reference to link every block with the one that came before this.

The first application of these ideas was the decentralised online payment system known as Bitcoin, which is based on a particular distributed ledger known as blockchain. The allocation of each and every Bitcoin now in existence to unique cryptographic identities is part of the whole transaction history included in the Bitcoin blockchain. In the years that followed, a number of alternative systems were created, following the lead of Bitcoin, expanding the potential applications beyond virtual assets transactions. Blockchain platforms including Ethereum, for example, allow users to save both transactional and public computer code by modifying the data structure and techniques to make it easier for public code to run ([Jonker, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r23)). Participants can create a series of operations in advance to be carried out under specific circumstances using this type of code. These programs, which go by the name of smart contracts, may have significant effects on all forms of e-commerce, which makes them particularly intriguing to automate contractual transactions (practically).

By design, these systems lack a central authority, and members themselves constantly update the common data structure. The mechanism propagates newly received entries that haven't yet been added to the blockchain by sending them to other users one at a time. Moreover, since users might not always get them in the same sequence as other network nodes and because single users can miss certain entries, users cannot just add untested entries to their individual replicas of the blockchain. In order to keep all copies of the blockchain consistent, participants must agree on the state of the chain through a decentralised majority voting process. This procedure is extremely difficult on blockchains since each user may create and utilise a staggering number of cryptographic identities (like in Bitcoin). Evil actors may easily control the majority vote, and hence the system. state if any of these cryptographic identities had a single vote during consensus mechanism. Contrary to the amount of cryptographic identities, other parameters that are not arbitrarily scalable are used to determine the voting weight of participants in order to prevent such manipulations. As a result, many blockchain systems demand that users who update the blockchain with new additions use increasingly more computational power or stake money in order to cast a vote. Other mechanisms exist, although as of this writing, they are employed less frequently ([Joshi, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r24)). The two most significant objectives of consensus procedures are (a) leadership selection and (b) rate restriction. The first makes sure that a primus inter pares is fairly chosen by the system and given the responsibility of briefly guiding the network toward a new, accepted, and shared state. The second makes sure that there is a cap on how quickly new leaders are chosen and, as a result, how quickly the blockchain's state changes. This reduces attacks and enhances fairness between people who might not be able to dedicate the same amount of time.

For instance, in the Bitcoin system, a participant must repeatedly carry out simple computing tasks in addition to validating the transactions that should be included in the block in order to compute a valid block ([Beck et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r5)). Through trial and error, each new block is slightly adjusted until a set of mathematical requirements is met. Due to the fact that users can only contribute The resultant chain represents the system's processing power consensus by having users add validated blocks to their own versions of the blockchain. As a result, it is rather resistant to efforts at manipulation since with each new block, the amount of computational power required to recalculate some portions of the blockchain grows rapidly. Users that contribute valid blocks towards this form of blockchain are compensated for using their processing power by receiving new virtual currency units and fees collected from customers whose transactions were recorded in the blockchain ([Biryukov & Tikhomirov, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r6)).

Blockchain can be divided into permissioned and permission-less chains, as well as public and private chains. These traits can be combined to create one of three main types of blockchain technology:

All nodes can read, submit, and write public permission-less chains transactions. Contrarily, in private permissioned chains, solely authorised Nodes have the ability to see, send, and modify transactions. These differences have a big impact on the blockchain's architecture, the amount of assurance required of each participant, and the work required to defend against malicious assaults. When using the blockchain to enable sharing Permissioned and private chains that don't rely on power-intensive consensus techniques may frequently be the better option for the exchange of information between businesses along a supply chain if the parties are acquainted with one another. Different blockchain types may be used simultaneously with relation to e-commerce. Companies might, for instance, use private, Regarding their supply networks, authorised chains, whereas enabling Bitcoin payments, which are based on a public, permission-less chain ([Kaminska, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r25)). The study objectives developed in the subsequent sections take into consideration this variation by integrating several blockchain technologies at a rather complex abstraction level. If a particular technology is more or less affected than others, it is mentioned in the conversation and emphasized in the framework.

Blockchain is a multi - dimensional construct that poses technical, legislative, administrative, quality, and consumer concerns that have an influence on e-commerce. It offers previously inconceivable technology capabilities, which creates new possibilities, but it also necessitates a rigorous evaluation of existing corporate practises, such as how they handle sensitive consumer data or how they create communication lines all along supply chain ([Kasahara & Kawahara, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r26)). The proper management of data, security and confidentiality challenges, systems design, deployment, and configuration, in addition to the possible impact of cutting-edge technologies like IoT, big data, cloud technology, AI, and machine-to-machine interaction, all are regarded as technological challenges. Problems with data collection, storage, and analysis are related to legal challenges, as are potential security breaches and the need to comply with laws referred to as “anti-money laundering” and “know your customer” standards (AML). Additional concerns relate to the legal compliance of blockchain-automated business operations and novel corporate structures, including, in the most extreme example, fully decentralized autonomous organizations (DAOs). Blockchain also creates new entry points to capital markets, many of which continue to operate illegally. Although the fundraising factor of this trend has received the most people's attention and resulted in an untenable bubble, this is only the initial step because the fundamental virtual assets were nearly always started to sell with the guarantee of usefulness and convenience with the projects' intrinsic e-commerce capitalist model. This implies that several virtual asset-based e-commerce systems, both connected and unconnected, may be on the increase ([Abramova & Bohme, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r1)).

Technical Problems

Blockchain's technological features are important catalysts for innovation. However, because there is still a great deal of uncertainty about the optimal way to go about constructing such systems, it is currently unknown how to best leverage on these qualities to generate business value. The current e-commerce study on these areas is briefly examined and assessed in the perspective of the advantages and hazards related with blockchain. In the sections that follow, we examine four critical areas of e-commerce: usability and availability, security and confidentiality, cutting-edge technology, and development process, as well as the advantages provided about by the blockchain for each case ([Abramova & Bohme, 2016](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r1)).

Effects of Blockchain on E-Accessibility Commerce and Traceability

Major success elements for e-commerce have previously been highlighted as being accessibility and traceability of data and payments. They propose a method that might generate a semi-automatic taxonomy collection of product information acquired from online stores and note that “e-commerce is among the areas where rising data traffic on the Online impair data accessibility.” As per ISO 9001:2000, traceability is the capability to trace an individual's development, usage, or existence along each connection in its supply chain. Advanced models were created in order to ensure information and payment traceability across the full supply chain.

Depending on how the blockchain system is technically implemented, It is used in e-commerce transactions to preserve buying data in an organised and persistent manner, that aids in data integrity and traceability. The trade may be priced in a fiat currency relies on the fundamental virtual asset, but it will likely be settled using market-to-market priced virtual assets ([Alabi, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r2)).

Effects of Blockchain on E-Commerce Security and Privacy

E-commerce systems are said to need two key components: privacy and security. The security measures in place have a significant impact on how much people trust web-based applications. Phishing websites, for example, and other fraudulent operations that steal users' private information, are damaging to the viability of online shopping as a whole. Mention how information technology systems, ethical business practises, physical architecture, and interconnected infrastructure are the key reasons of consumers' observed privacy and security vulnerabilities ([Li et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r31)). Privacy seals were once recommended as a way to address privacy concerns; however they have only been partially successful in doing so. If no privacy-enhancing measures are utilized, all parties can see transactions made on blockchains. Transactions on public blockchains are connected to fictitious identities. Enhanced security issues regarding (a) safe implementation, (b) unbiased execution, (c) secure interchange with several other networks, and (d) confidentiality would emerge if smart contracts were integrated to e-commerce. The development of consensus processes, chained storage, and sophisticated signing and validation systems using blockchain technology has opened the door to a number of new functionalities. Utilizing sensor information and safety traits including reliability, tamper-resistance, protection to Distributed Denial of Service and double-spending exploits, they combine privacy-preserving security measures. Blockchain technology does, however, also bring forth certain fresh hazards, such as the infrastructure being taken over by miners, difficulties with smart contracts, double spending assaults, or theft or loss of secret keys (Meiselwitz et al., 2019).

Effects of Combining Blockchain With Cutting-Edge Technologies

Numerous studies have identified the possibility of integrating cutting-edge information collection and analysis techniques into e-commerce uses, including the inclusion includes machine-to-machine (M2M) communication, the Internet of Things, big data analytics, cloud computing, and artificial intelligence (AI). The term “Internet of Things” in this sense describes the expanding network of real physical objects that are integrated with interconnected sensors and elements in order to link and interact with other devices and systems.

The goal of big data analytics is to extract value from rising data sets, which is tied to the proportional growth in information that can be accessed. Cloud computing, in general, is the instant accessibility of computational resources without continuous supervision by the user ([Liu & Li, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r33)).

To exchange essential data, an IoT architecture with three layers might be deployed (perception, network, and application layers). The client service provided by internet-based robots that employ an objective recognition model can be enhanced. Numerous speculative use cases have been generated by the marriage of blockchain and cutting-edge technology, including the insurance industry's usage of IoT and smart contracts as well as the ability to make payments without time-consuming administrative procedures. Be careful that blockchain technology can develop systems for compensating AI service providers for the development and application of special machine learning techniques in the medical industry, especially when it comes to the interpretation of radiological images and CT scans. Even though it is known that this subject has immense promise, in-depth study on how it influences e-commerce is still absent ([Peng et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r40)).

Use of Blockchain in System Development (E-Commerce Platforms)

On how to create and put into practice functional e-commerce architectures and system designs, specific instructions have been given. Create a B2C implementation framework, for instance, that incorporates multiple sub phases in addition to the conventional stages of Planning, choosing, analysing, designing, putting into practise, and managing systems.

For system engineers and developers, identifying and modelling the requirements for e-commerce solutions has been a significant problem. The requirement for B2C applications is determined by a goal-driven process that identifies vital functions, builds a use case model, evaluates the goals, and takes into account tradeoffs and options. The B2B e-commerce concept proposed by system engineers as a result of their investigation into four EDI efforts makes suggestions for the type of collaboration that should be conducted. It will be increasingly challenging for e-commerce enterprises to select the appropriate platforms and ensure cross-technological compatibility if more general-purpose and specialised blockchains are deployed. ([Pollock, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r42)). The emergence of new use cases necessitates the creation of foundations for deployment that are tailored specifically for blockchains, as well as new protocols and design patterns. The introduction of standardised programmable interfaces for smart contracts is forcing businesses to adopt standardised programmable interfaces and data exchange formats. Therefore, the goal of design-oriented research must be clearly stated, together with the characteristics of the underlying blockchain and the justification for choosing blockchain over a conventional database solution. In other words, the system design must intentionally take into account features of the blockchain, such as shared access and immutable data. The resulting applications must closely correspond with a company's overall strategy and operational procedures, including any legal considerations that are covered in the section that follows ([Rejeb et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r45)).

Legal Concerns

Blockchain, which in its early years was primarily a technical development, today offers a wealth of new possibilities that authorities must fully comprehend before deciding what legal adjustments should be made. Legislation in many nations is falling behind contemporary trends, similar to the beginning of e-commerce. This has to do with the regulations that control how personal data is handled, how money is exchanged for virtual assets, how smart contracts are used, and how autonomous organisations are starting to develop. Additionally, it is not yet apparent how compliance-related difficulties will be resolved. Our analysis identified five important legal domains where blockchain has the potential to affect: capital market access, conformity, organisational procedures and structures, information difficulties, and building blockchain-based e-commerce systems that are lawful ([Rejeb et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r45)).

Handling of Sensitive Data in E-Commerce

Since the inception of e-commerce, the fulfillment of data needs has been a topic of discussion. For instance, earlier studies looked into how artificial intelligence might affect the legal requirements for e-commerce and distance selling with regard to informational requirements. The protection of sensitive data and, by extension, customer privacy, is a serious concern for both customers and politicians. The majority of countries have laws that regulate various aspects of e-commerce, such as data collection, use, publication, preservation duration, security, and information needs. Regardless of the fact that the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) has established a common framework for the security of user information, various privacy regulations apply to the handling of personal information in blockchains. In the event that agreements are used to execute legal contracts, blockchain systems retain information on deals involving virtual assets as well as contractual agreements. Although most users in public blockchain systems that are currently in use utilize pseudonymous cryptographic identities, it is often still feasible to identify individuals using extra information. This raises the issue of how e-commerce companies that use blockchain technology can guarantee adherence to privacy regulations like the GDPR in the European Union. Data is made available to the public and may be accessed by anybody once it has been verified and added to a public blockchain. Applying key provisions of the GDPR to these technological advances is challenging, including the right to correction and deletion ([Research and Markets, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r46)). Many e-commerce companies are concerned that the GDPR may apply to blockchains that contain personal information.

Influence on Customer Knowledge Requirements

E-commerce companies are acquainted with disclosure regulations. As e-commerce companies may use virtual asset payments for transactional purposes, implementing KYC & AML might become a crucial part of the process for many organisations for the first time in several countries. Because of their dependence on third-party settlement services, e-commerce businesses who have not previously remained subordinate to any specific KYC or AML necessities may eventually find themselves subject to a laborious and costly regulatory scheme if transfers are to be performed via completely anonymous blockchain systems. Furthermore, since users may construct new identities on request, categorization and account matching for tracking reasons may become more difficult over time. There have been several blockchain-based systems proposed that do the essential KYC for customers just once, regardless of how many institutions they have business contacts with ([Salah et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r47)).

Implications for Organizational Structures and Procedures

The phrase “smart contract” describes a computer programme that is algorithmic, self-executing, and self-enforcing and can be utilized to handle numerous different kinds of transactions. In comparison to traditional e-commerce agreements, which are created by digitally communicated phrases or actions, individuals have the last say. There is no question that impediments remain, despite the fact that it uncertain how successfully ordinary contract law rules may be adapted to the novel technical framework of smart contracts and in what conditions smart contracts can give birth to legally enforceable rights and duties for their parties. E-commerce agreements were previously not viewed as a technical border asset that platform communities may leverage to extend network effects by providing their technological contractual interfaces available to other parties. DAOs in particular and smart contracts in general provide up new possibilities for e-commerce. Smart contract-based virtual DAOs, like those created on the Ethereum blockchain, can be used to conduct transactions with other parties on their own ([Baker, 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r3)).

Access to Financing Markets

Blockchain also creates previously unimaginable chances to gain entry to the capital markets. In a “Initial Coin Offering” (ICO), the supplier sells tokens to customers in exchange for cash. Due to increased legal security, security token offerings (STOs) and initial exchange offerings (IEOs) are becoming more popular. Such occasions are frequently announced online via forums and websites dedicated to virtual assets ([Chamola et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r9)). The majority of issuers will offer online access to a white paper that explains the project and important offering parameters (such as financial terms, subscription information, and schedule) and provides information on the project's current status and key team members. The subscription procedure generally involves the user transferring digital assets to the provider, usually to one or more predefined locations. Unsurprisingly, con artists and gamblers have been drawn to this new fundraising method. The noted distinctiveness of such types of financing is that the property is typically sold with the certainty that the offer or would receive it, which is unusual in a building e-commerce environment in which the digital asset is the sole valid form of payment ([Chamola et al., 2020](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r9); [Chanson et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r10)).

SOLUTION FOR MAINTAINING DATA INTEGRITY IN A BLOCKCHAIN ENVIRONMENT PREVENTING HARMLESS FLAWS

Issues with data integrity, especially innocent ones, are not new to the supply-chain sector. As a result, the tactics utilised to resolve data-integrity problems in a more traditional supply-chain environment and those relevant to preventing benign data-integrity failures in a blockchain context are quite similar.

| Figure 4. Solution for maintaining data integrity in a blockchain |
| --- |
| ![Figure978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.f04](i/ch013.f04.png) |
| --- |

It is quite easy to do so since doing so doesn't call for anticipating the potential conduct of cunning and smart adversaries. The great majority of benign issues can be avoided by using correct system design, maintenance, administration, and business practises; this follows the same conventional concepts and procedures. The strategies and solutions pertinent for preventing malicious flaws, an area where blockchain technology excels, will receive further attention in this lesson ([Ballestar et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r4)).

Safeguarding Against Harmful Flaws

Whether they be users, companies, oracles, or data suppliers, all malevolent flaws in the blockchain are caused by the privileges and powers granted to participants. While these rights are advantageous and supportive of essential blockchain deployment features under normal conditions, they may also be abused when things go wrong. Thus, preventing malicious errors requires restricting rights to only those that are required, reducing the potential harm caused by privilege misuse, spotting malicious behaviour, and holding accountable fenders when it does. Keeping the system's reliance on trusted actors to a minimum is also crucial. Solutions are designed to retain a high level of confidence in the accuracy of some data while minimizing the reliance on the parties providing it in the specific context of ensuring data integrity. This typically takes the shape of protocols and other procedures that aid in the preliminary validation of data inputs. The idea of trust minimization, as the name implies, attempts to minimize the trust and dependence placed on any parties participating in transactions, and it serves as the foundation for all of these strategies ([Catalini & Gans, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r8)). By using this idea, a system can withstand malevolent behavior as well as other sorts of benign defects and still be robust, resilient, and functioning.

Even when the blockchain system must rely on some parties, trust minimization is still important. For instance, even if an oracle is the sole entity with access to All of their data inputs should be clearly logged someplace so that any suspected misconduct may be examined later on, if necessary. Certain data are required by the blockchain. Due to the added complexity and costs, methods for preventing benign faults are less expensive than those for preventing malevolent faults. Naturally, it is up to the person responsible for designing the blockchain deployment to decide how important these protections are and how to weigh their necessity against other factors like cost, technical difficulty, and integration difficulties. Generally speaking, it is best to use as many of the ensuing methods as you can within practical bounds ([De Filippi & Wright, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r12)).

Conventional Methods for Ensuring Data Integrity

These very straightforward methods for ensuring data integrity are well-known, successful, and frequently used throughout the supply-chain sector in general, outside of blockchain installations.

Strictly vet any people or organizations that need to be trusted to carry out specific tasks. The same screening and qualification processes that are used to choose a new hire or contracting firm are typically used to select which people and organizations are given access to privileged roles in blockchain networks. For instance, people can be required to go through a certification procedure or supply “know your customer” (KYC) information as appropriate. One option is to choose people who are required by law to follow particular guidelines, such as converting public notaries to perform blockchain-based services. One could assess an organization's skills overall, performance history, company values, and management style ([Gao et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r13)).

The implementation of punitive measures through customary legal agreements, such as a fine stipulated for particular unlawful network conduct, is another strategy. Such a solution would inherit the well-known benefits and drawbacks of formal contracts, presenting issues like whether the contract is actually enforceable, if the amount at stake is significant enough to justify going to court, and so forth. Legal contracts could be a useful tool to create a strong commitment to protect data integrity if arbitration is sufficiently efficient and affordable ([Hawlitschek et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r19)).

Modern Methods for Data Integrity

Although they are more recent and challenging to implement, these somewhat sophisticated solutions are particularly relevant to and consistent with a blockchain deployment's data integrity requirements.

Reputation System: Over time, the outputs and actions created by privileged actors provide data that can be used to estimate their projected future dependability and trustworthiness. One can say that an oracle has a strong track record of successful performance, for instance, if it has never provided a value that significantly differs from those provided by other oracles (for the same request). This could be used to justify giving future inputs from that oracle a little bit more weight or paying them more for their services. If any wrongdoing is suspected, the track records that serve as the foundation of the reputation system can be utilized to audit earlier behavior. A reputation system that forms the foundation for future privilege and reward increases can be built using performance histories and manual ratings of dependable performers.

Nonetheless, it is advisable to proceed with great caution when introducing monetary rewards or better privileges granted to renowned performers since they increase the incentive for potential competitors to attempt to unjustly abuse the system. This can be accomplished, for example, through reputation farming, collusion, damaging rivals' reputations, misusing one's position of authority to obtain unfair financial advantage, and exit scamming. Because incentives are historically difficult to align correctly, Building reliable reputation systems is notoriously challenging ([Kethineni et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r27)).

Automation is another strategy that aims to reduce the reliance on possibly dangerous human agents. In order to prevent drivers from having any opportunity to fabricate delivery times for parcels, delivery trucks could automatically disclose their locations at all times. Instead of waiting on people and bureaucratic procedures that can purposefully or unintentionally delay payments, smart contracts could be used to make payments for goods and shipments when pre-specified conditions are met. In order to avoid those who profit from the inefficiencies and opacity of the current procedure With a blockchain, the legal documentation required for international exports might be digitalized ([Khan & Salah, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r28)). The automatic process's ability to generate objective findings even in the face of plausible attempts to game the system is a key factor in determining if automation is beneficial to data integrity. Increased levels of automation typically have the extra benefit of making processes more efficient and lowering costs, as well as making it easier to detect and repair human input errors, which helps to prevent more benign issues. This method is constrained, though, in that many of jobs still need to be done by people ([Kiktenko et al., 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r29)).

Accountability and fraud detection: In an ideal world, it would be possible to identify any erroneous or fraudulent data inputs. This is beneficial even if data integrity issues are first discovered after the data has already been utilized because the offenders can still be held liable. Offending parties may receive the appropriate punishment, such as having their reputation damaged, losing all or part of the financial collateral they provided as assurance of their present and future good behaviour, having their capacity to submit data inputs suspended or revoked, or even having their ability to submit data inputs suspended or denied. This will depend on the seriousness of the errors, whether they were unintentional or maliciously motivated, and other elements. Other options include incorporating machine learning models to automatically reject or flag submissions that contain unusual data. Although there are other versions that may be used, the fundamental goal remains the same: to identify inaccurate data inputs and hold dependable parties responsible for them ([Kim & Laskowski, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r30); [Lo et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r34)).

The main disadvantage of this approach is the inability to report redundantly because a sizable piece of Only one component in a supply chain has access to relevant data. Only the entity that sent the box can offer an accurate list of its contents, and only the entity now in control of the package is aware of its current location. However, this method is effective in enhancing the correctness, robustness, and reliability of any publicly available data or data that may be made available to many parties ([Markham, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r35)).

Examples of data that is suited for redundancy and aggregation include local circumstances outside, costs of commodities, and exchange rates, information contained in SEC filings, and pretty much anything that can be acquired from the internet. Numerous oracles may be able to interface with the API of an organization if the data may be retrieved through its API ([Lo et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r34); [Markham, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r35)).

Another illustration would be the Global Positioning System (GPS) positions of moving trucks owned by a significant shipping corporation. In addition to reporting its own location, a car would also reveal the whereabouts of neighboring corporate vehicles, making it more challenging to secretly modify one vehicle's GPS system. In order to successfully fake a location, more than just one company vehicle's GPS system would need to be compromised ([Neroda, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r36)).

The employment of oracles results in significant data-integrity enhancements through input aggregation and cross-validation. Because each additional sensor or oracle used comes with fixed and continuing expenses, the crucial question is whether the level of security achievable with the quantity and quality of these components chosen is appropriate for the requirements of one's use case ([Norvill et al., 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r37)).

Greater confidence in the occurrences and conditions suggested by the data is often made possible by additional data. For instance, the straightforward rule that a courier must take a quick video as they place a pricey box in a container designated for deposits only provides a far better certitude that the product was indeed delivered. While the possibility of the courier filming remains the delivery and then retrieve the box later, it gets more challenging to get away with it as more data is needed because The data must be comparable to data produced by other occurrences in addition to being self-consistent. Although it's probable that only a hash of the data would be kept on-chain, storing this data in an immutable tamper-evident data repository like a blockchain allows events to be verified in the future in case a disagreement arises ([O’Brien, 2019](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r38)).

Each use case will have unique requirements for to maintain data integrity, what more information and proof would be helpful; consequently, blockchain designers and supply-chain decision-makers must exercise ingenuity in this area. The main concept, however, remains constant: while it is challenging to completely prevent fraud and evidence can be faked, gathering When combined with other data-integrity solutions, additional data and proof make it more expensive and time-consuming for an attacker to submit fake data ([Onder & Treiblmaier, 2018](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r39)).

Oracles that provide data inputs can include a matching “proof” that the data they are giving is precisely the same as the data they received from the data source thanks to “provably honest” protocols. This is an alternative to using cryptographic protocols and specialized hardware. Data is deleted if the proof is determined to be untrue once the data and evidence have been published to the blockchain and cross-checked. Since the protocols are put up in this way, Data that has been changed after being collected from the data source is difficult to prove. As a result, oracles that have delivered data in this manner are “provably honest” and do not require faith other than for the fact that they will keep on offering oracle services. However, even if an oracle loses its usefulness or stops providing its services, a replacement can be found immediately ([Pesch & Sillaber, 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r41)). Which oracle presents the data is irrelevant from the viewpoint of the blockchain as long as the corresponding evidence is reliable.

Hypertext Transfer Protocol Secure (HTTPS): TLSNotary, a modification to the HTTPS protocol that enables any machine to demonstrate that a certain web page really loaded in its browser, is one of the better solutions for requests sent over the internet. For instance, a smart contract on the blockchain may prove that the data originated from UPS by utilising TLSNotary to show that the tracking data and timestamps it acquired from UPS were not changed before being uploaded to the blockchain ([Porru et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r43)).

One of the top technologies that can produce a proof of correctness similar to this for requests that primarily demand for some processing to be performed off-chain is TEEs, such as Intel Software Guard Extensions. The Trusted Execution Environment is essentially a separate component totally segregated from other computer components that are included in Intel CPUs that support this protocol ([Ramesh et al., 2017](#978-1-6684-7808-0.ch013.xhtml#r44)). TEEs are exceptional at offering reliable data integrity guarantees in hostile settings.

CONCLUSION

On the blockchain, storage space is entirely decentralized, which means that all nodes work together to maintain data, while many other nodes just keep data backups. On the other hand, conventional data storage simply keep data at a single server node. The information recorded in the blockchain will not be impacted by the altered or lost information of a single node. To ensure safe data storage, the content on the blockchain may indeed be regarded as being permanent. The blockchain system lessens the likelihood that data will be intercepted or corrupted, stops possible fraud, and shields data from intruders.

Finally, the adoption of blockchain as a framework for third-party authentication can guarantee data accessibility, safety, effectiveness, and privacy protection. Blockchain makes it simple to safely exchange, examine, and save digital data. Additionally, it encrypts each exchange using cryptography. Businesses may achieve greater heights in terms of safety and accessibility by doing this.
